# Clinic Hub security review

Owned by the `security-reviewer` subagent. Clinic Hub holds special-category health data, so every gap matters. A finding stays OPEN until the code changes or a human accepts the risk.

## Changelog

- [2026-11] Twenty-first review: what was added on `signaturerx` after the twentieth run, uncommitted: `20261118120000_signaturerx_resolve_send.sql` (close or confirm a stuck send), `resolveSignatureRxSend` in `lib/signaturerx/actions.ts`, `ResolveSend` in `components/prescriptions/signaturerx-panel.tsx`, `supabase/tests/19_signaturerx.test.sql`, and the PA contact form now also editing date of birth, sex at birth and contact email (`updatePatientContact`, `components/patients/contact-form.tsx`, `lib/data/patient-contact.ts`). **`pnpm db:lint` run by me: PASS** ('No schema errors found'). **`pnpm db:test` run by me: PASS, 20 files, 761 tests.** `db:advisors` clean per the owner (not re-run by me). The owner reports `20261117120000` and `20261117120100` applied on the hosted project without errors; `20261118120000` is not yet applied. No remote project, no SignatureRx call, no secret read. **Decisions:** S-162 RESOLVED (new migration; it fails safe); S-163 narrowed (sex at birth is now a field, validated as F, M or blank; phone and postcode format, warning before clearing, column comment remain). **New:** S-165 (LOW, settling a send: the in-flight close and the dialog wording were fixed in a follow-up; still open: a close can duplicate by a person's error, admin may settle; a recorded id is not verified; reason text stored and shown), S-166 (LOW, a PA can change the date of birth that unlocks an invitation, with no warning and no before value in the audit), S-167 (INFO, `contact_email` is not the sign-in email and is used first for SignatureRx). No CRITICAL or HIGH. Counts: 167 findings, 116 open, 51 resolved.

- [2026-11] Thirteenth review: staff Google Calendar (uncommitted on `dev`, for `feat/staff-google-calendar`: `20261111120000_google_calendar_connections.sql`, `07_google_calendar.test.sql`, `lib/calendar/actions.ts`, `google.ts`, `crypto.ts`, `time.ts`, `constants.ts`, `app/api/google/connect/route.ts`, `app/api/google/callback/route.ts`, `app/clinic/calendar/page.tsx`, `components/calendar/event-forms.tsx`, `getGoogleEnv` and `isGoogleConfigured` in `lib/env.ts`, `components/shell/nav.tsx`, and the hand-edited `lib/database.types.ts`). **Read from the code only.** There is no Docker on this machine, so `pnpm db:lint` and `pnpm db:test` were **not run**, the migration has never been applied to any database, and nothing was attacked; no remote project was contacted and no Google request was made. Held, from the code: RLS enabled and forced; one `SELECT` policy (own row, active staff), no `FOR ALL`; `anon` and `PUBLIC` get nothing; `authenticated` reads four columns only, never `refresh_token_encrypted` or `scope`; no insert, update or delete grant for anyone; both `SECURITY DEFINER` functions are in `private`, set `search_path = ''` and are `EXECUTE` for `service_role` only, with `SECURITY INVOKER` wrappers; `on delete restrict`, no cascade; the audit trigger stores column names only, so the token never reaches `audit_log`; the OAuth state is a 32-byte nonce in an `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax` cookie bound to the user id, compared in constant time, cleared after use; the callback address comes from `SITE_URL`, not the request; AES-256-GCM with a fresh 12-byte IV and the user id as AAD; `server-only` on `google.ts` and `crypto.ts`, and no Client Component imports them or `lib/supabase/admin`; CSP, `next.config.ts` and the proxy unchanged. The access rules hold: the seven new functions are `EXECUTE` for `authenticated` and `service_role` as listed in the new section below, `anon` has none; a patient was refused `enter_report` and `release_report`, a PA was refused `release_report`; only a doctor released; a doctor who is also a patient read only released results through the app query. The ones to decide first are S-88 (an import can be confirmed against the wrong patient, reproduced), S-89 (a doctor's comment can be rewritten after release, with no record of the old text) and S-92 (a doctor can enter and release their own results). S-73 gains the 11 MB limit. Also corrected the severity table, which had not been updated since the invitation work (it said 85 findings, 67 open, 18 resolved; the changelog above already said 87, 68, 19). `pnpm db:lint` clean; `pnpm db:advisors` no issues; `pnpm db:test` PASS, 7 files, 340 tests (06_results.test.sql is new). ## Executive summary

No CRITICAL finding, and no HIGH finding is open. A patient or guardian could not reach another patient's data or an unreleased result through PostgREST, RPC, embedded joins or Storage in any attack tried. `anon` reads nothing. Nobody can write their own role.

- **All five HIGH findings are resolved and held under attack:** the lab PDF gate (S-01), forged guardian links (S-02), client-made invitations (S-03), offboarding (S-04) and server updates of `staff_profiles` (S-05). Each fix leaves a smaller follow-on, recorded as its own LOW finding: S-38 and S-42 (S-43 is now resolved, see the next point).
- **The CSP now blocks injected script (S-12, resolved in the working tree).** Against a production build, every route answered with a CSP carrying a fresh nonce and `strict-dynamic`, with no `unsafe-eval`, and every `<script>` tag in the pages tested carried that request's nonce. A client-sent nonce or CSP header changed nothing. `style-src` still allows `'unsafe-inline'`, which cannot stop injected styling (details in S-12). One gap: paths the proxy matcher skips (`/foo.png`, `/favicon.ico` and similar) return the full HTML 404 page with no CSP at all (S-57).
- **Session limits can no longer be edited from the browser (S-31, still OPEN).** `ch_last_seen` and `ch_session_kind` are signed with `SESSION_COOKIE_SECRET`, bound to the cookie name and the account. Unsigned, truncated, malformed, re-valued, other-account and other-cookie values were all refused, and a staff session with a patient kind cookie is refused on `/portal`. The secret is required and the app fails closed without it (500 on every signed-in request). What remains is the copied-refresh-token route, which needs Supabase's Pro-plan limits, and replay of signed values inside their window (also across sessions of one account).
- **The idle timer works end to end (S-48, resolved).** With its 30-minute delay shortened to 3 seconds, an expired staff session was sent to `/login?error=expired` with the cookies cleared and the screen replaced. The real 30-minute wait and a sleeping device were not tested (S-61).
- **S-10 is resolved [2026-11].** The clinic answered on 11 November and `in_scope` is narrowed to the care team on `feat/clinic-decisions-nov` (see the fourteenth-run point below). Earlier: every write policy had been made to ask `private.in_scope()` while the decision was open.
- **Invitations hold against every direct attack tried (tenth run), and S-43 is resolved.** `anon`, a patient, a doctor, a health coach, a PA and an admin were all refused on the five server functions through the Data API (401 or 403), and `get_invitable_patients` answered only for the PA and the admin (empty for the rest). `token_hash` cannot be read, the table cannot be written by any signed-in role, and `private` is not exposed. Forged form fields (`_inviter`, `invited_by`, `role`, `token_hash`), an admin-only kind from a PA, a team role `patient`, a patient the record of whom already has an account, and cross-origin posts all failed. Acceptance is one locked transaction: two simultaneous accepts of one link gave one success and one "already used"; expired, cancelled, used, suspended-invitee, deactivated-inviter, inviter-without-the-role and wrong-address cases were all refused. Links are 43-character random tokens, stored only as a SHA-256, opened by a `GET` that sets a cookie and changes nothing, so a mail scanner cannot use one up (S-36 for this link). **What is still open is who may be invited and what the link proves:** the link is the only check on a patient's or guardian's record (S-74), one PA or admin can bind any record to any address (S-75, S-42), and an acceptance that fails late for another reason could still leave an orphan account (S-77, reduced). **Update [2026-11]:** `20261105130000_invitation_identity.sql` adds the patient's date of birth at acceptance (five wrong dates cancel the link), a confirmation when the address differs from the record, and notices for doctor, admin and can-act guardian invitations. S-74 is reduced (a team link is still the only check), S-75 is eased, and S-86 and S-87 record what the new controls do not cover.
- **S-07 is partly fixed.** A service-role write with no actor or system job is now refused, and this holds for a real PostgREST `service_role` request (403), inside a `SECURITY DEFINER` wrapper, and without touching the owner's SQL or the Auth service. A signed-in user cannot set the actor or the job name through the Data API. "Viewed" is still never audited, the server code that sets the actor does not exist, and a job name is an unchecked label (S-62).
- **The throttle works but does not cover the Auth API.** Direct calls to `/auth/v1/token` with the public key bypass it and rely on Supabase's per-IP limit (S-32, partly fixed). **CAPTCHA (S-32) and MFA for staff (S-11) are deliberately deferred by the owner until the clinic has the accounts.** They stay OPEN.
- **S-13 is resolved on the owner's observation:** the hosted `/auth/v1/settings` reports `disable_signup: true`. This review did not connect to the hosted project.
- **S-09, S-33, S-46 and S-47 stay resolved.** S-16 and S-18 are still only partly fixed.
- **The screens are sound on authorisation.** They read only through the user's session. The only service-role use reachable from sign-in is the throttle call, on the server. `/auth/confirm` cannot redirect off-site, and cross-origin server actions are refused. A doctor who is also a patient sees only released results on `/portal` (one gap for letters: S-41).
- **The knowledge base is admin-only (S-64, INFO).** `/knowledge-base` and `/knowledge-base/test-results` need a signed-in, non-suspended account holding the `admin` role (proxy checks signed in; the page checks `isAdmin`). `isAdmin` reads `user_roles` only and, like `isStaff`, does not check the active staff profile (see S-64 item 7). Both pages call the shared `requireKnowledgeBaseReader`. The first renders `content/knowledge-base.md`: the roles, the access table, session limits, planned integrations and 21 open questions. The sign-in return path uses a fixed allow-list, so there is no open redirect. The second renders `content/test-results-flow.md`, a step-by-step test of the results flow (see S-99). What remains is content drift and housekeeping.
- **The Profile screens hold on authorisation (ninth run).** The actions read the person only from the session: forged `userId`, `email`, `path` and `bucket` fields were ignored, a PA calling `uploadSignature` directly was refused, a signed-out call was redirected to sign-in, and the Storage policies refused another person's folder, a patient's or PA's signature, a doctor's name other than `<id>/signature`, `..` paths, SVG and oversize files. A patient cannot read a doctor's signature through the Data API or a signed URL, and no other role can (a second doctor, a PA, an admin and the coach were tried). Photos of other patients are refused to patients, guardians and the coach.
- **What the app cannot prevent, because Auth is reachable directly (S-37, S-68, S-69).** With any signed-in session and the public key, `PUT /auth/v1/user` changed a password with no current password (even with `secure_password_change` on locally), started an email change with no password, and told the caller whether any address holds an account (422 `email_exists`, with no limit). The profile forms ask for the current password, but only the app asks. One confirmation link was enough to complete an email change although `double_confirm_changes` is true, and, until the notice was added, no mail went to the old address (it now does: S-68). These are the main things to decide before real patient data.
- **S-23 is resolved:** the trigger on `auth.users` keeps `profiles.email` in step (verified live, and a client still cannot change that column).
- **Results entry and release hold on authorisation (eleventh run).** Entry is for a PA or doctor in scope, release for a doctor in scope only; each check sits inside the `SECURITY DEFINER` function (`has_role`, `in_scope`, `my_staff_id`), the public wrappers are `SECURITY INVOKER` and only pass the call on, and the actor in the audit log is the signed-in person (verified for a doctor's release and comment edits). Patients, PAs and `anon` cannot release; a released report cannot be released twice (row lock). Values, units, flags and ranges cannot be edited by anyone after entry (column triggers and no grants), so a wrong value has no correction path either (S-95). What is open is the edges: a comment can be rewritten after release (S-89), orphan files and duplicate uploads (S-90), the 10 MB upload and the 11 MB body for every action (S-91, S-73), no second person for a doctor's own results (S-92), and the conversion factors and single reference ranges that decide the flags (S-93).
- **Nurse, record-view logging and the Activity screen (twelfth run, uncommitted).** A nurse is a team role with no patient access (not in `is_clinical_staff`; verified: zero rows from `patient_profiles`, `lab_reports`, `results`; refused on both new functions). `log_record_view` and `activity_log` check the caller inside, set `search_path = ''` and have `SECURITY INVOKER` wrappers; `anon` is refused. Three gaps found in this run were fixed and re-read: the Activity screen no longer shows safeguarding, diagnosis or medication events (S-100), `log_record_view` refuses invented or mismatched record ids (S-101) and the report page fails closed when it cannot log (S-102). Still open: nothing but the report page logs (S-103), and what admins may see (S-104).
- **Staff Google Calendar (thirteenth run, uncommitted, read from the code only).** The database design holds on paper: forced RLS, own-row reads of four columns, no direct writes, service-role-only definer functions that set the actor, and a token that is encrypted before it is stored and never reaches the browser, the audit log or a Client Component. The OAuth state handling is sound. Nothing was run: the migration, the pgTAP file, the column grants under PostgREST and the audit actor are unverified until `pnpm db:test` runs on a local stack. What is open: any Google account can be connected, including a personal Gmail account, so clinic event titles can leave the clinic's control (S-106, MEDIUM, a decision for the owner); a leaver keeps a working connection (S-107); `/api/google/*` does not force staff session limits (S-108); a reconnect leaves the old token live at Google (S-109); and `00_schema_checks` still expects 33 tables (S-110).
- **Care-team access, doctor invitations, clinical notes, education and wearables (fourteenth run, `feat/clinic-decisions-nov`).** `private.in_scope` now gives PAs and admins every patient and doctors, nurses and health coaches only their current care-team patients, and every patient policy goes through it, so the narrowing is in one place (S-10 resolved). Under attack on the local stand-in it held: a doctor could not add herself to another doctor's patient, could not move a care-team row to another patient or person (no column grant), and a coach or nurse saw only their own patients' care team. Signed notes are locked for every role including the database owner; a guardian without `can_act` cannot enter wearable readings; course content reaches a patient only when published and assigned to them. **One MEDIUM gap, to fix before merge:** `in_scope` does not exclude the staff member's own patient record, so a doctor whom a colleague or PA puts on her own care team reads her own unreleased results and safeguarding alerts, writes notes about herself and releases her own results; any PA or admin who is also a patient does so with no care-team row at all (S-112, verified). That contradicted the clinic's answer to question 4; **resolved [2026-11] in `a55db7b`**, read from the code in the fifteenth run. The rest is LOW: doctors still read every patient's care-team rows (S-58), unmatched files and imports are open to every doctor and nurse (S-117), a note can point at another patient's appointment and claim to be a recording (S-114), anyone in scope can correct another author's signed note and block the author's own correction (S-115), and wearable dates and ranges are checked only by the app (S-116). The nurse upload gap (S-113) is fixed in the uncommitted working tree.
- **Booking, call recording, GoHighLevel, photos, guardian removal and final notes (fifteenth run, `feat/clinic-build-2`, read from the code only).** No HIGH. Held on reading: RLS enabled and forced on `appointment_types`, `clinician_availability` and `call_recordings`, no `FOR ALL`, no delete grant, `anon` nothing, every new definer function sets `search_path = ''`; `book_appointment` lets a patient or guardian book only for a record they can act for, only with a current care-team clinician and only a type open to patients; the overlap check runs after a row lock on the clinician; patients cannot cancel; the transcription webhook needs a constant-time secret, then fetches the transcript itself, and a second delivery writes nothing; the upload path is built on the server from a validated patient id and a random UUID, and both the row and Storage policies require the patient to be in the recorder's scope; the GoHighLevel token stays on the server and its errors are not echoed; the Google event carries the type and clinic reference, never a name. Signed notes are now final (S-115 resolved) and unmatched files are for PAs and admins (S-117 resolved). **Fix before merge (MEDIUM):** S-119 (`clinician_busy` gives any signed-in user the diary of any clinician, past and future), S-120 (a patient can book without limit and cannot cancel), S-121 (GoHighLevel can send a patient's messages to an old or someone else's address) and S-122 (transcripts and audio are never deleted at AssemblyAI). The Permissions-Policy change is LOW (S-123).
- **Ask the clinic, letters and documents, privacy and consent, the fuller record, and the finalise hardening (sixteenth run, `feat/finalise-points`, read from the code only) [2026-11].** No CRITICAL, HIGH or MEDIUM finding is new, and **nothing blocks the merge**. Held on reading: `question_replies` has RLS enabled and forced, no insert, update or delete grant for `authenticated`, no delete or truncate for `service_role`, an audit trigger and `ON DELETE RESTRICT` keys; `private.reply_to_question` and `private.close_question` are `SECURITY DEFINER` with `search_path = ''` and `SECURITY INVOKER` public wrappers, revoked from `anon`. A reply is written as staff (`from_clinic`) only when `private.in_scope` is true, which already excludes the caller's own record and wards; otherwise `private.can_act_for` is required, so **a patient cannot reply as staff and a view-only guardian cannot reply at all** (42501). The author is `auth.uid()`, never a parameter. Closing needs `in_scope`. A patient opens a document only through her own session: the row policy needs `shared_with_patient`, not archived, the patient in `my_patient_ids()` and `document_gate_open`, and Storage serves the object only when that row is readable (`storage_path` is unique), so **an unshared document or an unreleased lab report cannot be opened through `/portal/documents/[id]`**. Every new email, WhatsApp text and bell notice is content-free: no question subject or words, no document title or kind, no appointment type. The record actions check the role on the server and RLS checks it again; safeguarding stays doctor-only on the page, the action and the policy. **New service-role use:** `tellPatient` in `lib/documents/actions.ts` reads one `first_name`, and `notifyPatient` now inserts the bell notice; both run only after a write through the user's own session has passed RLS for that patient, except that the patient-side reply can trigger the clinic's "replied" message (S-130). Small gaps: S-130 to S-135. The finalise migration resolves S-107, S-116, S-125 and S-126 and most of S-127; `2847f26` resolves S-124; S-119 to S-123 were already fixed in `29f602f` on `dev`.
- **Allergy removal, the portal patient switcher and the leaver revocation job (seventeenth run, `feat/small-items`, read from the code only) [2026-11].** No CRITICAL, HIGH or MEDIUM finding is new, and **nothing blocks the merge**. The `portal_patient` cookie cannot widen access: it is only ever matched against the rows `get_my_patient_record` returns for the session, both when it is set (`choosePortalPatient`, which also checks the value is a UUID) and when it is read (`getPortalPatient`), and every read behind it still goes through RLS; it is `httpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax`, `Secure` in production, and the action only redirects to the fixed `/portal`. The cron route fails closed: with no `CRON_SECRET`, or one shorter than 32 characters, it answers 404; otherwise the whole `Authorization` header is compared with `Bearer <secret>` by `timingSafeEqual` after an equal-length check (which reveals only the length, already public in `.env.example`); it returns counts only and a fixed message on error. The queued token is never exposed: `google_revocations` has RLS forced, nothing for `anon` or `authenticated` (test 16 checks a patient is refused), no insert or delete for `service_role`, and it reaches only `lib/calendar/leavers.ts` (`server-only`), which never logs it and clears it once Google confirms. `d777b66` hides archived allergies from patients and guardians in the read policy and adds `archived_at is null` to the update policy's `using`, so an archived row can no longer be edited or restored; it also queues and deletes the Google connection when an account is suspended. Follow-ons: the archive time is still chosen by the caller, not the database (S-137, partly fixed); the queue's updates are not audited (S-138); a token that fails ten times, or every token while `CRON_SECRET` is unset, waits silently (S-139). S-134 is resolved.
- **Repeat prescriptions and Stripe (eighteenth run, `feat/repeat-prescriptions`, read from the code only) [2026-11].** No CRITICAL, HIGH or MEDIUM finding is new, and **nothing blocks the merge**. Held on reading: `prescribable_medicines` and `repeat_request_items` have RLS enabled and forced, one policy per command, nothing for `anon`, no delete for anyone, audit triggers and `ON DELETE RESTRICT` keys; `repeat_request_items` is read-only to signed-in people and its composite keys tie each line to the request's patient and a current medicine to that same patient. `repeat_requests` lost its insert and update policies and grants, so `paid_at`, `status`, `decided_by` and `cancelled_at` change only through the definer functions, which set `search_path = ''` and check the caller inside: `request_repeat` needs `can_act_for` (patient, or verified can-act guardian, not archived, not suspended) or a PA in scope; `cancel_repeat_request` the same or staff in scope; `decide_repeat_request` a doctor or PA in scope (admins removed in `c94f9bb`, since they write no clinical data; so not their own record, S-112); `mark_repeat_request_paid` and `mark_run_out_reminded` are `EXECUTE` for `service_role` only and name a system actor. **A patient or guardian cannot create, read or withdraw another patient's request**, and the portal detail page shows a request only when it belongs to the patient chosen in the portal. **No open redirect:** the success and cancel addresses are built from `SITE_URL` and a validated UUID, and the action redirects only to a URL Stripe returned that starts with `https://checkout.stripe.com/`. **The webhook** checks Stripe's HMAC in constant time within 300 seconds, stores each event once by its id, re-checks the amount against `fee_pence` under a row lock, and records a payment once. **No medicine name or clinical answer reaches Stripe, email, WhatsApp, bell notices, `webhook_events` or the audit log.** No service-role client reaches a Client Component; the CSP is unchanged (no Stripe script; the browser is sent to Stripe's hosted page). **Re-read against the fix commit `c94f9bb` [2026-11]:** S-141 (pay action checks the portal patient, `can_act` and an open unpaid request), S-142 (a second payment or one after a decline is refused and stored as a failed event; pages last 31 minutes and charge the row's fee), S-144 (only sessions whose `metadata.repeat_request_id` is a UUID are handled; the rest get 200) and S-145 (archived patients skipped, pages of 1,000) are RESOLVED, and S-143's retry half is fixed (any error other than 22023 answers 500). **What is open: S-143, failed Stripe payments are stored in `webhook_events` but no screen, alert or email shows them to anyone, so a second payment is visible only in the Stripe dashboard or the host log. Close it (an admin list of failed events, or a content-free email to the clinic inbox) before live keys.** S-146 stays open for its minor points; its CSP `form-action` point is unchanged and still untested in a browser. Not covered by any test: the webhook route, `payRepeat` and the reminder job (the database side, including the declined-request refusal, is tested).
- **Results sign-off and treatment options in notes (CQC run, `feat/cqc-audit`, read from the code only) [2026-11].** No CRITICAL, HIGH or MEDIUM finding is new, and **nothing blocks the merge**. Held on reading: a patient or guardian cannot read a sign-off or its action note, directly or embedded under `lab_reports`, because the only policy on `lab_report_signoffs` is `private.in_scope(patient_id)`, which is false for patients, guardians and anyone on their own record, and PostgREST applies that policy to embedded rows. `authenticated` has `select` only; the sole writer is the `SECURITY DEFINER` `private.release_report`, which takes `signed_by` from `my_staff_id()`, the patient from the report row and `ordered_by` from the order, and refuses anyone who is not a doctor in scope, so nurses, health coaches, PAs and admins cannot sign off. A trigger refuses every update and delete for every role, and `service_role` has no update, delete or truncate. No caller uses the dropped two-argument signature, and a two-argument call now fails with 22023. A signed note's option columns are covered by the existing lock trigger, which refuses any update of a signed row. Two gaps were found and are now mostly closed by `d309621`: `service_role` could insert a sign-off (S-147; `insert` now revoked, the tie to a release still missing) and the audit entry carried no record id (S-148; now the report's id, but the Activity screen still resolves no patient for it).
- **SignatureRx and the prescriber's signing PIN (nineteenth run, `signaturerx`, uncommitted, read from the code; `pnpm db:test` run by me, `pnpm db:lint` fails for another reason) [2026-11].** No CRITICAL or HIGH. **The PIN holds on reading:** it is a form field read once in `sendToSignatureRx`, checked as six digits, placed in the JSON body to SignatureRx and dropped; no column exists for it, the audit trigger stores column names only, the only `console.error` lines are fixed strings, the action's result type has no field that could carry it, the field is uncontrolled (not in React state) and the form is remounted after a send. The integration code sits in a table only `service_role` can read (no grant for `authenticated`, forced RLS, audit trigger with column names only), and the browser is told only whether one is saved. The sending switch is off unless `SIGNATURERX_SIGNING_ENABLED` is exactly `true` and the credentials are set; the base URL is an enum of two SignatureRx origins, so no request can be sent elsewhere; the doctor role is checked in the action and again in the database; a nurse, PA or admin cannot send, save a code or record a row (pgTAP). **What is open:** the scope check is "the doctor can read the request", which includes a doctor's own request and one for a patient they are only a guardian of (S-150); nothing stops a second send of the same request, and a failed record leaves no trace in Clinic Hub (S-151); SignatureRx becomes a processor of names, dates of birth, addresses and medicines with no data processing agreement on file (S-152). Smaller: the integration code is not encrypted (S-153), the PIN throttle fails open and shares a bucket (S-154), the outbound calls follow redirects and show SignatureRx's own error text (S-155), `prescriber_ip` trusts the first `x-forwarded-for` entry (S-156), failed sends are not audited and a status check names no person (S-157), the PIN field is a password input that browsers may offer to save (S-158). **Not in this branch:** S-159 (the sign-off section showed "No action needed" on every released report, live on `dev`) and S-160 (`db:lint` fails on `private.request_repeat`).
- **Settling a stuck send and the wider PA contact form (twenty-first run, `signaturerx`, uncommitted, read from the code; `pnpm db:lint` and `pnpm db:test` run by me, both pass) [2026-11].** S-162 is resolved: an admin or a doctor on the patient's care team can close a `sending` row (so the request can be sent again) or record the SignatureRx id they found, each audited and decided by the database, not the action. It cannot record someone else's id (a used id is refused, 23505) and creates, signs and sends nothing. **The remaining risk is a duplicate prescription through a person's error or timing (S-165):** a send under five minutes old is now refused (follow-up fix, so a close cannot race the first call), but a person's mistaken close can still lead to two prescriptions; and an admin can settle a send, which the owner should accept or remove. A PA can now also edit date of birth, sex at birth and contact email. Sex is validated (F, M or blank, S-163). A changed date of birth changes which date unlocks a pending invitation, with no warning and no before value in the audit (S-166); a changed contact email does not unlock anything (an invitation is accepted by the account email) but is used first when sending to SignatureRx (S-167).
- **SignatureRx after the fixes (twentieth run, `signaturerx`, uncommitted, read from the code; `pnpm db:lint` and `pnpm db:test` run by me, both pass) [2026-11].** S-150 and S-151 are resolved in the database, not only in the action: the sender's scope is a definer function that is asked before and repeated inside `start_signaturerx_send`, and a send is opened under a row lock and a partial unique index before anything is created at SignatureRx. The price is S-162: a `sending` row left by a crash or a timeout blocks that request until someone edits the row in SQL. S-160 is resolved (`db:lint` is green again). S-153, S-155 and S-158 are partly fixed (see their rows). A PA can now edit a patient's address, country and phone on the clinic record through their own session: column grants, the existing in-scope update policy and the audit trigger hold, and a refused update is reported (S-163 lists what the form still cannot do). **Remaining before signing is switched on: S-152 (data processing agreement) and a decision on S-162 (now resolved, see S-165), S-153 and S-155.**
- **Fix before building** the release and import server code: S-07 and S-62 (how the actor is set), S-45 and S-53. Invitations are built (S-07 held for them and S-43 is resolved).
- **Fix before commit:** nothing blocks the commit. S-59 (client bundle) and S-57 (matcher) are small and worth doing with it. Set `SESSION_COOKIE_SECRET` in Vercel before the first sign-in (S-60). For invitations, S-78 is resolved and S-77 is reduced (one live patient invitation per record, and a link for an archived or already-registered patient reads as cancelled); S-74 (team half) and S-75 still need a decision before the first real staff invitation and the first real patient are invited; the date-of-birth proof and the notices ease both.

These pass with every OPEN finding below present, so the suite does not cover them. Each MEDIUM fix should come with a pgTAP test. `03_scope_and_audit_actor.test.sql` checks that the listed policies mention `in_scope` and that the actor rule works, but it does not narrow `in_scope` and run the attacks, and it does not exercise a PostgREST request or a `SECURITY DEFINER` wrapper (both done by hand in the fifth run). `isSessionExpired`, the cookie signing and the proxy's session handling have no automated test at all.

### How claims were verified

"Verified" means the attack was run against the running local database with `psql`, in a rolled-back transaction, under `set local role authenticated` with `request.jwt.claims` set to a seed user, or `service_role` for server code. Data API and app checks used read-only HTTP requests with a locally minted token, the app being the dev server on port 3000, not a production build. "From the code" means justified by reading the code, not executed. In the second run `audit_log` stayed at 301 rows, `storage.objects` at 0 and `auth.sessions` at 0.

In the third run, "verified" also means real server action requests to the dev server on `http://localhost:3130`, confirmed by its CSP to be using `http://127.0.0.1:54321`, with seed accounts. Idle cookies were aged by editing them in the request. The total limit was tested by backdating one session's row in the local `auth.mfa_amr_claims` table. For S-47, a temporary second container of the same local Auth image was run with only its email settings changed, against the local database, and then removed. Afterwards `audit_log` was still 301 rows and `storage.objects` 0. The sessions and throttle counters the run created were deleted. Global sign-out tests may also have ended other local sessions of the same seed users, and reset requests left test emails in the local mailbox. No remote project was contacted and `.env.local` was not read.

In the fourth run the dev server on port 3130 was started with the local URL and keys in its environment, as in the `web-local` launch configuration, and curl with a cookie jar sent real server action requests and edited cookies. For S-47 a temporary second container of the same local Auth image (`GOTRUE_RATE_LIMIT_EMAIL_SENT=2`, `GOTRUE_MAILER_AUTOCONFIRM=false`, same database) ran behind a small loopback proxy that sent `/auth/v1/*` to it and everything else to the local stack, and the dev server was restarted against that proxy for the test. Valid recovery links came from the local Auth admin `generate_link` endpoint. Afterwards the container, proxy and dev server were stopped, the throttle counters and the sessions the run created were deleted, and `audit_log` was still 301 rows and `storage.objects` 0. Test emails remain in the local mailbox. Eleanor's password was changed and set back to the seed value. No remote project was contacted and `.env.local` was not read.

In the fifth run, the app was a production build (`next build`, then `next start` on port 3131) made in a copy of the tree under the scratchpad, without any `.env*` file, with the local URL and keys and `SESSION_COOKIE_SECRET=local-development-only-secret-0123456789` in its environment. Sessions were taken straight from the local Auth service, and the session cookie was built by hand. Where a test needed an aged `ch_last_seen`, it was signed with that local secret, which is public in `.claude/launch.json`: this tests what the proxy does with a validly signed value, and does not forge anything against a real secret. Real server-issued cookies came from the sign-in server action and from a valid recovery link (made with the local Auth admin `generate_link`). The idle timer was tested in the browser pane: cookies were written from the page as non-HttpOnly cookies, and the page's `setTimeout` was patched to shorten the 30-minute delay to 3 seconds. Narrowed scope was tested by redefining `private.in_scope` inside rolled-back transactions. Side effects: `audit_log` went from 301 to 303 rows (two Data API updates of one seed patient's `country`, put back to its original value; the log is append-only); the sessions and throttle counters the run created were deleted, which also ended other local sessions of seed users; the temporary servers were stopped. One mistake to record: a first `pnpm build` in the repository itself, before the copy was made, loaded `.env.local` automatically (Next does), with the local values exported over the three Supabase variables. No remote project was contacted by anything this run started on purpose, the build reads no data (every route is dynamic), and `.env.local` was never opened. That build did overwrite the repository's `.next` output (see S-56).

In the tenth run (invitations), the app was a production build of a copy of the tree under the scratchpad on port 3141, with the local URL, the local keys and `SESSION_COOKIE_SECRET` only in its environment, and no `SITE_URL` or Resend key, so no email could be sent and the screen said so. Server actions were called with real multipart requests (the `Next-Action` id from the build's server reference manifest, fields as `_1_<name>` and the state argument last, the format the browser sends), with seed sessions taken from the local Auth service. The Data API was called with `curl` as `anon`, Thomas (patient), Dr Schoeman (doctor), Cécile (health coach), Priya (PA), the admin and the service role. Test links were made with `public.create_invitation` as the service role and 43-character tokens of my own, hashed with SHA-256. Races used two `psql` sessions at once. Anything that the main session had already seen in the browser (the screens) was not repeated.

## Findings

### HIGH

#### S-01 A PA or doctor can open the release gate by changing `documents.kind`

- **Status:** RESOLVED in `2128195` (`20261102111720_rls_policies.sql`, `20261102111718_helper_functions.sql`, `20261102111731_grants_hardening.sql`, `20261102111707_care_and_requests.sql`)
- **Files:** `20261102111720_rls_policies.sql` (documents policies), `20261102111731_grants_hardening.sql` (`grant update (kind, ...)`)
- **Re-test [2026-11]:** `update documents set kind` is refused by grant and trigger. The gate is now `private.document_gate_open`, which depends on the link: with the kind changed to `letter` by the server itself and the report unreleased, Eleanor and the health coach still read nothing, and the same held for the file's `storage.objects` row. Two reports sharing a document stay closed while either is unreleased. Follow-on: S-38.
- **Risk:** The gate on lab PDFs is `kind <> 'lab_report' or private.document_report_released(id)`, and `kind` is client-writable. Verified: as a PA, `update documents set kind = 'letter'` on seed document `...0005`, whose report is unreleased and which has `shared_with_patient = true`. Eleanor then read the row and the file's `storage.objects` row, while `lab_reports` still hid the report. The health coach's gate opened the same way. This breaks "patients see a lab report only after release", and release is a doctor's act done through server code. It can happen by accident, for example when refiling a document.
- **Fix:** Revoke `update (kind)` from `authenticated`, or add `kind` to `documents_reject_client_changes`. Also consider a trigger rejecting `shared_with_patient = true` on any document linked from an unreleased `lab_reports` row, whatever its kind, so that the gate depends on the link and not on a label. A PA uploading a copy as `kind = 'other'` remains possible (verified) and can only be reduced by procedure and the audit log.

#### S-02 A PA can create a verified guardian link for any account, with a forged verifier

- **Status:** RESOLVED in `2128195` (`20261102111720_rls_policies.sql`)
- **Files:** `20261102111720_rls_policies.sql` (`patient_guardians` insert and update policies)
- **Re-test [2026-11]:** refused: a link naming another verifier, a PA linking herself (verified or not), rewriting the verifier, changing `guardian_user_id`, a second PA changing a colleague's link while keeping her name, and a deactivated PA. Follow-on: S-42.
- **Risk:** The check is only `has_role('pa')`, so `verified_by` and `guardian_user_id` are unconstrained. All verified:
  - A PA linked patient Thomas to Eleanor with `verified_by` set to Dr Schoeman. Thomas then read Eleanor's 30 results and 3 medications.
  - A PA linked herself with `can_act`, then inserted a research consent for Eleanor and asked a question as her guardian.
  - A PA rewrote `verified_by` on an existing link to a different staff member.

  One person can disclose a whole record, and the record of who verified it can be false. Once `in_scope` narrows, self-linking also escapes it through `my_patient_ids()`.
- **Guardians themselves cannot self-verify.** Verified: Mark's update of his own `verified_by`, `can_act` and an insert of a new link were all refused or matched no row. An unverified link gives nothing.
- **Fix:** In both policies, add `(verified_by is null or verified_by = (select private.my_staff_id()))` and `guardian_user_id <> (select auth.uid())`. Consider a second approver for `can_act`.

#### S-03 Invitation rows take a client-chosen token hash and expiry

- **Status:** RESOLVED in `2128195` (`20261102111720_rls_policies.sql`, `20261102111731_grants_hardening.sql`, `20261102111657_identity_and_accounts.sql`)
- **Files:** `20261102111657_identity_and_accounts.sql`, `20261102111720_rls_policies.sql`, `20261102111731_grants_hardening.sql` (`grant insert (... token_hash, expires_at)`)
- **Re-test [2026-11]:** PA and admin insert and update are refused by grant. `token_hash` cannot be read. A short hash and a long expiry are refused by check constraints. Follow-on: S-43, which holds what the first run listed for acceptance.
- **Re-test, tenth run [2026-11]:** with `20261105120000_invitations.sql` the acceptance this finding asked for is built and held under attack: expiry, inviter still active and still allowed to send that kind, patient has no account, signed-in email equals the invited address, account not suspended (see S-43). Direct `POST`, `PATCH` and `DELETE` on `invitations` as an admin and as a PA still gave 403; `token_hash` still reads as permission denied; a duplicate `token_hash`, a 30-day expiry, a short hash, an accepted-and-cancelled row and every wrong mix of kind and fields (`invitations_kind_fields`) were refused. The decision on a second approver for team-role invitations is not made (PRD, open questions).
- **Risk:** Verified: a PA inserted an invitation with `role = 'patient'`, `patient_id` of a patient who already has an account, an address of her choosing, an empty `token_hash` and `expires_at = 9999-01-01`. An admin inserted `role = 'doctor'` and `role = 'admin'` invitations the same way. The inviter therefore knows the token and it need never expire. Clients cannot revoke a row. An expired pending row keeps blocking that address through `invitations_one_pending_per_email` (verified).

  The acceptance server function is not built, so the end of this chain is from the code. Unless acceptance guards against it, a PA could bind an account they control to any patient record, and a suspended admin could later accept a stored invitation for a team role. It also gives an admin a route to clinical access through a second address, against the decision that admin is not clinical staff.
- **Fix:** Remove the client insert grant and create invitations only in server code, which generates the token, hashes it and sets a short expiry. Add `check (expires_at <= created_at + interval '14 days')` and a non-empty hash check. Acceptance must check expiry, that the inviter is still active, that `patient_profiles.user_id` is empty, and that the signed-in email matches. Decide whether team-role invitations need a second approver.

#### S-04 Deactivating a staff member does not remove their access

- **Status:** RESOLVED in `2128195` (`20261102111718_helper_functions.sql`, `private.is_active_staff`)
- **Files:** `20261102111718_helper_functions.sql` (`has_role`, `is_clinical_staff`, `is_team`)
- **Re-test [2026-11]:** with `active = false` or `archived_at` set, a PA, doctor, health coach and admin all read 0 patients, results, documents and invitations, and writes are refused. Nobody can reactivate themselves. A deactivated doctor who is also a patient keeps only her own patient view. A role with no staff row grants nothing, so the code that grants a staff role must also create that row. Left over: the deactivated person still reaches the clinic shell (S-35) and can change their own signature (S-16).
- **Risk:** These helpers read only `user_roles` and `profiles.status`. Verified: with `active = false` and `archived_at` set on the PA's `staff_profiles` row, she still read 11 patient profiles, 65 results and 8 documents, changed a guardian link and changed a document's kind. Only `my_staff_id()` stops, so only writes attributed by staff id fail. An admin who deactivates a leaver reasonably believes access has ended.
- **Fix:** Make the staff roles in `has_role`, `is_clinical_staff` and `is_team` also require an active, unarchived `staff_profiles` row, or define one offboarding server function that suspends the account, removes roles and deactivates the row in one transaction. Test it in `01_role_matrix`.

#### S-05 Server code cannot update `staff_profiles`

- **Status:** RESOLVED in `2128195` (`20261102111720_rls_policies.sql`)
- **Files:** `20261102111720_rls_policies.sql` (`private.protect_staff_columns`)
- **Re-test [2026-11]:** as `service_role`, updates of `job_title`, `active` and `ghl_user_id` succeed.
- **Risk:** Verified: as `service_role`, `update public.staff_profiles set job_title = ...` fails with `permission denied for function has_role`. The trigger's condition is `current_user = 'authenticated' and not private.has_role('admin')`. Postgres checks execute permission when it prepares the expression, before the `and` can short-circuit, and `service_role` has no execute on that function. Server code therefore cannot deactivate staff, or write `ghl_user_id` and `semble_user_id`, which only it may write. Migration comments say server code is not affected. `protect_profile_columns` avoids this by nesting its `if`, and a `service_role` update of `profiles` works (verified).
- **Fix:** Nest the check as in `protect_profile_columns`. Add a test that updates `staff_profiles` as `service_role`.

### MEDIUM

#### S-06 Attribution columns are not bound to the caller

- **Status:** RESOLVED in `2128195` (`20261102111720_rls_policies.sql`, `20261102111701_team_and_sites.sql`)
- **Files:** `20261102111720_rls_policies.sql`, `20261102111731_grants_hardening.sql`
- **Re-test [2026-11]:** another doctor as prescriber is refused on insert, and the column cannot be updated. `handled_by` naming someone else is refused. `recorded_by` is bound too. A signature under another person's id is refused by check constraint. Follow-on: the constraint reads only the first path segment (S-40).
- **Risk:** Verified: a doctor set `medications.prescriber_id` to another doctor on insert and update, and a PA set `result_imports.handled_by` to a doctor. A staff member set their own `signature_path` to a path under another person's id. No signature bucket exists yet, so the effect of that one depends on how letters are built.
- **Held:** `documents.uploaded_by` cannot be spoofed (verified: insert refused by policy, update refused by grant and trigger). Nor can `lab_orders.ordered_by`, `invitations.invited_by`, `safeguarding_alerts.added_by`, `repeat_requests.requested_by` or `questions.asked_by`.
- **Fix:** Bind `handled_by` to `my_staff_id()` in the `with check`. Bind `prescriber_id` the same way, or to a row whose role is doctor if a doctor may record for a colleague. Constrain `signature_path` to start with the user's own id.

#### S-07 Service-role writes reach the audit log with no actor

- **Status:** OPEN, partly fixed. The rule is in `20261102111725_audit_triggers.sql` and `20261102150000_scope_and_audit_actor.sql` (uncommitted), with tests in `03_scope_and_audit_actor.test.sql`.
- **Files:** `20261102150000_scope_and_audit_actor.sql` (`private.write_audit_log`), `lib/supabase/admin.ts`
- **Fixed and verified [2026-11]:**
  - With `set_config('app.actor_id', ...)`, a `service_role` release records the real actor and role. A signed-in user cannot spoof it, because `auth.uid()` wins. Verified again in the fifth run: a PA's Data API update with `app.actor_id` and `app.system_actor` set to other values in the same transaction (through SQL) was still recorded under the PA.
  - **A write by `service_role` with no actor and no job is refused with `42501`.** It was checked, as asked, that this covers the way the real server will write:
    - A PostgREST request with the local secret key (`PATCH /rest/v1/staff_profiles`) returned 403 with the new message, and nothing was written. PostgREST runs the request under `SET LOCAL ROLE service_role`, and the `role` setting that the trigger reads is that role, not `current_user`.
    - The same refusal came from inside a `SECURITY DEFINER` function owned by `postgres` that was called by `service_role`: `current_user` changes there, `role` does not. So a definer wrapper does not escape the rule.
    - With `app.system_actor` set, the same write is allowed, the row records no person, and the metadata carries `system`.
  - **A signed-in user cannot set `app.system_actor` or `app.actor_id` through the Data API.** `POST /rest/v1/rpc/set_config` returned 404 for `authenticated` and for `service_role` (not in an exposed schema), and a request header named `app.system_actor` was ignored. PostgREST sets only its own `request.*` settings. In SQL an authenticated user can set them, but then `auth.uid()` wins and the job name is only recorded when there is no person.
  - **Invitations, tenth run:** every function in `20261105120000_invitations.sql` sets `app.actor_id` to a named person with `set_config(..., true)` inside its own transaction, and the PostgREST call is one transaction. Verified in `audit_log`: a PA's new-patient insert, patient record link, role grant and acceptance are under the PA with role `pa`; an admin's team invitation, role grant and staff profile under the admin; a guardian link under the PA. The account the Auth service creates for a new person has no actor (the owner's-SQL case below). Acceptance is logged under the inviter, not the person who accepted (S-84). Service-role calls with an unknown inviter, a patient, a doctor or a PA asking for a team invitation were refused by the function before anything was written.
  - **The owner's SQL is not affected.** A `postgres` write (dashboard SQL editor, migrations, the seed) went through with no actor. So did a user insert by the Auth service (`supabase_auth_admin`, role setting `none`), which fires the `profiles` trigger.
  - The guard fires on `INSERT`, `UPDATE` and `DELETE` (27 tables have the trigger). `TRUNCATE` is not covered (S-28).
- **Still to do:**
  - **No server function exists yet,** so nothing sets the actor in real use. A `set_config` must share a transaction with the write, so through `supabase-js` it has to live inside one database function. Use `set_config(..., true)`. Verified: with `is_local = false` the setting survived into the next transaction on the same connection, so on a pooled connection one request's job name or actor could attach to the next one's writes.
  - **"Viewed" is still never written.** Reads are not audited at all, although the table comment says "viewed".
  - **Four tables have no audit trigger and so no guard:** `notifications`, `notification_preferences`, `webhook_events`, `integration_sync_log`. They hold no clinical values, but service-role writes there are unattributed.
  - **The job name is an unchecked label** and the actor can be any profile id: S-62, S-28.
  - **The guard is only for the `service_role` role.** Code that connects as `postgres` or another owner role (a direct connection string in a future job) is outside it by design.
- **Original risk:** `write_audit_log` took the actor from `auth.uid()`, which is empty for the service role, so release, role grants and import confirmation wrote rows with an empty actor.
- **Fix:** write the server functions that set the actor, as above. Decide how "viewed" gets written. Add a pgTAP test that calls a `SECURITY DEFINER` wrapper as `service_role`.

#### S-08 Import rows and report links can cross patients

- **Status:** RESOLVED in `2128195` (`20261102111720_rls_policies.sql`, `20261102111711_labs_and_results.sql`, `20261102111718_helper_functions.sql`)
- **Files:** `20261102111711_labs_and_results.sql`, `20261102111720_rls_policies.sql`
- **Re-test [2026-11]:** re-pointing an import away from its document's patient is refused. A confirmed import matches no row. `confirmed` cannot be set from the client. A report cannot link another patient's or an unmatched document, by composite foreign key. A document under a report cannot be moved. Follow-on: S-39.
- **Risk:** All verified:
  - A PA changed `result_imports.patient_id` on an import whose document belongs to a different patient.
  - A PA moved a `confirmed` import back to `matched` and re-pointed it. The `with check` limits the new status but not the old one, so a confirm could be repeated.
  - `lab_reports.document_id` accepts a document of another patient, because there is no composite key, unlike `results`.
  - A released report for patient A pointing at a document of patient B opened the gate on B's unreleased PDF, because `document_report_released` only asks whether any released report links it. Two reports sharing one document behave the same.

  The result can be values filed on the wrong patient, a clinical safety problem as well as a disclosure.
- **Fix:** Add `using (status <> 'confirmed')` to the update policy. Add a composite foreign key from `lab_reports (document_id, patient_id)` to `documents (id, patient_id)`, and make `document_report_released` require that no unreleased report links the document. The confirm function must check the import's patient against the document.

#### S-09 A patient can claim a file that has no `documents` row yet

- **Status:** RESOLVED in `a76efe0` (`20261102111718_helper_functions.sql`, `20261102111720_rls_policies.sql`, `20261102111731_grants_hardening.sql`, `private.storage_object_is_mine`)
- **Files:** `20261102111720_rls_policies.sql` (documents insert), `20261102111728_storage.sql`
- **Re-test [2026-11]:** the patient branch of the insert policy now requires an object in the `documents` bucket at that path whose `owner_id` is the caller. All verified in rolled-back transactions:
  - **Refused:** the row-first attack (no object yet), an orphan with no owner, an object owned by a PA, and an object of the same name in the `avatars` bucket.
  - **Still works:** a patient's own upload, and a guardian's upload for the patient they act for.
  - **Oracle:** a hidden path and a free path now give the same refusal on the row. An object upload to an occupied path still fails differently, on the unique key.
  - **Remaining:** S-45.
- **Risk:** Reading a file requires only a row the caller can read with the same `storage_path`. Verified: with an object at `<patient id>/orphan-staff-upload.pdf` and no row, Eleanor could not see it, then inserted a `patient_upload` row with that path and could. The target is any object in her own folder uploaded before its row exists, or left orphaned by a failed write. Guessing is plausible if paths are predictable: seed path `.../tdl-sch-1000139.pdf` uses the barcode, which patients can read on `lab_orders`. The unique constraint protects a path once it has a row, but that gives a small oracle: a refused insert tells the patient a hidden path exists (verified). Patients cannot write outside their own folder, overwrite, rename or delete (verified).
- **Fix:** In the patient branch of the insert policy, require that the path carries a server-issued random component, or check `storage.objects.owner_id` for any existing object. Server code should create the row before the object, and use random file names.

#### S-10 `in_scope` is not applied everywhere, so narrowing it will leave gaps

- **Status:** RESOLVED [2026-11] in `57514b9` (`20261112120100_access_by_care_team.sql`). The clinic answered on 11 November 2026: PAs and admins see every patient; doctors, nurses and health coaches see only the patients on whose care team they currently are. `in_scope` is narrowed to that, and `09_care_team_access.test.sql` tests it with the narrowed function (29 checks, passing on the local stand-in). Re-attacked in the fourteenth run: a doctor was refused a `care_team` insert for a patient she is not on, and an update of `patient_id` or `staff_id` (no column grant). What it leaves is recorded elsewhere: S-58 (doctors still read every care-team row), S-112 (own record not excluded) and S-117 (unmatched files and imports). Earlier status: OPEN, partly fixed in `20261102150000_scope_and_audit_actor.sql` while the decision was open.
- **Files:** `20261102111718_helper_functions.sql`, `20261102111720_rls_policies.sql`, `20261102150000_scope_and_audit_actor.sql`
- **Re-test [2026-11] (fifth run):** `private.in_scope` was redefined inside a rolled-back transaction to allow one patient only, and the altered policies were attacked as a PA and a doctor. All held:
  - `care_team` insert for an out-of-scope patient was refused; for the in-scope patient it worked. `patient_guardians` insert for an out-of-scope patient was refused. `repeat_requests` insert for an out-of-scope patient was refused. `patient_profiles` update matched 0 rows out of scope and 1 in scope. The doctor read 0 of the 3 `safeguarding_alerts`.
  - `result_imports`: the PA read 2 of 3 (the in-scope one and the one with a null `patient_id`). An update of the out-of-scope import matched 0 rows. Re-pointing an unmatched import to an out-of-scope patient was refused by the `with check`, and to the in-scope patient worked.
  - `import_items` followed their import: 0 items for the out-of-scope import, 3 for the null-patient import, 7 in all. An update of an out-of-scope import's item matched 0 rows.
  - Nothing regressed: `pnpm db:test` passes 188 tests, including the role matrix.
- **What the decision still has to cover:**
  - **Unmatched imports are readable by every PA and doctor, whatever the scope.** `import_items` hold the extracted marker values, name and result of a patient the system does not know yet. That is needed so that anyone can match it, but under a narrowed scope it is a deliberate gap. An in-scope PA can also set an import back to unmatched (verified), which hands it to everyone.
  - **Two reads do not call `in_scope`: S-58** (`care_team` and `invitations`).
  - The wording of the policy for who may change `care_team` for themselves is still open (a PA in scope may add herself, verified).
- **Original risk:** several policies never called `in_scope`, so the comment's claim that narrowing it needs no policy change was wrong. A PA inserted a repeat request for an arbitrary patient and added herself to `care_team` for any patient (both verified then).
- **Fix:** the clinic decides the scope; then narrow `in_scope`, add the attack tests above to pgTAP with the narrowed function, and settle S-58.

#### S-11 MFA for staff cannot be enforced as built

- **Status:** OPEN. **Deliberately deferred by the owner until the clinic has the accounts** [2026-11]: the enrolment screen and the policy change are not worth building before the people who will use them exist.
- **Files:** `supabase/config.toml` (`[auth.mfa.totp]` off), `20261102111720_rls_policies.sql`
- **Risk:** From the code: no policy or helper reads the `aal` claim, and local TOTP enrolment is off. Turning MFA on in the hosted dashboard lets staff enrol but does not require it, so a stolen staff password still reads every patient.
- **Fix:** Turn on TOTP in `config.toml`, and require `aal2` for staff, in the helpers or in a restrictive policy, once the enrolment screen exists. This must be done before real patient data is held.

#### S-12 CSP allows inline scripts

- **Status:** RESOLVED in the working tree (`lib/csp.ts`, `lib/supabase/proxy.ts`, `next.config.ts`, `app/layout.tsx`), not yet committed. Follow-ons: S-57, and `style-src 'unsafe-inline'` below.
- **Files:** `lib/csp.ts`, `lib/supabase/proxy.ts` (nonce per request), `next.config.ts` (CSP removed there), `app/layout.tsx` (`await connection()`)
- **Re-test [2026-11] (fifth run), against a production build:**
  - **Every route has a CSP and a nonce.** `/`, `/login`, `/forgot-password`, `/update-password`, `/portal`, `/clinic`, `/dev/ui`, `/nonexistent`, `/_not-found`, `/LOGIN`, `/robots.txt`, `/login?error=expired`, and the redirects from `/auth/confirm` (with no parameters, a junk token and `next=//evil.com`) all carry `Content-Security-Policy`, including 307 redirects and 404s. `GET`, `HEAD`, `POST` and `OPTIONS` carried it. `/dev/ui` returns 404 in production with the policy. The build lists every route as dynamic, so none is served from a prerendered file.
  - **No unreplaced nonce and no script without one.** On `/login`, `/forgot-password`, `/nonexistent`, `/dev/ui`, `/login?error=expired`, and the signed-in `/clinic` (49 scripts) and `/portal` (24), every `<script>` tag, inline or external, carried exactly the nonce in that response's header. No placeholder text was in any page. The nonce differs in every response (checked on repeated requests). It is the base64 of a `crypto.randomUUID()`, about 122 bits from a CSPRNG.
  - **The client cannot choose the nonce.** A request that sent `Content-Security-Policy`, `x-nonce` and `x-middleware-request-content-security-policy` headers with `ATTACKER` in them got a page and header with no trace of it (the proxy overwrites the forwarded headers). No `x-nonce` is sent back.
  - **No `unsafe-eval` in production** (0 matches in the header). `lib/csp.ts` adds it, and `ws://localhost:*`, only when `NODE_ENV` is not `production`.
  - **Not cacheable.** Every dynamic response had `Cache-Control: private, no-cache, no-store, max-age=0, must-revalidate`, so a nonce cannot be served from a shared cache. The Vercel setting must not override this: see the checklist.
  - **In a browser:** the sign-in and clinic pages hydrate and client navigation works under the policy. A parser-inserted inline event handler (`onerror`), a `javascript:` link and a frame of another origin were blocked, with `securitypolicyviolation` events. Inline script and `eval` from the debugging tool's own context were not blocked, but that context may bypass the page's CSP, so those two results are not evidence either way. The conclusion about script rests on the header and the nonce checks.
- **What `style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'` still allows, recorded honestly.** It exists because React and Radix write `style` attributes at runtime (the pages already hold one) and a nonce cannot cover an attribute. An attacker who can inject markup can therefore still:
  - Restyle, hide or overlay the page, for example draw a fake prompt over a real form (UI redress). That is a real risk on a screen showing health data.
  - Read values out of attributes with CSS selectors, but only send them to places the policy allows: `img-src` and `connect-src` allow `'self'`, `data:`, `blob:` and the one Supabase origin, and `font-src` only `'self'`. Nothing reaches a host the attacker owns. The injected content cannot run script, cannot set `<base>` (`base-uri 'self'`), cannot post a form off-site (`form-action 'self'`) and cannot frame the app (`frame-ancestors 'none'`, plus `X-Frame-Options`).
  - It does not weaken `script-src`. A possible tightening, not tested: `style-src-elem 'self' 'nonce-…'` with `style-src-attr 'unsafe-inline'`, if Next adds the nonce to its `<style>` elements (none were present in the pages tested, only a `<link rel="stylesheet">` that carries the nonce).
- **Left over, not a finding:**
  - `connect-src` and `img-src` still name the Supabase origin, and `connect-src` its `wss` origin, although no browser code calls Supabase (a documented decision). They could go.
  - There is no `report-to`, so violations are seen only in the browser console, and no `upgrade-insecure-requests` (HSTS covers it in production).
  - `strict-dynamic` means a script that is already allowed can load more. That is its design.
  - Matcher exclusions get no header: S-57. A 500 error from the proxy is plain text with no CSP.
- **Original risk:** `script-src` included `'unsafe-inline'`, which removed the CSP's main protection against script injection.

#### S-13 Sign-up must be off in the hosted project

- **Status:** RESOLVED on the owner's evidence [2026-11]. The owner read `disable_signup: true` from the hosted project's `/auth/v1/settings`. This is the owner's observation of the hosted setting: this review did not connect to the hosted project and has not seen it. Recheck it after any change of Auth settings (see the checklist).
- **Files:** `supabase/config.toml`
- **Risk:** Off locally (verified: `/auth/v1/settings` reports `disable_signup: true`). If the hosted project had it on, anyone could create an account. They would get no role, but could read reference data and the staff directory, including names, titles and ids.
- **Fix:** none needed while the setting stays off.

#### S-31 Sessions never expire, and their cookies are long-lived and readable by script

- **Status:** OPEN, mostly fixed in the working tree (not committed): `lib/supabase/session-cookies.ts`, `lib/auth/session-start.ts`, `lib/auth/actions.ts`, `app/auth/confirm/route.ts`, `lib/supabase/proxy.ts`, `lib/env.ts`
- **Files:** those above, and `supabase/config.toml` (`[auth.sessions]` commented out)
- **Original risk:** the `@supabase/ssr` defaults applied: `httpOnly: false`, 400 days, no `Secure`, and no time-box or inactivity limit. Someone who closed a browser on a shared clinic computer without signing out left the next person a live staff session, and through S-37 a way to change its password. With `'unsafe-inline'` in the CSP, injected script could read the tokens (S-12 is now resolved).
- **Fixed and verified earlier [2026-11]:** `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax`, `Max-Age=604800` on all three cookies; idle and total limits (30 minutes and 12 hours for staff, 2 hours and 7 days for patients) enforced by the proxy with the refresh token revoked; staff limits on any `/clinic` path; a missing, future or non-numeric `ch_last_seen` ends the session; the `amr` timestamp is signature-checked and does not move on refresh; `/auth/confirm` no longer clears the idle clock (S-46).
- **Fixed and verified in the fifth run [2026-11], production build:**
  - **`Secure` is now observed, not read from the code:** all three cookies are set `Secure; HttpOnly; SameSite=lax; Max-Age=604800` at sign-in.
  - **The two `ch_*` cookies are `value.signature`,** an HMAC-SHA256 over `name|user id|value`, checked with the Web Crypto `verify` (constant time). Each of these, on an otherwise valid session, ended the session with a redirect to `/login?error=expired` and cleared the cookies: an unsigned `ch_last_seen` set to now; a zero signature; a signature taken from `ch_session_kind` and put on `ch_last_seen`; a value signed for another account (Eleanor's, by hand); Eleanor's real `ch_last_seen` from her own sign-in; a truncated signature; an odd-length one; upper-case hex of the same bytes; non-hex characters; an empty signature; no dot; a value edited after signing (+1). A missing `ch_last_seen`, and a lone `ch_session_kind`, also ended it. A valid signed value was accepted, and a signed value 28 minutes old was accepted and renewed, 31 minutes old refused, 5 minutes in the future refused, 30 seconds in the future accepted (the skew allowance).
  - **The kind edit on `/portal` no longer works.** A staff session idle for 1 hour with `ch_session_kind=patient` unsigned, or with the staff signature reused on `patient`, or signed `staff`, was refused on `/portal` (an unknown kind gets staff limits). Only a validly signed `patient` kind gets patient limits, and only the server signs one.
  - **A patient** idle 1 hour is accepted on `/portal`, refused at 2 hours 5 minutes, and refused on `/clinic` at 1 hour (staff limits there). A patient with no kind and 1 hour idle is refused on `/portal`.
  - **Real server-issued cookies work:** sign-in sets both `ch_*` cookies signed, and `/clinic` gave 200. A valid recovery link set a signed `ch_last_seen` (age 0, and its signature matched an independent computation), no kind, and `/update-password` and `/portal` then gave 200. `startNewSession` now calls `getUser()` first, so a failed call sets no cookie and the next request ends the session (fail closed, from the code).
  - **The secret is required and fails closed.** With `SESSION_COOKIE_SECRET` unset, or set to 5 characters, anonymous pages still worked, but every request that carried a session returned a plain 500 (`Invalid session environment: SESSION_COOKIE_SECRET` in the server log, which names the variable and never a value), including `/login`, and the sign-in action returned 500. Nothing is allowed through. S-60 records the side effects.
  - **The secret is in no client bundle or log.** The local secret's value appears in 0 files of `.next`, and in 0 lines of the server log. The variable's name appears in one client chunk (S-59), which is names only.
- **What remains:**
  - **A copied token is outside these limits.** The same person can copy the refresh token from devtools. Verified earlier: past the 12-hour limit, it refreshed twice directly against `/auth/v1/token` and read a patient row through the Data API. It was revoked only when the app next saw that session. A session whose browser never returns is never revoked. Only Supabase's own settings end this, and they need the **Pro plan** (Supabase docs: "only available on Pro Plans and up"). They would apply to the whole project, not per role.
  - **A signed timestamp is replayable until its window passes.** A `ch_last_seen` stays valid for 30 minutes (staff) or 2 hours (patients) after it is issued, however many times it is presented. That cannot lengthen an idle session, because an old value only expires sooner, but it is not tied to the Auth session: verified, the real `ch_last_seen` and `ch_session_kind` from one sign-in were accepted on a different, newer session of the same account (200). Whoever holds a copy of the cookies from a recent window can reuse them with any session for that account until it passes.
  - **The kind cookie has no expiry of its own,** so a signed `patient` kind stays valid for up to 7 days or until the next sign-in or password change. A person given a staff role in mid-session keeps patient limits outside `/clinic`: S-54.
  - **One static secret, no versions.** Changing it signs everyone out at once (acceptable, and noted in `.env.example`), and nothing detects a weak value beyond 32 characters. The local development secret is public in `.claude/launch.json`: it must never be used in Vercel.
  - **On-screen data:** S-48 is resolved, with S-61. **Actions at excluded paths:** S-53. **How the total limit is measured:** S-54.
- **Fix:** turn on the Supabase settings once on Pro. Until then a human must accept the copied-token route in writing before S-31 can be closed. Optionally put the Auth session id in the signed message, and add an expiry to the signed kind.

#### S-32 Rate limits count the app server, not the attacker

- **Status:** OPEN, partly fixed in the working tree (not committed): `20261102143000_auth_throttle.sql`, `lib/auth/throttle.ts`, `lib/auth/actions.ts`. **CAPTCHA is deliberately deferred by the owner until the clinic has the accounts** [2026-11]; the rest of the fix (the hosted limits) is in the checklist.
- **Files:** those above, and `supabase/config.toml` (`[auth.rate_limit]`)
- **Original risk:** sign-in and reset go through server actions, so the Auth service sees the app server's address for everyone. With no limit in the action, all users shared one limit, and anyone could use it up and block the clinic.
- **Fixed and verified [2026-11]:**
  - Sign-in is limited per 15 minutes to 10 per address and account, 30 per account and 60 per address. Reset is limited per hour to 3 per address and account, 3 per account and 20 per address.
  - 10 wrong passwords from one address then refuse the attempt, and 30 from three addresses then refuse the correct password from a fresh address.
  - The limits apply the same way to an account that does not exist, so the throttle itself reveals nothing. The email's case does not make a new bucket.
  - `anon` and `authenticated` get 401 and 403 on the RPC over HTTP. `private.auth_throttle` cannot be read by either, or by `service_role`. The only `private` function `service_role` can execute is `auth_throttle_hit`, so its new `usage` on the schema adds nothing else. The function is `SECURITY DEFINER` with `search_path = ''`. It has no `auth.uid()` check, which is correct, as only `service_role` may call it.
- **What remains:**
  - **Direct calls do not pass through the throttle.** `/auth/v1/token` and `/auth/v1/recover` take the public key, which is in no way secret. Verified: with the app refusing an account, a direct call with the correct password returned 200. Those calls depend entirely on Supabase's per-IP limit, so a distributed attacker has no per-account limit there.
  - **The hosted shared limit can still be reached.** Calls through the app still all come from the app server's address. The app allows far more from a few client addresses than `sign_in_sign_ups = 30` per 5 minutes would, if the hosted limit is like that. Then everyone gets "Too many attempts".
  - **Not testable here.** The local Auth container has no `GOTRUE_RATE_LIMIT_HEADER`, and 180 sign-ins in a burst through the app got no 429, so no per-IP limit was seen. The sign-in 429 branch has never run. This corrects the earlier reading that `config.toml` sets a working local limit. Treat the hosted behaviour as unknown.
  - Follow-ons: S-49, S-50, S-51, S-52. S-47 is resolved.
- **Fix:** Review the hosted limits (see the checklist). The local Auth image contains handling for an `Sb-Forwarded-For` header, which may let the server pass the client address to Auth: check whether the hosted project supports it, as that would address the shared limit properly. Consider a CAPTCHA on both forms, which Supabase Auth would also apply to direct calls.

#### S-46 Opening `/auth/confirm` clears the idle clock

- **Status:** RESOLVED in the working tree (`lib/supabase/proxy.ts`, `app/auth/confirm/route.ts`, `lib/supabase/session-cookies.ts`), not yet committed
- **Files:** `lib/supabase/proxy.ts` (`NEW_SESSION_PATH`), `app/auth/confirm/route.ts` (`startNewSession`)
- **Original risk:** the proxy skipped the limits on `/auth/confirm` and deleted `ch_last_seen` and `ch_session_kind` before anyone knew whether the link was valid. A staff session idle for 1 hour could be brought back by opening that address with a junk `token_hash`, with no developer tools.
- **Re-test [2026-11]:** a staff session with `ch_last_seen` 1 hour old opened `/auth/confirm` with a junk `token_hash` and `type=recovery`, `type=bogus` or `type=email`, with `code=junk`, and with no parameters. Each gave a redirect to `/login?error=link`, set no `ch_*` cookie, and left `ch_last_seen` exactly as it was. `/clinic` straight afterwards gave a redirect to `/login?error=expired`. A valid recovery link (made through the local Auth admin endpoint) opened on the same idle session set a fresh `ch_last_seen`, deleted `ch_session_kind` and redirected to `/update-password`, which returned 200. The route's `Set-Cookie` headers survive its `redirect()`.
- **Left over, not a finding:** after a valid link the kind is unknown, so staff limits apply until the password is set. Safe, only stricter. The proxy still skips the limits on `/auth/confirm` itself, so an over-limit session is not ended by that request, only by the next one (verified above).

#### S-47 The reset reply reveals an account once the email quota is used up

- **Status:** RESOLVED in the working tree (`lib/auth/actions.ts`, `app/(auth)/forgot-password/forgot-password-form.tsx`), not yet committed
- **Files:** `lib/auth/actions.ts` (`requestPasswordReset`)
- **Original risk:** Auth returns 200 for an unknown address without sending anything, so its email quota can only be used up by a real account. The action showed "Too many attempts" for a 429 and so told the caller which addresses had accounts.
- **Re-test [2026-11]:** against a temporary local Auth container with `GOTRUE_RATE_LIMIT_EMAIL_SENT=2` and `GOTRUE_MAILER_AUTOCONFIRM=false`, behind the loopback proxy described above. Seven resets in a row went through the real server action: an unknown address, five real accounts and another unknown address. All seven returned 200 with the "sent" screen, and none showed "Too many attempts". A direct call to the container confirmed it was limiting: a real account then returned 429 `over_email_send_rate_limit`, and an unknown address returned 200. The server log held one line, "Password reset email limited by Supabase.", for each of the three real accounts that were limited, with no address in it. The app's own throttle still works: through the app, the fourth reset for one account from one address in an hour showed "Too many attempts", which is the same for any address. The "sent" screen now ends with "If nothing arrives in a few minutes, try again later or contact the clinic."
- **Left over:**
  - The log line has no alert on it (S-50 applies in the same way). A real person whose reset is limited gets no email and a "sent" screen, so the new wording is their only help.
  - The action ignores every Supabase error, not only 429, and logs only the 429. A 500 or a network failure gives "sent" with nothing in the log. Log any error, still with no address.
  - Reset timing may still differ for a real account: S-34.

#### S-48 A page already open stays readable after the limits pass

- **Status:** RESOLVED in the working tree (`components/shell/session-guard.tsx`, `components/shell/app-shell.tsx`, the layouts), not yet committed. Follow-on: S-61.
- **Files:** `components/shell/session-guard.tsx`, `lib/supabase/proxy.ts`
- **Re-test [2026-11] (fifth run), production build in the browser pane:** a staff session was loaded on `/clinic` (the page rendered with the guard). Its `ch_last_seen` was then made 4000 seconds old and the idle timer was re-armed with its delay shortened. The timer was created with the staff delay of 1,800,000 ms (30 minutes), as the code says. After 3 seconds it called `router.refresh()`, the proxy ended the session, and the pane went to `/login?error=expired` with every cookie gone and the clinic screen replaced. The heartbeat was tested earlier by the main session.
- **Not tested:** the real 30-minute wait, and a device that sleeps (S-61).
- **Original risk:** the limits were checked only when a request arrived and no browser timer existed, so whatever was on screen stayed readable until somebody clicked.
