- **Note:** this is not a security boundary, as the file says. The proxy remains that, and the server idle clock moves only on a heartbeat (at most one every 5 minutes of activity), so the server may end a session up to about 4 minutes before the screen's own timer would.

#### S-68 An email change completes on one link, with no password, and the old address is not told

- **Status:** OPEN, partly fixed. The notice to the old address is built and enabled in `config.toml` (verified locally: it reaches the old address when a change is confirmed). Still open: one link completes the change, and the Data API takes no password.
- **Files:** `supabase/config.toml` (`double_confirm_changes`), `lib/profile/actions.ts` (`requestEmailChange`), the Auth service
- **Risk:** a session is enough to take an account for good. Through the Data API, `PUT /auth/v1/user` with `{"email": ...}` and a session token started an email change with no password (200, a pending `email_change`, two emails sent). Both emails went out, as `double_confirm_changes = true` says, yet `POST /auth/v1/verify` with only the token from the **new** address finished the change: `auth.users.email` and, through the S-23 trigger, `profiles.email` became the new address, and the call returned a full session. The old address's link was never used. So someone who holds a session (an unattended browser, a copied token: S-31, S-37) and a mailbox of their own can move the sign-in email, then use "forgot password" to set a password nobody else knows. The victim's usual remedy, changing the password and signing other devices out, no longer helps, because a reset now goes to the attacker. Before the fix no message told the old address; now the `email_changed` notice does, after the fact. It warns, it does not prevent.
- **Verified [2026-11]** on the local Auth image, with Thomas's account (put back through the Auth admin API afterwards, with `profiles.email` following). The app's own form is stricter: it checks the current password first (a wrong one is refused) and is limited to 5 an hour. What the hosted project does with `double_confirm_changes` was not tested.
- **Fix:** (1) **Done locally:** the email-changed notification for the old address (`[auth.email.notification.email_changed]`, branded template). Switch it on and paste the template in the hosted dashboard. (2) Test the hosted behaviour: request a change and confirm that the old address's link is required; if one link still finishes it, treat the notification as the only control. (3) Decide whether staff and patients may change their own sign-in email at all (PRD decision: yes, double-confirmed), or whether the clinic does it, because the Data API cannot be closed. (4) MFA (S-11) is the real answer for staff.

#### S-69 Any signed-in person can find out whether an email address has an account

- **Status:** OPEN (found in the ninth run)
- **Files:** the Auth service (`PUT /auth/v1/user`); `lib/profile/actions.ts` hides it only in the app
- **Risk:** the app's reply to an email change is the same whether or not the address is taken (verified: seven requests for existing and unused addresses, all "If that address can be used...", about 70 to 80 ms each, so no timing gap locally). The Auth API is not so careful. With one patient's token, eight requests each for `gina.schoeman@example.test` and `admin@example.test` all returned 422 `email_exists` ("A user with this email address has already been registered"), and an unused address returned 200 once and then 429 `over_email_send_rate_limit`. There was no limit on the 422s. A patient, or a guardian, can therefore test any list of addresses, and a yes means "this person is a patient or staff member of a hormone and longevity clinic", which is health information about them. Sign-up is off and reset and sign-in are uniform, so this is the one open route.
- **Verified [2026-11]** locally, from `curl`. Eleanor's pending change to an unused address was cleared afterwards.
- **Fix:** it cannot be closed in the app, because the Data API stays open to every session. Options: check whether the hosted Auth rate limits count the 422 replies (they did not locally), or accept it as a clinic decision. A limit in front of Auth is not possible, because Auth is on Supabase's host. Patient addresses are the sensitive ones. Record the decision. Not closed by MFA.

#### S-74 An invitation link is the only check on a record, and on a staff role

- **Status:** OPEN (reduced [2026-11]; found in the tenth run). Patient and guardian links: the date-of-birth proof and the address confirmation are in place (`20261105130000_invitation_identity.sql`, from the code and the grants; the app flow was not re-attacked). Team links: unchanged, the link is still the only check, and staff still have no MFA (S-11).
- **Files:** `lib/invitations/accept-actions.ts` (`createAccountFromInvitation`, `acceptInvitationWhileSignedIn`, `checkIdentity`), `20261105120000_invitations.sql` (`private.accept_invitation`), `20261105130000_invitation_identity.sql` (`private.verify_invitation_identity`, `private.create_invitation`)
- **Risk:** whoever holds the link, for any reason, becomes that person: the account is created with `email_confirm: true` and a password the holder chooses, and no second fact is asked. For a **patient** invitation that is the whole portal for the record (results, letters, medications, messages); for a **guardian** invitation it is the same view of someone else's record, with `can_act` if the inviter ticked it. The usual causes are ordinary: an address mistyped by the PA (a stranger's mailbox gets a patient's record), a shared or forwarded mailbox, a compromised mailbox, a link copied into chat. Verified: with only the link and a password of my own, an account was made and bound to the patient record (Oliver Grant) and the guardian link was made for an existing account; nothing compared the address with `patient_profiles.contact_email`, the date of birth or anything the patient knows. For a **team** invitation the holder becomes a doctor, PA, health coach or admin on the link alone, and staff have no MFA yet (S-11). The link lasts 3 days and works once, which limits the window, not the harm. The cookie that carries the token lives for one hour after a failed attempt, so on a shared computer the next person can finish it.
- **Done [2026-11]:** fix points (1) is built: both accept forms ask for the date (the patient's own for a patient link, the patient's for a guardian link); `checkIdentity` calls `verify_invitation_identity` before `createUser`, so a wrong date creates no account; `accept_invitation` checks the date again and refuses without it; five wrong dates set `revoked_at` and notify the sender; `create_invitation` refuses a patient invitation whose address differs from `contact_email` until the sender ticks a confirmation (hint `email_mismatch`). The address check does not run when the record has no `contact_email`, when the record is created in the same step, or for guardians (S-86). Points (2) to (4) below are not built.
- **Fix:** (1) For patient and guardian invitations, ask for the patient's date of birth (or the guardian's, with the patient's) on the accept page, checked in `accept_invitation` against the record, with a small attempt limit per link; and refuse or warn when the invited address differs from `contact_email`. (2) Show the PA the address a second time before sending (type it twice, or a confirmation line). (3) For team roles, require MFA enrolment at the first staff sign-in (S-11), and decide on notice or approval for doctor and admin invitations (PRD open question). (4) Clear the cookie on a failed final step as well as on success.

#### S-75 One PA, or one admin, can bind any patient record to any address

- **Status:** OPEN (eased [2026-11]; found in the tenth run; extends S-42 and the S-03 note about admins)
- **Files:** `20261105120000_invitations.sql` (`private.create_invitation`, `private.may_invite`, `private.get_invitable_patients`), `lib/invitations/actions.ts`
- **Risk:** `create_invitation` takes any `_patient_id` that has no account, from any inviter who passes `may_invite`: all PAs and all admins. There is no second person, no check on the address (S-74) and no notice. Verified: Priya (PA) created a patient invitation for Oliver Grant to an address of her choosing, with extra forged fields ignored; the admin did the same (an invitation for the same record, to another address, was created by `admin@example.test`), and accepting it gives the holder the patient's portal exactly as in S-74. For a PA this is inside her clinical reach, but one person can disclose a record (S-42). **For an admin it breaks the decision that admin is not clinical staff:** an admin can see every patient's name through the form's picker (S-83), invite a second mailbox of their own to any patient record or as a guardian with `can_act`, and read that patient's released results, letters and medications through the portal. Admins can also invite their own second address as a doctor, which they could already approximate by editing `staff_profiles` (S-22). The audit log names the inviter, so it is traceable afterwards, but only if someone looks (nothing alerts).
- **Eased [2026-11]:** (a) to bind an existing record the inviter's holder now needs the patient's date of birth at acceptance; `get_invitable_patients` does not return it and an admin has no read on `patient_profiles` (the policy is `in_scope` for clinical staff), so an admin cannot supply it for an existing record, though a PA can read it. (b) A doctor or admin invitation sends an in-portal notice to every other admin, and a guardian invitation with `can_act` to every other PA (verified in the tests; the sender is not told, and a notice does not block). (c) A different address from the record needs a ticked confirmation. Still open: one person decides, a notice is not an approval; patient invitations and a guardian invitation without `can_act` (which still grants a read of the whole record) send no notice; an admin can create a new patient record with a date of birth of their own and invite an address to it, but that is a new record, not a disclosure.
- **Fix:** decide first (PRD open questions): whether admins may invite patients and guardians at all (the simplest rule is that only PAs and doctors do, and admins invite team members only); whether a second person confirms a guardian with `can_act` and a team invitation for doctor or admin; and whether a PA's patient invitation must go to the address on the record. Then enforce it in `may_invite` and `create_invitation`. Add a notification to the other admins when a team invitation for `admin` or `doctor` is sent or accepted.

#### S-100 The Activity screen shows safeguarding and other clinical-table events to an admin

- **Status:** RESOLVED [2026-11] in the uncommitted `20261110120100_nurse_gender_and_record_views.sql` (found in the twelfth run). `activity_log` now returns only `patient_profiles`, `lab_reports`, `results`, `lab_orders`, `documents`, `result_imports`, `invitations`, `staff_profiles`, `user_roles` and `profiles`, so safeguarding, diagnoses, medications, consents and allergies never appear. Verified by reading the function; `pnpm db:test` passes with the coordinator's new tests, and I did not repeat my own attack after the fix. The listed `user_roles`, `invitations`, `staff_profiles` and `profiles` rows show an admin who changed roles and accounts, which is within an admin's remit.
- **Files:** `20261110120100_nurse_gender_and_record_views.sql` (`private.activity_log`), `app/clinic/activity/page.tsx`
- **Risk:** `activity_log` reads every `audit_log` row and has no list of entities. `write_audit_log` is attached to 27 tables, including `safeguarding_alerts`, `diagnoses`, `medications`, `consents`, `patient_allergies` and `repeat_requests`. The `CLAUDE.md` rule is that safeguarding alerts are for doctors only, and an admin is meant to see names but no clinical records. Verified on the local database: as the seed admin, `activity_log` returned three `safeguarding_alerts` / `created` rows and `diagnoses` / `created` rows (actor and patient blank only because the seed has no actor and the patient is not resolved for those entities). With real use the admin would see "Added: safeguarding_alerts" with the doctor's name and the time, which discloses that an alert exists and when, and the page's `words()` fallback prints any unknown entity name. No value, no `changed` column list and no patient name for those entities is returned today, so this is existence and timing, not content.
- **Fix:** in `private.activity_log`, return only an allow-list of entities (for example `patient_profiles`, `lab_reports`, `results`, `documents`, `lab_orders`, the same five the page labels) and exclude `safeguarding_alerts`, `diagnoses`, `medications`, `consents`, `patient_allergies`. Add a pgTAP test that an admin sees no `safeguarding_alerts` row. Decide separately whether an admin should see `created` events on results (S-104).

#### S-101 `log_record_view` lets clinical staff write unlimited, unbound, permanent rows

- **Status:** RESOLVED [2026-11] in the uncommitted `20261110120100_nurse_gender_and_record_views.sql` (found in the twelfth run). A `lab_reports` id must now exist and belong to `_patient`, and a `patient_profiles` id must equal `_patient`, otherwise 22023. Made-up ids can no longer be written, so the log is bounded by real records, each at most once every 10 minutes per person. No per-actor cap was added. Residual, LOW and not a new finding: one person can still write one row per real report per 10 minutes, which is slow and bounded by the clinic's own records.
- **Files:** `20261110120100_nurse_gender_and_record_views.sql` (`private.log_record_view`, `public.log_record_view`)
- **Risk:** The function checks the caller is clinical staff and `in_scope(_patient)`, and the entity name is on a list of two, but `_entity_id` is accepted as given. (1) It is not checked to be a record of `_patient`, or to exist: verified that a PA wrote three `viewed` / `lab_reports` rows for three random uuids in one transaction. (2) The 10-minute repeat check is keyed on actor, entity and `_entity_id`, so a new id defeats it: any doctor, PA or health coach can add rows without limit by calling `/rest/v1/rpc/log_record_view` with their own session, and the table is append-only, so the rows can never be removed (even by the service role). That fills the log that the Activity screen and a CQC audit rely on, and costs the clinic storage and screen pages. (3) The same freedom lets a staff member write a false trail: a `viewed` row naming another colleague is not possible (the actor is `auth.uid()`), but a row saying the caller opened a report that belongs to another patient is, and the Activity screen resolves the patient from `metadata.patient_id` first. (4) A deliberately incomplete log is easy: nothing stops a person calling the function for every patient, which makes real opens hard to find.
- **Fix:** for `lab_reports` require `exists (select 1 from public.lab_reports where id = _entity_id and patient_id = _patient)` and for `patient_profiles` require `_entity_id is null or _entity_id = _patient`; reject otherwise with 22023. Add a per-actor cap (for example, refuse after 200 `viewed` rows in the last hour). Consider calling the function only from server code with a fixed form, so a browser user cannot choose the arguments (the app has no browser client, but the Data API is still open to any signed-in session). Add tests for a mismatched id and for the cap.

#### S-106 Any Google account can be connected, including a personal one, so clinic event details can leave the clinic's control

- **Status:** ACCEPTED [2026-11-11] by the project owner: any Google account may be connected, personal accounts included, by staff only. Since the review, `exchangeCode` requires `email_verified` and enforces `GOOGLE_WORKSPACE_DOMAIN` when it is set; it is left unset, so this stays an accepted risk. Mitigations in place: staff only (active staff profile, not suspended), the add form asks for a clinic reference instead of a patient's name, and disconnecting revokes access.
- **Files:** `lib/calendar/google.ts` (`authorisationUrl` lines 40-55, `exchangeCode` lines 90-93), `app/api/google/callback/route.ts` (lines 44-51)
- **Risk:** The consent request carries no `hd` (hosted domain) hint, and the callback stores whatever address Google returns: it does not check `hd` or `email_verified` in the userinfo reply, and the migration only limits the address to 3 to 320 characters. A staff member can therefore connect a personal Gmail account. Events written from Clinic Hub (title, place, notes up to 4,000 characters) then live in an account the clinic does not own, cannot audit or wipe, and that is not covered by any data processing agreement, and they stay there after the person leaves. The form asks staff to use a clinic reference rather than a name, but that is advice only (PRD); a title such as "Ref 1234 testosterone review" is still special-category data once linked to the reference. The same applies to the connect screen telling staff to click through Google's "not verified" warning: if the OAuth app is External, any Google account can grant it, and staff are taught to dismiss a security warning. Event changes made here are not in the audit log (by design, PRD), so there is no record of what was sent.
- **Fix:** Decide which accounts are allowed. If only the clinic's Google Workspace: set the OAuth consent screen to **Internal** in the clinic's own Google Cloud project (removes the unverified warning and refuses outside accounts at Google), add `hd: <domain>` to the authorisation parameters, and in `exchangeCode` refuse (and revoke) unless the userinfo reply has `email_verified === true` and `hd` equal to a new server-only `GOOGLE_WORKSPACE_DOMAIN`. Add a check constraint or function check on the domain if wanted. If personal accounts are to be allowed, a human records the accepted risk here, with the reason, and the staff policy says what may go in an event. Until decided, this stays OPEN.

#### S-112 A staff member on their own care team, or any PA or admin who is a patient, reaches their own restricted record

- **Status:** RESOLVED [2026-11] in `a55db7b` (on `dev`), read from the code in the fifteenth run and not re-attacked: `private.in_scope` in `20261112120100_access_by_care_team.sql` now starts with `_patient_id not in (select private.my_patient_ids())`, so nobody opens their own record, or that of a patient they are a verified guardian of with `can_view`, as staff; and the `care_team_reject_self` trigger refuses a care-team row whose staff member is the patient. `release_report` goes through `in_scope`, so it follows. A guardian link without `can_view` does not exclude the record (a lesser case). Earlier: OPEN [2026-11] (fourteenth run). **Fix before merge:** the knowledge base records the clinic's answer to question 4 ("A doctor who is also a patient sees, as a patient, only what any patient sees") as built, and the database does not enforce it.
- **Files:** `supabase/migrations/20261112120100_access_by_care_team.sql` (`private.in_scope`, lines 16 to 37 of the committed file; the care-team insert and update policies, lines 334 to 347); `20261102111704_patient_record.sql` and `20261102111701_team_and_sites.sql` (no rule that a care-team member is not the patient)
- **Risk:** `in_scope(p)` is true for a PA or admin for every `p`, and for a doctor, nurse or coach with a current `care_team` row for `p`. Nothing excludes the patient record whose `user_id` is the caller, or a patient the caller is a guardian of. Nothing stops a care-team row that names the patient's own staff profile either. **Verified** on the local stand-in, in a rolled-back transaction, with the seed doctor Laura, who is also patient `…000b`: with an unreleased report and a safeguarding alert on her own record, she read neither; Dr Schoeman (on Laura's care team) then added Laura to it, which was accepted; Laura then read her own unreleased report (1 row) and her own safeguarding alert (1 row), wrote a clinical note on herself, released her own report through `release_report`, and added a health coach to her own care team. A PA or admin who is also a patient needs no care-team row: `in_scope` is already true for their own record, so they read their unreleased results, other people's notes about them and, for an admin, everything else an admin reads (from the code; there is no such seed user). A safeguarding alert can concern the staff member as a risk, so this is the most sensitive case. This also widens S-92: entering and releasing one's own results now needs a care-team row, but one colleague's click gives it.
- **Fix:** In `private.in_scope`, add `and not private.is_own_patient(_patient_id)` and, if guardians who are staff are to see only what a guardian sees, `and not exists (select 1 from public.patient_guardians g where g.patient_id = _patient_id and g.guardian_user_id = (select auth.uid()))`. Refuse a `care_team` row whose staff member's `user_id` equals the patient's `user_id` (a `before insert or update` trigger, since a check constraint cannot look across tables), so nobody can be put on their own team. Make `release_report` refuse the releaser's own record whatever `in_scope` says. Add pgTAP cases: a doctor-patient on her own team, a PA-patient and an admin-patient, each reading their own unreleased report and safeguarding alert, and the care-team insert itself.

#### S-119 Any signed-in person reads any clinician's diary, past and future

- **Status:** RESOLVED [2026-11] in `29f602f` (on `dev`), read from the code in the sixteenth run, not attacked. `private.clinician_busy` returns rows only to active staff or when `_staff` is on the current care team of a patient the caller sees (`private.is_my_clinician`, through `my_patient_ids()`), and clamps the range to now until 121 days ahead; the `clinician_availability` read policy is narrowed the same way. `13_ghl_and_booking` was changed with it. Earlier: OPEN (fifteenth run), fix before merge.
- **Files:** `supabase/migrations/20261113120500_booking.sql` (`private.clinician_busy`, lines 86 to 106; grants, lines 248 to 258; "Anyone signed in reads clinician hours", lines 75 to 76); `lib/booking/slots.ts` line 37
- **Risk:** `clinician_busy(_staff, _from, _to)` is a definer function that any `authenticated` caller may run for any staff id over any range. Its only check is that someone is signed in. Every staff id is handed to every signed-in person by `get_staff_directory`. A patient, or a guardian, can therefore list the start and end of every booked or confirmed appointment of every clinician for any period, including the past and appointments copied from other systems, not just the clinicians they may book. No name is returned, but the result is still personal data about other patients: the length of a slot often identifies the appointment type (type names and lengths are readable by everyone), and anyone who knows when a relative, partner or colleague was at the clinic can confirm it, see how often they return, and with which clinician. The app only ever asks for the next 14 days of a care-team clinician, so the wider reach serves no feature. `clinician_availability` (hours and site of every clinician) is also readable by every signed-in person; that is lower risk but equally wider than needed.
- **Fix:** Inside `private.clinician_busy`, return rows only when the caller is active staff, or `_staff` is on the current care team of a patient the caller can act for (the same rule as `get_bookable_clinicians`); and clamp the range to the booking window (`greatest(_from, now())` to `least(_to, now() + interval '120 days')`), refusing anything wider. Narrow the `clinician_availability` read policy the same way. Add pgTAP cases: a patient asking for a clinician outside their care team gets nothing, and a past range returns nothing. Change the existing assertion at `13_ghl_and_booking.test.sql` line 80 to use a patient who shares the clinician.

#### S-120 A patient can book without limit and cannot cancel

- **Status:** RESOLVED [2026-11] in `29f602f` (on `dev`), read from the code: when the caller books as a patient (not in scope), `book_appointment` refuses a fourth open future booking (22023), and `bookAppointment` is throttled (`booking`: 20 an hour per account). Patients still cannot cancel; that is the clinic's design (only staff cancel, 48-hour policy), not part of this finding any more. Earlier: OPEN (fifteenth run).
- **Files:** `supabase/migrations/20261113120500_booking.sql` (`private.book_appointment`, lines 109 to 174); `lib/booking/actions.ts` lines 35 to 65
- **Risk:** Nothing limits how many appointments a patient or guardian books. One account, by mistake, by a script against `public.book_appointment`, or once taken over, can fill every free slot of each care-team clinician for the next 120 days. Patients cannot cancel, so staff must cancel each booking by hand, and each booking also creates a Google Calendar event and sends an email and a WhatsApp message through GoHighLevel. Patients are invited, so this needs a real account, but it denies care to every other patient of those clinicians. The server action has no throttle either.
- **Fix:** In `book_appointment`, when the caller books as a patient (not in scope), refuse when the patient already has more than a small number of open future `clinic_hub` bookings booked by a non-staff person (for example 2 or 3, a clinic decision), and perhaps one per day. Add a per-person throttle to `bookAppointment` with the existing `allowAuthAttempt` pattern. Add a pgTAP case for the cap.

#### S-121 GoHighLevel can deliver a patient's messages to an old or someone else's address

- **Status:** RESOLVED [2026-11] in `29f602f` (on `dev`), read from the code: `notifyPatient` upserts the GoHighLevel contact on every send with today's email and phone, sends only the fields the chosen channels need, and a unique partial index on `patient_profiles.ghl_contact_id` makes `set_patient_ghl_contact` fail when the contact already belongs to another patient, in which case email goes through Resend and WhatsApp is not sent. The GoHighLevel duplicate-contact setting is still a checklist item. Earlier: OPEN (fifteenth run).
- **Files:** `lib/notify/patient.ts` lines 44 to 62 and 69; `lib/ghl/client.ts` lines 46 to 57; `supabase/migrations/20261113120300_ghl_contact_link.sql`; `20261102111704_patient_record.sql` line 15 (no unique index on `ghl_contact_id`)
- **Risk:** Clinic Hub checks the patient's current address, then sends to a GoHighLevel contact id, and GoHighLevel uses whatever address that contact holds. Three ways it goes wrong. (1) The contact id is stored the first time and never refreshed. When a patient changes their sign-in email (for example after losing a shared mailbox) or their phone, every later message, including "a new result is ready", still goes to the old address held in GoHighLevel. (2) `/contacts/upsert` finds an existing contact by email or phone. Where a parent and child, or a couple, share a phone or an email, the patient is matched to the other person's contact, which may be updated with this patient's name; both patients' messages then go to one contact, and the stored id points at someone else. Nothing stops two patients holding the same `ghl_contact_id`. (3) The upsert sends the email address to GoHighLevel even when the patient turned email off and only wants WhatsApp. The messages hold no values, but they reveal that the person is a patient of the clinic and has a new result or an appointment.
- **Fix:** On every send, upsert (or update) the contact with the current email and phone, or store the email and phone last sent with the contact id and refresh the contact when either changes. After an upsert, refuse to use a contact whose id is already held by another patient: add a unique index on `patient_profiles.ghl_contact_id` (nulls allowed) so `set_patient_ghl_contact` fails, and send through Resend instead in that case. Consider a custom field holding the clinic reference, and match on it rather than email or phone. Send only the fields the chosen channels need. Confirm the GoHighLevel location's duplicate-contact setting (checklist).

#### S-122 Call recordings and transcripts are never deleted at AssemblyAI

- **Status:** RESOLVED [2026-11] in `29f602f` (on `dev`), read from the code: `completeRecording` calls `DELETE /v2/transcript/{id}` after the draft note is saved and after a job fails for good, and the signed audio link lasts 30 minutes. A failed deletion is only written to the console and never retried or recorded: S-135. The DPA, EU region and retention terms are still on the checklist. Earlier: OPEN (fifteenth run).
- **Files:** `lib/recordings/complete.ts` lines 19 to 32; `lib/transcription/assemblyai.ts` (no delete call); `lib/recordings/actions.ts` line 64
- **Risk:** Each recording is a full consultation, special-category data. AssemblyAI downloads the audio from the one-hour signed link and keeps the transcript, and its copy of the audio, on its side until it is deleted through the API. Clinic Hub copies the transcript into a draft note and then leaves it there, so every call ever recorded stays with a processor for good, beyond what the clinic needs and outside its own audit trail and retention rules. Failed jobs are left behind as well.
- **Fix:** Once `recording_transcribed` succeeds, and when a job fails for good, call `DELETE /v2/transcript/{id}` on the EU endpoint and record that it was done (for example a `provider_deleted_at` column written by a service-role function, or a line in `integration_sync_log`). Retry the deletion from the "Check now" path if it failed. Shorten the signed audio link from one hour to the time AssemblyAI needs (minutes). Confirm the DPA, the EU region and AssemblyAI's own retention terms (checklist).

#### S-150 A doctor can send a prescription for their own request, or for a patient they are only a guardian of

- **Status:** RESOLVED [2026-11], twentieth run, in `20261117120000_signaturerx.sql` (uncommitted, on `signaturerx`): `private.staff_may_send_prescription(_by, _patient)` requires an active doctor on the patient's care team today who is neither the patient nor a guardian of them; `private.can_send_to_signaturerx(_request)` (public wrapper, called from the action through the doctor's own session) applies it to an approved, uncancelled request; `private.start_signaturerx_send` repeats it for the sender, so a bug in the action cannot bypass it. `pnpm db:test` (run by me) covers: a care-team doctor may; a doctor for her own request may not; a doctor outside the care team, a PA and a request not approved may not; server code refuses the same three (42501, 22023). **No test for a doctor who is only a guardian** (the `not exists patient_guardians` branch is read, not tested). Becomes final with the commit. Original finding, nineteenth run, follows.
- **Files:** `lib/signaturerx/actions.ts` lines 79 to 89 (`sendToSignatureRx`); `20261117120000_signaturerx.sql` `private.record_signaturerx_prescription` lines 119 to 147; the read policy in `20261102111720_rls_policies.sql` line 245
- **Risk:** The action says the request is checked "through the doctor's own session: it must be approved, open, and in their scope". What it actually checks is that the doctor can **read** the row. The `repeat_requests` read policy is `patient_id in (select private.my_patient_ids()) or private.in_scope(patient_id)`, so a doctor who is also a patient reads her own requests, and a doctor who is a verified guardian reads the requests of the patient she is guardian of, without being in `in_scope` (which excludes the staff member's own record, S-112). `decide_repeat_request` does require `in_scope`, so she cannot approve her own request, but a colleague can, and she can then send it to SignatureRx herself with her own PIN, as a draft or signed and issued. The patient record is also read through the session (`getPatientForSignatureRx`), which passes for the same reason. The database function repeats no scope test: it checks that the request is approved and that `_by` is an active doctor, and trusts the service-role caller for the rest (it cannot call `private.in_scope`, which reads `auth.uid()`). Self-prescribing, and prescribing for a family member, is a professional-conduct and safeguarding matter and the clinic's rule is that a staff member never works on their own record. The same holds for medicines that are controlled drugs.
- **Fix:** Decide in the database, not in the action. Add a definer function that takes the request id, checks `has_role('doctor')`, `private.in_scope(r.patient_id)` and that the patient's `user_id` is not the caller, and have the action call it through the doctor's session before anything is sent. Have `record_signaturerx_prescription` repeat the test for `_by` (a version of `in_scope` that takes the user id), so a bug in the action cannot bypass it. Add pgTAP cases for a doctor-patient and a doctor-guardian.

#### S-151 A prescription can be sent twice, and a failed record leaves no trace in Clinic Hub

- **Status:** RESOLVED [2026-11], twentieth run, in `20261117120000_signaturerx.sql` (uncommitted): the send is opened first (`start_signaturerx_send` writes a `sending` row under a row lock on the request), `srx_prescription_id` is nullable, a partial unique index allows one open send per request (`failed`, `voided` and `expired` do not count), `finish_signaturerx_send` closes it with the id and state, `fail_signaturerx_send` only on a clear refusal (kind `auth` or `invalid` with an HTTP status), and a timeout or unreadable answer leaves it `sending` on purpose. `record_signaturerx_prescription` no longer exists. pgTAP covers a second open being refused (23505), `sending` staying after a non-closing step, and a failed send allowing another try. A failed `finish` now leaves a visible `sending` row and tells the doctor not to resend. What it leaves behind is S-162 (a stuck `sending` row cannot be cleared from the app). Whether SignatureRx refuses a repeated `client_ref_id` is still unknown (checklist). Original finding follows.
- **Files:** `lib/signaturerx/actions.ts` lines 106 to 150; `components/prescriptions/signaturerx-panel.tsx` line 144 (`showForm`); `20261117120000_signaturerx.sql` lines 84 to 98 (no unique constraint on `repeat_request_id`)
- **Risk:** (1) The form stays on the page for an approved request after one has been sent (`showForm` does not look at `panel.sent`), and the table allows many rows for one request, so a second press, a second tab or a second doctor creates a second prescription. With "Sign and issue it now" the second is also signed. The only duplicate guard is `srx_prescription_id unique`, which stops the same SignatureRx id being recorded twice, not a second prescription. Whether SignatureRx refuses a repeated `client_ref_id` (the request id is sent as it) is not known from the code. (2) Clinic Hub calls SignatureRx first and records afterwards. If `record_signaturerx_prescription` fails (network, a database error, a refused check), the prescription exists at SignatureRx and nothing in Clinic Hub says so; the doctor is told "Do not send it again: check it in SignatureRx", and the form is still offered. The `console.error` line carries the SignatureRx id, which is the only trace. (3) The throttle (20 an hour) does not prevent any of this. A duplicate prescription of a hormone, a controlled drug or a weight-loss injection is a patient-safety event, not only an untidy record.
- **Fix:** Record intent first: insert a row in a `sending` state (one open row per request, enforced by a partial unique index) before calling SignatureRx, update it with the SignatureRx id afterwards, and mark it `failed` or leave it `unknown` if the call or the record fails, so the screen can show "check SignatureRx" and refuse a new send until someone resolves it. Hide the form, and have the action refuse, when a row exists for the request unless the doctor confirms a second prescription on purpose. Check with SignatureRx whether `client_ref_id` is unique and use that as a second guard.

#### S-152 SignatureRx becomes a processor of special-category data with no data processing agreement on file

- **Status:** OPEN [2026-11], nineteenth run. Not a code fault; a condition for switching signing on.
- **Files:** `lib/signaturerx/client.ts` lines 168 to 196 (`createPrescription`); `lib/data/signaturerx.ts` lines 62 to 109
- **Risk:** Each send gives SignatureRx the patient's first and last name, sex, date of birth, postal address, phone number, email address, the clinic's own patient reference and, for each medicine, its name, quantity and directions, which together state a diagnosis or a treatment: special-category data. The prescriber's signing PIN, integration code and the clinic's client id and secret also pass through. `notify: false` is set, so SignatureRx is told not to message the patient. Clinic Hub keeps none of the medicine detail (see S-161 item 4), so SignatureRx holds the only copy of what was prescribed. Nothing in the repository shows a data processing agreement, the data location, SignatureRx's sub-processors or how long it keeps a draft that is never signed, and the patient privacy notice and consent do not yet name it.
- **Fix:** Before `SIGNATURERX_SIGNING_ENABLED` is set in production: a signed DPA (the Article 28 terms, UK data location or a transfer mechanism, sub-processor list, retention and deletion of drafts), the privacy notice and the clinic's records of processing updated, and a decision on whether the clinic keeps its own copy of the medicines sent (S-161 item 4). Confirm which of the sent fields SignatureRx requires and drop the rest. Listed in the checklist.

#### S-159 The lab report sign-off section showed "No action needed" on every released report (not in the `signaturerx` branch's code)

- **Status:** OPEN [2026-11], nineteenth run. The fix is in the working tree of `signaturerx` and **uncommitted**; this becomes RESOLVED with the commit that carries it. Found by the main session while verifying; I read the fix, not the failure.
- **Files:** `lib/data/results.ts` `getReport` line 209 (before: `const signoffRow = data.signoff;`, now `data.signoff[0] ?? null`); `lib/database.types.ts` (regenerated); `app/clinic/results/[id]/page.tsx` lines 91 to 112
- **Risk:** The query embeds `signoff:lab_report_signoffs(...)` under `lab_reports`. The link is a composite foreign key, so PostgREST returns a **list** of zero or one, not one object. The code read it as one object, so on every released report `signoffRow.outcome`, `action_note`, `signed_by` and `signed_at` were `undefined`. The screen therefore said "No action needed", with no action note, an unknown signer and no date, **whatever the doctor had recorded**. A report signed off with "Action needed" and a written action showed the opposite on screen. This is the CQC sign-off added on `feat/cqc-audit`, and it was live on `dev`. It is a patient-safety and record-integrity display fault, not an access fault: the data in `lab_report_signoffs` was right throughout, and no one could read more than before. The cause was a hand-edited `lib/database.types.ts`, which typed the embed as one object and so hid the mismatch from `tsc`. **My CQC run (`feat/cqc-audit`) read `getReport` and `lab_report_signoffs` from the code and held the sign-off as working; I did not check the shape of the embed PostgREST returns, and the types I read agreed with the code. That run's "held" statements are about who can read and write the table, which stand, not about what the screen showed.**
- **Fix:** In the working tree: `data.signoff[0] ?? null`, types regenerated with `pnpm db:types` (they now differ in order and in that relationship). Commit it with its own message and tell the clinic that any report viewed on `dev` before the fix showed a wrong sign-off section. Add a test that renders or queries `getReport` for a report with an "action" sign-off and checks the note reaches the page, so a type change cannot hide this again. The rule in `CLAUDE.md` that `lib/database.types.ts` is never edited by hand is what would have prevented it. Check every other embed that reaches a table through a composite key (`grep` for `:table(` selects) for the same shape.

| S-65 | Email set-up is not done or verified, and several settings are what keep it safe. (1) SPF, DKIM and DMARC for the sending domain; without them mail is spoofable and may land in spam, and a reset email is the key to an account. (2) The Resend API key can send as any verified domain unless restricted: use a key limited to sending on this one domain, held only as a server variable (`RESEND_API_KEY`, and as the SMTP password in Supabase), never `NEXT_PUBLIC_`. (3) `SITE_URL` must be set per environment, and the hosted Site URL and redirect allow-list must match the domain, or links point elsewhere. (4) The Auth OTP expiry is one hour (`otp_expiry = 3600`) and the copy must say the same. (5) Reset and email-change links carry one-time tokens in the query string: the Referrer-Policy is `strict-origin-when-cross-origin` (origin only, so the token is not sent on), and analytics cut URLs to the path (S-63); keep both. (6) The password-changed notification is on in `config.toml`; it must also be on in the hosted project. (7) The domain spelling is unconfirmed (CONTEXT finding 32). | `lib/email/`, `supabase/config.toml`, `lib/env.ts` | From the code. No real send has been made. Files can also be renamed inside the folder (`move` worked, though delete is refused), so a photo removed from `profiles.avatar_path` keeps existing, and a person can keep as many files as they like (S-19). The decision is that admins have no clinical access; a clinic's patient list is health-related (being a patient of a hormone and functional medicine clinic is itself private), and the form loads the whole list into the page. Admins already see who is a patient, through `profiles` and the invitations' patient ids (Documented decisions), so this adds names and references in one list, not a new kind of access. | `20261105120000_invitations.sql`, `lib/data/invitations.ts` | Verified. | S-86 | The date of birth is a weak second factor, and the address check has gaps. (1) A date of birth is not a secret: about 36,500 plausible dates, five tries per link, so a blind guess succeeds about once in 7,000 links, but anyone who knows or can look up the patient's date (family, a data breach, a forwarded referral) passes. (2) A PA who creates the record in the same step (`_new_patient`) sets both the date and the address, so no independent fact exists; the mismatch check is skipped there by design. (3) The mismatch check is skipped when `contact_email` is empty, and for guardians. (4) `confirmMismatch` is a form field the sender can tick, so it is a prompt, not a control. (5) A resend makes a fresh row with `failed_attempts = 0`, so a mailbox holder gets five more tries per resend; resends are limited to the sender's 40 an hour, and each lock notifies the sender. (6) A malformed date that matches the pattern, such as `2000-13-45`, errors in the database cast and is not counted. (7) The patient's date of birth is a health-related identifier, and it now travels in a form field to a server action (not logged, not in a URL). | `20261105130000_invitation_identity.sql`, `lib/invitations/accept-actions.ts` | From the code. Ask also for a second fact the clinic holds (the clinic reference on the sender's call, or a postcode); require `contact_email` before a patient invitation; carry `failed_attempts` over on resend, or limit locks per record; reject impossible dates before the call. | OPEN |

The screen always sends the document, which is checked, so this needs a direct call or a future bug. No disclosure, but the queue then says a patient's file was entered for someone else (the S-08 family). | `20261109120000_results_entry_and_release.sql` (`private.enter_report`, the import update) | Fix: when `_import_id` is given, require the import's document to exist and to belong to `_patient` (or the import to be `needs_match` with a null document patient), and refuse a different `_document_id`. Add a pgTAP case. | RESOLVED [2026-11] in `20261109120000_results_entry_and_release.sql`: the import must belong to `_patient` (and to `_document_id` if passed), else 22023. Test: `06_results.test.sql`, 'an import cannot be confirmed against another patient, even without the file'. |
| S-89 | A doctor's comment on a result can be rewritten after release, and the patient sees the new text with no record of the old. If so, add a trigger that refuses a `doctor_comment` change once the report is released, or keeps the old text in the audit metadata. Add `check (char_length(doctor_comment) <= 2000)`. | OPEN |
| S-90 | Entry can leave files nobody can remove, and a retry uploads again. `enterReport` stores the object first, then inserts the `documents` row, then `register_import`, then `enter_report`. (1) If the row insert fails, the object stays: there is no delete policy on Storage and no cleanup, so only the service role can remove it. (2) The storage insert policy lets a PA or doctor write under any uuid (`in_scope` is true for any non-null uuid), and `patientId` is only checked as a uuid before the upload, so a valid-looking unknown patient leaves an object in a folder that belongs to no one (the row then fails on its foreign key). (3) If `register_import` fails, a `documents` row (`kind = lab_report`, `shared_with_patient = true`) exists with no queue entry. (4) If `enter_report` fails, the file and import stay in the queue (as the code says), but the action returns no import id, so the staff member who corrects the form and sends it again uploads a second copy. **Checked from the code, not run.** A file with no report stays hidden from patients (the gate needs a released report), so this is storage, tidiness and the queue, not disclosure. | `lib/results/actions.ts` (`enterReport`) | Fix: check the patient exists before uploading; on a later failure return the new `documentId` and `importId` so the form reuses them; have a server job remove objects with no row (service role, with a system actor). | OPEN |
| S-91 | Upload size and volume. The file limit is 10 MB checked after the body is read; the server-action body limit is now 11 MB for **every** action, including signed-out sign-in and reset (S-73). The `results-entry` throttle is 120 an hour per account and 300 per address, so one account could add about 1.2 GB an hour of lab files; the throttle is spent only after validation passes, so malformed requests are not counted. The bucket limit is 25 MB and allows HEIC, which the server refuses. The server buffers the whole file (`arrayBuffer` and a copy) per request. The magic-byte check reads only the first bytes, so a file that starts as a PDF can hold anything after it (served from the Storage origin, not the app's, with the stored content type). Platform limit not checked: Vercel's request body limit for functions is about 4.5 MB (from the vendor's documentation, not tested here), which would refuse a larger file before the action runs; the profile photos solved the same problem by shrinking in the browser. | `next.config.ts`, `lib/results/actions.ts`, `lib/auth/throttle.ts` | Check the hosting limit before promising 10 MB. Consider a lower per-account upload quota, counting a refused attempt, and a scan or at least a stricter parse of PDFs. Keep `bodySizeLimit` as low as the largest real form. | OPEN |
| S-92 | One person can enter and release their own results. `enter_report` allows a PA or doctor and `release_report` a doctor, both with `in_scope`, which is true for every clinical staff member and includes their own patient record. **Verified:** a seed doctor who is also a patient entered a report for her own record, read it through the staff policy before release, and released it. | S-93 | Conversion factors and one reference range per marker decide what patients are told. (1) `marker_conversions` is written by any admin, who is not clinical staff; a changed factor alters every later conversion (not earlier ones) and is audited. The migration says the factors are the standard ones and the clinic's list will replace them, so none is clinically confirmed. (2) Nothing checks a factor against the marker's unit. (3) A row with no unit is taken to be in the marker's unit, with no warning. (4) The flag is worked out from the marker's single `ref_low`/`ref_high`, with no sex, age or lab-specific range, and `critical` is never set by the database, so a value can read `normal` for the person in front of the doctor. (5) The range is copied onto the result at entry, so a later change to the marker does not alter old results. | `20261109120000_results_entry_and_release.sql` (`marker_conversions`, `private.result_flag`) | Have the clinic confirm the factors and ranges before real results; consider doctors, not admins, owning conversions; record the lab's own range on the result when given; show the original value beside the converted one (the staff screen does). | OPEN |
| S-96 | An exception after a successful release is not caught. In `tellPatientByEmail`, `createAdminClient()` (which throws if `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` or the public URL is missing) and `sendEmail` (a thrown network error rather than a returned one) sit outside the `try`; only `getEmailEnv()` is inside. `releaseReport` has already committed the release, so the doctor would see a server error, not the message the code promises, and a retry gets 23505 "already been released". The release and the portal notice stand, so no data is lost, but the doctor may believe it failed. Also, an error from the `profiles` read (`account.error`) is shown as "Their account is not active", and a failed report read as "no account yet", which hides a fault behind a reason. The patient's email choice has the same two faults: the read of `notification_preferences` ignores its error, so a failed read counts as "on" (fails open towards sending, against a patient who chose off), and when the choice is off the function returns `null`, so the doctor is told "The patient has been told" although no email went (the portal notice did). Fix: wrap the body of `tellPatientByEmail` in one `try`/`catch` returning `failure`, and test `account.error` separately. Return a distinct note for a preference of off, and treat a read error as a failure. | `lib/results/actions.ts` | RESOLVED [2026-11] in the working tree (`lib/results/actions.ts`, not committed). Re-read: the whole of `tellPatientByEmail` is in one `try`/`catch` that returns the failure note and logs a fixed message with no address; the report, `profiles` and `notification_preferences` reads each check `error` and return the failure note, so an unreadable choice is no longer taken as a yes; a choice of off returns its own note ("They have switched results emails off..."), so the doctor is no longer told the patient was emailed. Not run. |
| S-97 | No resend, and two groups never emailed. Release is single use, so if the email fails (or the doctor ignores the message) nothing can send it again; the portal notice is the only fallback. A patient who has no account, and a guardian with `can_act` on a child's record, get no email (guardians are not looked up), so a guardian may not learn a result is ready. Both are arguably intended; the code states the first and is silent on the second. Fix: decide whether guardians are told, and whether an admin or doctor may resend a notice without re-releasing. | `lib/results/actions.ts` | OPEN |
| S-102 | **RESOLVED [2026-11]:** the report page now throws ("Could not write to the activity log.") when `log_record_view` fails, so the report is not shown without a record (fails closed; the owner's decision as relayed by the coordinator). A database error, a deployment where the migration is missing, or a refusal by the function (for example a doctor whose staff profile was deactivated mid-session) leaves a read with no trace, and the message names no record, so it is hard to find in the host logs. For a CQC audit trail the usual expectation is that access without a record does not happen. **Decision for the owner**, which I cannot take: either fail closed (show a refusal page and do not render the report) or keep fail-open and make it visible (an alert on that log line, and a count of reports opened versus rows written). Since `8e6e3a6` nurses and admins are logged with their role. Still not logged: the results lists, `/clinic/patients` (names and dates of birth of every patient for PAs and admins), the portal, and lab PDFs. | OPEN |

What stays: sessions are still not stored or expired when a new one is made, so two live pages for one request are still possible inside 31 minutes (two tabs, or the Pay button during the seconds before the webhook lands), and the refund is manual. **That the failed row is seen by nobody is the one open point: S-143.** Not tested: the second-payment branch of the route (no test calls the route). | S-147 | `service_role` could write a sign-off, and nothing ties a sign-off to a release. As first reviewed (`7cf5ad8`), `20261116120100_cqc_signoff_and_treatment_options.sql` revoked only `update, delete, truncate` from `service_role`; the project's default privileges (`20261102111410_schemas_and_extensions.sql` lines 13-16) revoke from `public`, `anon` and `authenticated` only, so `service_role` keeps `insert` (and `references`, `trigger`). Server code holding the key (a bug, a compromised dependency, a future job) could insert a sign-off with any `outcome`, `action_note`, `signed_by` and `ordered_by`, for a report that is not released or was released by someone else. The permanence trigger then makes the forged row impossible to remove, and it blocks the real release: `release_report` would fail on the primary key with a raw unique-violation message, which `friendly()` passes through (S-94). The audit log records it, but only under a free-text actor or job name (S-62). No code uses the service role on this table today, so the risk is latent; it matters because the table is the CQC evidence that a named doctor signed off. **Fix:** in a new migration, `revoke insert, references, trigger on public.lab_report_signoffs from service_role;` (keep `select`), and add a `before insert` trigger that refuses a row unless `lab_reports` has `id = new.report_id`, `patient_id = new.patient_id`, `released_at is not null` and `reviewed_by = new.signed_by` (it holds for `release_report`, which updates the report first in the same transaction). Add pgTAP checks: `not has_table_privilege('service_role', 'public.lab_report_signoffs', 'insert')`, and an owner-run insert for an unreleased report throws. **Partly fixed [2026-11] in `d309621`:** the migration now revokes `insert, update, delete, truncate, references, trigger` from `service_role`, and test 18 checks `not has_table_privilege('service_role', 'public.lab_report_signoffs', 'insert')`, so only the table owner (through `release_report`) can write one. Still open: no `before insert` trigger ties a row to a released report signed by the same doctor, so a future definer function or migration could still write an unmatched sign-off. | OPEN, partly fixed |
| S-148 | The audit entry for a sign-off names no record. As first reviewed (`7cf5ad8`): `private.write_audit_log` takes `entity_id` from `id`, `user_id`, `staff_id` or `panel_id` (`20261102150000_scope_and_audit_actor.sql` line 177); `lab_report_signoffs` has none of them (its key is `report_id`), so every sign-off is logged as `created` on `lab_report_signoffs` with a null `entity_id`. The actor and time are kept, and the release itself is logged against the `lab_reports` row in the same transaction, but the sign-off row cannot be tied to its report from the audit log alone, and `activity_log` cannot resolve a patient for it. Test 18 (the "each sign-off is in the audit log" check) counts the rows and does not check the id. **Fix:** in a new migration, `create or replace function private.write_audit_log()` with `row_data ->> 'report_id'` added to the `coalesce` (after `id`), or give the table its own `id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid()` with `report_id` unique; extend `activity_log`'s patient lookup to `lab_report_signoffs`; assert in test 18 that `entity_id` equals the report id. **Partly fixed [2026-11] in `d309621`:** the table now has `id uuid not null unique check (id = report_id)`, set by `release_report`, so `write_audit_log` records the report's id, and test 18 checks it. Still open: `activity_log`'s patient lookup (`20261110120100_nurse_gender_and_record_views.sql` line 122) has no branch for `lab_report_signoffs`, so the Activity screen shows these rows with no patient. | OPEN, partly fixed |
| S-153 | The integration code is stored in plain text, can never be removed, and cannot be corrected from the screen. `signaturerx_prescribers.integration_code` is `text`; the Google tokens, by contrast, are AES-256-GCM encrypted (`lib/calendar/crypto.ts`). Held: only `service_role` can read it (no grant for `authenticated`, forced RLS, test checks a doctor is refused), the audit trigger records column names only, and the browser learns only a yes or no (`has_my_signaturerx_code`). But a database dump, a backup, a leaked service key or anyone with dashboard access reads every doctor's code in clear, and with the clinic's client secret a code lets the holder read that doctor's prescriptions at SignatureRx (`GET /prescriptions/{id}`, no PIN). The PIN is what stops a holder creating or signing one. (2) `revoke all ... from service_role` then `grant select` means **nobody can delete or clear a code**, so a leaver's code stays (the leaver job of S-04 and `private.drop_suspended_connections` do not know this table). | S-156 | `prescriber_ip` comes from the first entry of `x-forwarded-for`, which the host may not have written. SignatureRx records this address with the doctor's signature. On Vercel the platform sets the header from the connection and discards a client-sent value, which makes the first entry the client's address; on any other host, or behind another proxy, the first entry is whatever the client sent, so a doctor (or an attacker with the doctor's session) can put any IPv4 address on a signature record. Only IPv4 is accepted, so a doctor on IPv6 only is refused with a message that suggests another connection (an availability point). | S-165 | Settling a stuck send: what the new functions allow and what they leave (twenty-first run, `20261118120000_signaturerx_resolve_send.sql`, `lib/signaturerx/actions.ts` `resolveSignatureRxSend`, `components/prescriptions/signaturerx-panel.tsx` `ResolveSend`) [2026-11]. **Held:** both functions are `security definer`, `set search_path = ''`, lock the row (`for update`), require `status = 'sending'`, and check the caller's own `auth.uid()` through `has_role('admin')` or `staff_may_send_prescription` (a doctor on the patient's care team today, never the patient or a guardian); the public wrappers are `security invoker`, so the caller's session is the identity and the audit trigger names the person; anon and public are revoked; `close` stores 'Closed by hand after checking SignatureRx: ' plus 1 to 200 characters (the column limit is 300, so it fits); `confirm` sets the id, clears the state and checked time, and a second request using the same id gets 23505 (the column is `unique`); the action requires a doctor or admin role first, then the database decides; a PA, nurse, patient and a doctor outside the team are refused (pgTAP). The reason is rendered by React as text, so it cannot inject markup. **Open:** (1) **RESOLVED [2026-11] (follow-up, migration not yet applied on the hosted project): both functions now refuse a send under five minutes old (`sent_at > now() - interval '5 minutes'`, 22023, checked after the role check; pgTAP covers it). As first found: a send could be closed while it was still in flight.** The form appears for any row in `sending`, however new. The first call to SignatureRx may still be running, or its answer not yet written; a second person (or the same one in another tab) who closes it lets the request be sent again, and when the first call finishes, `finish_signaturerx_send` finds no `sending` row and raises, so SignatureRx holds two prescriptions and Clinic Hub shows one failed. (2) **Wording RESOLVED [2026-11] (follow-up): the dialog now says only 'Only do this if you looked in SignatureRx and the prescription is not there.' Still true: close can make a duplicate by a person's error, because nothing checks SignatureRx and the reason is free text.** Small leftover: the panel still shows the form for a send under five minutes old, and the action maps the refusal (22023) to 'no longer waiting for a check, or the box is empty', which is misleading; show the form only after five minutes or give the age refusal its own message. (3) **An admin can settle a send.** The migration header and tests allow it. An admin cannot send or sign (the doctor-only rule holds), but closing re-opens the request to a second send and confirming attaches an id that is then shown as the patient's prescription, which is a write to a clinical workflow record by a role that is meant to write no clinical data. It is an operational decision, not a clinical one, and the person is audited, but this is for the owner to accept or remove. (4) **A recorded id is not verified.** `confirm` accepts any unused id of up to 100 characters, including one that belongs to another patient's prescription at SignatureRx; the next status check then shows that prescription's state against this request, and nothing compares the patient. It cannot hide a real prescription for *this* request in any harmful way (the request stays visibly linked and the id is unique), but a wrong id will show a wrong state. A typo is caught only when the next check fails. Mitigation: the check could compare the patient's reference in SignatureRx's answer. (5) **Audit.** Both calls are audited with the person and the changed column names (`status`, `last_error`; `srx_prescription_id`, `status`, `detailed_status`, `last_checked_at`), but not the values, so the audit says 'closed by X' only through the row's own `last_error` text, and a confirmation shows the id only in the row. The audit trigger gives the actor from the session; I read that, I did not run it. (6) **The reason text is stored in `last_error` and shown to anyone who can read the row** (the care team and PAs); it may hold clinical detail typed by the person. The field is limited to 200 characters but not warned. (7) The resolve action uses the same throttle bucket as the signing PIN (S-154), so repeated attempts to settle can use up a doctor's PIN attempts, and the limiter fails open. | `20261118120000_signaturerx_resolve_send.sql`; `lib/signaturerx/actions.ts` lines 231 to 259; `components/prescriptions/signaturerx-panel.tsx` lines 61 to 104 and 220 | As in each open point: owner decision on admins, compare the patient on the next check, warn that the reason is stored and read by staff, and use a separate throttle key. | OPEN |
| S-166 | A PA can now change a patient's date of birth, and the invitation check reads it (twenty-first run) [2026-11]. **Held:** the change goes through the PA's session under the existing column grant (`date_of_birth` in the update grant since `20261102111731`) and policy; it is audited as 'the column `date_of_birth` changed', by the PA, with no old or new value; Zod needs `YYYY-MM-DD`, a real date, not in the future and 1900 or later, and the database column is `not null`. **Why it matters:** `private.accept_invitation` and `verify_invitation_identity` compare the entered date with the **current** date of birth on the record, and accepting also needs the account email to equal the invitation's address. So whoever sets the date decides which date unlocks a pending invitation. A PA could already invite any email address for any patient, so this does not widen what a PA can do; it does make the date, which patients are meant to know and others not, something staff can set after an invitation is sent. **Open:** (1) nothing warns, or asks for a reason, when a date of birth changes while an invitation is pending, and the pending invitation is not revoked; a correction made after the invitation was sent silently locks the patient out until they ask the clinic (the identity check counts failed tries); (2) the audit keeps no before and after value, so a wrong or malicious change cannot be undone from the log (S-62 and S-37 are the general limit of the audit); (3) the form always sends the date, so every save rewrites the column (harmless, the trigger lists only columns that differ); (4) a date of birth drives anything age-based (SignatureRx, reference ranges), and a wrong one is a patient-safety matter, not only an identity one; (5) there is no rule that stops a change after the patient has signed in, other than the PA role. | `lib/patients/actions.ts` lines 92 to 98 and 139 to 141; `components/patients/contact-form.tsx` line 36; `20261114120000_finalise_hardening.sql` (`accept_invitation`) | Decide whether a changed date of birth needs a reason or a second person. At least: store the old value in the audit `meta` for this column, warn on the screen when an invitation is pending and offer to cancel and resend it, and tell the PA the patient's identity check will use the new date. | OPEN |

The URL is a bearer link: an anonymous request to a signed avatar URL returned 200, and it keeps working for its hour after a photo is replaced or removed (the file stays, see S-70) or after the person is suspended. It sits in the page HTML and the Next.js payload; `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin` and `no-store` on the page limit how far it travels, and `img-src` allows the Supabase origin only. A signature URL is made only for its owner. Shorten the hour (the top-bar image reloads on each page anyway) when documents are built. | OPEN |
| S-44 | `requestPasswordReset` builds `redirectTo` from the request's `Origin` header.
