Whether that works in production depends on the host overwriting the header. The damage is limited: Auth checks the allow-list, and `recovery.html` uses `{{ .SiteURL }}`. Build the URL from a configured site URL. See the checklist. **The invitation link is built from `SITE_URL`** (`lib/invitations/actions.ts`, from `getEmailEnv`), never from a header, and the `/invite` redirect kept the request's own host with a forged `Host` and `X-Forwarded-Host` (verified); this one `requestPasswordReset` is still open. | OPEN |
| S-55 | `auth_throttle` keys are unsalted SHA-256, so the table holds pseudonymised data. The comments (migration and `throttle.ts`) now say the table holds "no plain email or IP address", which is true [2026-11], so the misleading claim is gone; the weakness is not. An IPv4 address or a known email can be recovered by trying candidates. The rows are short-lived and only the definer function can read them. Use an HMAC with a server secret. | OPEN, partly fixed |
| S-56 | `.env.local` and the `web` launch configuration point at the one hosted project, so plain `pnpm dev` runs unreviewed local code against it, and could reach real data when it holds any. `web-local` is the safe route. Use the local stack by default, and keep real data out of any project a development machine holds keys for. Seen again in the fifth run: `pnpm build` and `next start` also load `.env.local` on their own, so a local production build in the repository takes whatever that file holds for any variable not set in the shell. A review or test build should be made in a copy of the tree without `.env*` files. See the checklist. | OPEN |
| S-59 | `components/shell/session-guard.tsx` (a Client Component) imports `SESSION_LIMITS` from `lib/supabase/session-cookies.ts`, which imports `getSessionEnv` from `lib/env.ts`. The browser bundle therefore holds the HMAC signing code and the whole env schema: the names `SESSION_COOKIE_SECRET`, `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` and `RESEND_API_KEY`, no values (0 matches for the secret, the service key or any key pattern). The functions throw in a browser, so nothing works there, but a secret-reading module that a Client Component can import has no `server-only` guard, and a later edit could make it matter. Fix: move `SESSION_LIMITS` and `SessionKind` to a file with no env import, and put `import "server-only"` on the signing code. | OPEN |
| S-61 | The idle timer is a browser timer. It counts only while the page is awake, so a device that sleeps may show the old screen after waking until the remaining time passes, a hidden tab may run it late, and a browser with script switched off never runs it. The screen can also outlast the server's limit by up to about 4 minutes, because activity pings the server at most every 5 minutes. Not tested here: a real 30-minute wait, and sleep. Consider checking on `visibilitychange` and on a wall-clock comparison. The proxy remains the boundary. | OPEN |
| S-63 | Two third-party scripts now load on every page of a clinical portal: Vercel Web Analytics and Speed Insights (`components/vercel-insights.tsx`, in `app/layout.tsx`, so they also run on `/portal` and `/clinic`). Each sends the page URL, referrer, browser, operating system, device type, country and performance timings to Vercel. Both are cookie-free, and both `beforeSend` hooks cut the URL to origin plus path, dropping the query string and fragment, so an emailed link's `token_hash` and `?location=` do not leave the browser. The scripts come from the app's own origin (`/_vercel/...`), so the CSP is unchanged and nothing is loaded from a third-party host; they run only on Vercel. Paths such as `/portal` and `/clinic` hold no patient identifier today. Risks that remain: a later route with an identifier in its path (for example `/clinic/patients/<id>`) would be sent as it stands, so route design must keep identifiers out of paths or the hook must mask them; Vercel is already the host and so already a processor, but its data processing terms and the analytics settings (retention, IP handling) have not been confirmed by the owner; UK PECR and the clinic's privacy notice should cover measurement of visitors to a health service; the stripping is read from the code and not yet seen in a request. The packages are exact new dependencies (2.0.1 and 2.0.0), reviewed only by version. **Invitation link [2026-11]:** `/invite?token=` answers with a redirect and no page, so no beacon runs on it, and `/invite/accept` has no token in its path or query; `stripUrl` would remove a query anyway. The token does reach the host's request logs (S-82). | OPEN |
| S-66 | No bounce, complaint or delivery handling for Resend (no webhook route). A bounced invitation or reset is invisible, and a complaint can harm the domain's sending reputation. Not built; `webhook_events` exists in the schema but nothing receives Resend events. When it is built, verify the webhook signature and keep addresses out of logs. | OPEN |
| S-67 | `pnpm email:test <template> <to>` sends a real email through Resend using `.env.local` (with the key). It runs only when a person runs it, takes the recipient as an argument and sends fixed sample content with no clinical values, but a mistyped address sends a message from the clinic's domain to a stranger. Use the owner's own address; never run it in CI. Also: the email templates add no free text from users, and `escapeHtml` is applied to names, titles and links in `layout.ts` and `app.ts` (read, not attacked). The detail rows helper inserts `row.value` unescaped, so any caller must escape it first. | OPEN |
| S-73 | Body size and errors on uploads. The 3 MB action body limit (`next.config.ts`) applies to **every** server action, including sign-in and reset from a signed-out visitor (it was 1 MB), so a stranger can make the server read 3 MB per request; the throttle does not run until the body is read. A 2.5 MB or 2.9 MB image reaches the action and gets "Use an image of 2 MB or less"; a 3.3 MB one is stopped by Next.js with a 500 "Internal Server Error" and `Body exceeded 3mb limit` in the log, with no friendly message (a hydrated page may show its error boundary). No image reached storage in any case. A valid PNG with an HTML tail was accepted, as expected: the check is on the first bytes and the type, not a full decode, and the file is only ever served as an image from the Supabase origin under the CSP. | `next.config.ts`, `lib/profile/actions.ts` | Verified on the production build. **Partly fixed [2026-11, uncommitted]:** the file field now shrinks a large image in the browser (`components/profile/image-input.tsx`, `lib/profile/shrink-image.ts`: up to 6 attempts at a smaller size and quality, a JPEG on a white ground; types other than JPEG, PNG and WebP, including HEIC, are refused with a message and the field is cleared), so a phone photo no longer reaches the limit. This is a convenience, not a control: a person with JavaScript off, or one who posts the form another way, still gets Next.js's plain 500 over 3 MB, and a stranger can still make the server read 3 MB per request. The server checks are unchanged. Not verified: Safari, Firefox, memory on a very large image. A signature PNG with transparency becomes a JPEG on white when large. Consider a smaller body limit with a custom route for uploads, or accept. **Update [2026-11]:** the limit is now 11 MB for every action, for lab reports (S-91). | OPEN (reduced) |
| S-82 | The token travels once in a query string, and one header intended for it does not apply. `GET /invite?token=…` is a request path with the token, so it reaches the host's request logs (Vercel keeps them for a while and the team can read them), any mail gateway that follows links, and, if click tracking is on, Resend's link rewrite and its stored copy of the message. The route sets `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer`, but the global header in `next.config.ts` replaces it: the response carries only `strict-origin-when-cross-origin` (verified). No leak follows today, because the redirect target has no token and the page loads nothing cross-origin, but the intent is not met. The token is not in the HTML, the accept page, the app log (checked with the real token) or the analytics path (S-63). Cookie flags verified on a production build: `HttpOnly`, `Secure`, `SameSite=Lax`, `Path=/invite`, `Max-Age=3600`; only exactly 43 characters of `[A-Za-z0-9_-]` are accepted, anything else sets no cookie. | OPEN |
| S-84 | The audit log names the inviter for the acceptance, not the person who accepted. All writes inside `accept_invitation` (profile activated, role granted, record linked, guardian link, `accepted_at`) are under the inviter, with the inviter's role, because the grant is theirs (verified). The accepting account is visible only through the entity ids. The new-patient insert is under the PA. "Viewed" is still never logged (S-07). Say so in the audit reader when one is built. | OPEN |
| S-85 | The seed's two demo invitations (`claire.benson@example.test`, pending; `new.doctor@example.test`, expired) show on the Team screen and have hashes that no 43-character token can produce (they are not hex), so neither link can be used (verified: the accept page says "not valid" for any token). They can be resent: with email set up, that would send a real message with a working link to an `example.test` address. The seed must never run on the hosted project (checklist). | OPEN |
| S-87 | The lock can be used to cancel someone's invitation, and its audit trail is thin. (1) Anyone holding a link, or a stranger who guesses nothing but posts five wrong dates, cancels it (`revoked_at` set, the sender sees an in-portal notice and the patient sees "no longer valid"). The link is a 43-character secret, so this needs the link itself; it is a nuisance, not a way in, and the fix is a resend. (2) `verify_invitation_identity` runs as `service_role` with `app.system_actor = 'invitation-identity-check'`, so each wrong date and the lock are in `audit_log` under that job name with no person or IP. The check is a read for a valid date and writes only on a wrong one. (3) The lock notice includes the invitee's address, to the sender only. (4) `verify_invitation_identity` answers `ok`, `wrong`, `locked` or `invalid` only to the service role (grants verified: `anon` and `authenticated` false), so the Data API cannot be used as a guessing oracle; the app's answer to a link holder is the same for patient and guardian. | `20261105130000_invitation_identity.sql` | From the code and the live grants. Accept, or record the client address in the notice. | OPEN |
| S-94 | Error text and an existence oracle. `friendly()` in `lib/results/actions.ts` shows the database message for any error with code 42501, 22023 or 23505 from the RPC. Those are the clinic's own sentences when raised by the functions, but a unique violation or a privilege error from elsewhere would show a constraint or function name. `register_import` answers "That file was not found." for an unknown document id and "You cannot send this file in for entry." for one that exists but is not the caller's (it reads `documents` with definer rights), so a signed-in person who guesses a random uuid learns whether it exists; 122 random bits make that impractical. | OPEN |
| S-95 | What the results screens cannot do. (1) There is no way to correct or withdraw a released or unreleased report: values, units, flags and ranges are locked (correct for integrity), and `lab_reports` has no update grant, so a wrong entry stays unless server code fixes it. (2) A file with no patient (`needs_match`) cannot be entered: `enter_report` needs the document filed under the same patient, the code comment says to move it first, and nothing moves it. (3) `register_import` and `enter_report` accept a document of any kind belonging to the patient (a letter, for example), which then stays hidden from the patient until that report is released. (4) Guardians are not shown a patient's results on `/portal/results` (the query uses the signed-in person's own record), so nothing wrong is shown; the gap is a feature. (5) A report page needs only a signed-in staff role to open; RLS decides what loads (the health coach sees released reports only; admin sees none). | OPEN |
| S-98 | The result-ready email: what it discloses and what was not checked. (1) It tells whoever reads that inbox that this person has an account with a hormone and longevity clinic and has a new result, with their first name; no clinical content. That is the intended trade-off and matches the template's own line, but it is a decision for the clinic (a shared or work inbox is the risk). (2) Only `status = 'suspended'` is skipped: an `invited` account (address not yet confirmed by acceptance) would also be emailed; the account is created at acceptance, so this is unlikely. (3) The email goes to `profiles.email`, which tracks the confirmed sign-in address (S-23), not the record's `contact_email`: correct. (4) A throttle is not applied, which is acceptable because a report can be released once. (5) The service-role read of `profiles.email` is not written to the audit log ("viewed" is never audited, S-62). (6) The log lines carry Resend's error text; Resend's messages were not seen for a bad address, so check once that none echoes the `to` address (log only a fixed message if one does). (7) The link and the template were not rendered or sent. (8) The patient's choice: `notification_preferences` (topic `results`, channel `email`) is read with the service role, by `user_id` taken from the report, and `enabled = false` stops the email; no row counts as on. The table is the patient's own (RLS: read, insert and `update (enabled)` for the owner only), so a patient can switch off but nobody else can change it for them, and the service-role read is narrow (one row, one user). Only an explicit `false` stops it (see S-96 for the ignored error). A second `createAdminClient()` is made for this read, which is harmless. Not tested: the screen that sets the choice, if any, and that the topic and channel values match what it writes. | `lib/results/actions.ts`, `lib/email/templates/app.ts` | OPEN |

The text and the page do not warn that the steps write real, permanent data. | S-105 | Other points in the twelfth run. (1) **Nurse:** `private.is_clinical_staff` is unchanged, so a nurse has a staff profile and the `nurse` role but reads no patient data; the `/clinic` layout and `isStaff` now admit a nurse, so a nurse reaches the shell and own profile; what else a nurse can read through the non-patient policies (for example the staff directory) was not tested. (2) **`patient_profiles.gender_identity`:** column grants for `insert` and `update` to `authenticated`, with at most 60 characters; the row policies still decide, and only PAs insert or change patient profiles (verified: a patient's update touched no row; a 61-character value was refused). It is special-category data in free text; the table's `select` is table-level, so whoever reads the row (patient, guardian, clinical staff in scope) reads it. Nothing in the app reads or writes it yet. Its changes are audited by column name only. (3) **Emails:** `lib/email/contact.ts` holds the clinic's phone, email and WhatsApp numbers and `layout.ts` prints them in every email, escaping the label and the link target; there is no secret. The numbers are written in code, so the owner must confirm they are correct and that the WhatsApp link opens the clinic's number, because a wrong number in a sign-in email is a phishing aid. `supabase/templates/*.html` were regenerated, so the hosted Auth templates must be pasted again by hand (checklist). **Update [2026-11, fourteenth run]:** point (1) is superseded. The clinic decided that a nurse works like a doctor on the patients of their care team; `is_clinical_staff`, `in_scope`, the document, import and allergy policies, `enter_report`, `register_import` and the record-view role were changed together in `20261112120100_access_by_care_team.sql`, and nurses write notes. | OPEN |
| S-111 | Other points in the thirteenth run (staff Google Calendar), all from the code [2026-11]. (1) `decryptToken` (`lib/calendar/crypto.ts` lines 30-32) does not pin the tag length; Node accepts tags shorter than 16 bytes unless `authTagLength: 16` is passed to `createDecipheriv` (and Node 22 warns about it). Only the service role can write the column, so this is hardening. (2) The `v1` prefix names a format, not a key, so the key cannot be rotated without disconnecting everyone (the docs say so). (3) A decryption failure or a 401 from Google, met during a page view, deletes the connection (a `GET` with a side effect); a wrong `GOOGLE_TOKEN_ENCRYPTION_KEY` in production would silently disconnect every person who opens the page. (4) The event link (`app/clinic/calendar/page.tsx` line 245) uses Google's `htmlLink` without checking that it starts with `https://www.google.com/`; Google sets it and React refuses `javascript:` links, so this is hardening. (5) Calls to Google have no timeout. (6) `google_calendar_connections` is not in `docs/reference/schema-draft.md`, so there is no agreed read and write rule to check it against; it was checked against the design given for this review. `CLAUDE.md` still says GoHighLevel keeps the calendar (the PRD explains patient booking stays there). (7) `lib/database.types.ts` was edited by hand; it gives the token column to the signed-in client's types, which the grants refuse; regenerate with `pnpm db:types`. (8) The redirects in both routes are built from `request.url` (the host), like the existing routes; the path is fixed, so only the host header could change them (see the checklist item on `X-Forwarded-Host`). (9) `save_google_calendar_connection` checks `is_active_staff` but not `profiles.status`; the callback checks suspension before calling it. (10) `include_granted_scopes=true` and the `calendar.events` scope give read and write access to events on every calendar the person has, though the app uses `primary` only. | OPEN |
| S-118 | Other points in the fourteenth run (clinic decisions of 11 November) [2026-11]. (2) Stale text says a health coach sees released results only: the comment on `ResultsPage` (`app/clinic/results/page.tsx` line 57), the "Results review" row of the knowledge base and the policy names on `results` and `lab_reports`. Coaches and nurses now read unreleased results of their care-team patients, which matches the clinic's answer to question 3 ("everything except safeguarding alerts"). (3) A doctor on a patient's team can end any colleague's row, including the primary doctor's; that matches "doctors keep the care team up to date" and is audited. The `relationship` is not checked against the person's role (a health coach can be added as `primary_doctor`, which also takes the one primary-doctor slot); it grants nothing extra. (4) A doctor's "new patient" invitation creates a `patient_profiles` row with no PA in the loop and no duplicate check, and skips the address check (there is no address on record yet); the date-of-birth proof at acceptance still applies. A doctor keeps reading the patient invitations they sent (address and `patient_id`) after leaving that care team. (6) `signNote` (since `8e6e3a6`) saves the text and then signs it in two requests, both through the author's session; only the author can change the draft in between, so what is signed is what was saved. (7) The lock trigger cannot be dropped or disabled by `authenticated` or `service_role` (owner only); the database owner, and anyone allowed to set `session_replication_role`, can still bypass triggers, as for every other lock (S-28). (8) A patient sees the ids of courses assigned to them even while a course is unpublished (`education_assignments`), not its title or content. (9) Admins and PAs can assign courses to any patient and every in-scope staff member can take one away, as the access table says. (10) The uncommitted `private.london_today()` is `SECURITY INVOKER`, not a definer, with `search_path = ''`; it is called inside `in_scope`, so care-team days now start at London midnight. | OPEN |
| S-129 | Other points in the fifteenth run (`feat/clinic-build-2`) [2026-11], from the code. (1) **Resolved [2026-11] in `29f602f`:** the lock now says a signed note is final. Earlier: the lock trigger on `clinical_notes` (`20261112120300_clinical_notes.sql` line 93) still answered "A signed note cannot be changed. Write a correction instead.", which `friendly()` shows to staff, though corrections no longer exist. (2) A draft note made from a recording is written in the recorder's name; if the recorder leaves the care team before signing, nobody can sign or archive it. (4) The webhook: constant-time comparison of a secret of at least 32 characters, checked before the body is read; the body supplies only a job id, and the transcript is then fetched from AssemblyAI with the server's key, so a forged or replayed call can at most trigger an early check; a second delivery returns the existing note. The secret also travels to AssemblyAI in each job request, as their design requires. (6) `get_staff_directory` gives patients no photo path any more, and the avatars policy lets staff see only staff photos and the photos of patients in scope (read, not attacked). (7) `notifyPatient` treats only `suspended` accounts as off; an archived patient record is still messaged. (1) **Held:** `question_replies` and both functions are as described in the executive summary; `from_clinic` is set from `in_scope` inside the definer, and the body is trimmed and limited to 4,000 characters there as well as in the action. (2) A staff member who is also a guardian with `can_act` but not `can_view` is not excluded by `in_scope` (S-112's lesser case), so `reply_to_question` treats them as staff. (3) Admins and, on their care team, health coaches read and answer questions and close them, as the clinic's access decision (admins read every record) allows; the knowledge base table still lists Ask the clinic under GoHighLevel and "Planned", and the `questions` table comment says replies happen in GoHighLevel. (4) The portal names a patient-side reply after the patient even when a guardian wrote it; the audit log and `author_id` keep the real author. (6) `uploadDocument` writes the Storage object before the row; if the row is refused, the object stays in the patient's folder with no row, so nobody can read it, and nothing clears it. (7) `setDocumentShared` sends a new message on every share and has no throttle. (8) `QUESTIONS_ALERT_TO` and `TEAM_INVITE_ALERT_TO` are read from `process.env` without validation in `lib/env.ts`; neither is a secret, but a wrong value sends the content-free alert to a stranger. (9) The document link is a 60-second bearer URL from Storage; whoever has it in that minute can fetch the file. (10) `notifyPatient` still messages an archived patient (S-129 item 7) and tells only the patient's own account, never a guardian. (11) `29f602f` edited migrations already in the history (`20261113120000`, `20261113120300`, `20261113120500`); a database that already applied them will not see the edits, so confirm no environment has applied them before pushing (checklist). (12) `ConfirmButton` (`4fcb94d`) only replaces `window.confirm`; every action it guards still checks on the server. | OPEN |
| S-140 | Other points in the seventeenth run (`feat/small-items`) [2026-11], from the code. (1) **Reconnection after a mistaken switch-off.** If a team member is switched off and on again and reconnects Google before the next 03:00 UTC run, the queued old token is still revoked; Google's revoke ends the app's grant for that Google account, so the new connection will most likely stop working too. Availability only; the person reconnects. Consider dropping a queued row when the same `user_id` saves a new connection. (2) **Suspension now queues too** (`d777b66`, `private.drop_suspended_connections`, an `after update of status` trigger on `profiles`: definer, `search_path = ''`, execute revoked from `public`, `anon` and `authenticated`, fires only on a change to `suspended` and only when a connection exists; `15_finalise` checks it). Removing a person's staff roles without switching off the staff profile or suspending still leaves the connection. (2a) **The job name outlives the trigger.** `set_config('app.system_actor', 'offboarding', true)` lasts for the rest of the transaction, and both triggers (`profiles_drop_suspended_connections`, `staff_profiles_drop_leaver_connections`) sort before the table's `*_write_audit_log` trigger, so the audit row for the suspension or switch-off itself, and any later write in the same transaction, is marked `offboarding`; a `service_role` write that named no actor is then let through the S-07 guard instead of refused. Today nothing in the app suspends or switches off through the service role (admins do it through their own session, where `auth.uid()` is the actor), so it is latent. Fix: set the job name back to its earlier value at the end of each trigger, or set it only around the delete. (3) **The `portal_patient` cookie is not cleared on sign-out** (`signOut` in `lib/auth/actions.ts` deletes only `ch_last_seen`). On a shared device the next person's portal ignores a value that is not in their own list, so nothing is shown, but a patient UUID stays in the browser for up to 30 days. Clear it in `signOut`. (4) **Archived allergies:** since `d777b66` the read policy hides them from the patient and guardians; staff in scope still read them, and only `getClinicalSummary` filters them, so any later staff reader (including a prescribing check) must filter `archived_at`. (5) **Grants are otherwise minimal:** `google_revocations` revokes everything from `public`, `anon`, `authenticated` and `service_role` first, then gives `service_role` `select` and a three-column `update`; no insert or delete for anyone (the trigger inserts as the definer). `create or replace` keeps the earlier `revoke all on function private.drop_leaver_connections()`, and the function still sets `search_path = ''`; it needs no `auth.uid()` check because only the trigger on `staff_profiles` runs it, after RLS has allowed that update. (6) **Batch size and time:** 50 rows a run, one Google call each with a timeout; enough for a clinic, but a long run could exceed the function's time limit, leaving later rows for the next day. (7) `CRON_SECRET` is read from `process.env` directly, not through `lib/env.ts`; acceptable because the route fails closed. | `lib/calendar/leavers.ts`, `lib/auth/actions.ts`, `lib/data/patient-record.ts`, `20261115120000_allergies_and_leaver_revocation.sql` | From the code [2026-11]. Items 1, 2a and 3 are worth small fixes; 4 is a rule for future code. | OPEN |
| S-146 | Other points in the eighteenth run (`feat/repeat-prescriptions`) [2026-11], from the code. (1) **CSP `form-action 'self'` and the redirect to Stripe.** The CSP was not changed and need not be for the usual path: with JavaScript loaded, a server action's redirect comes back as a header and the browser navigates, which `form-action` does not govern. Before hydration, or with JavaScript off, the "Pay" form posts natively and the answer is a 303 to `checkout.stripe.com`, which Chromium refuses under `form-action 'self'` (it checks redirects after a form post). **Re-read against `c94f9bb` [2026-11]:** `lib/csp.ts` line 32 still reads `form-action 'self'` and the commit did not touch it, so the point stands. Still untested in a real browser: the native post (before hydration, or JavaScript off) answered by a 303 to `checkout.stripe.com`, and in which browsers it is refused. If that case matters, add `https://checkout.stripe.com` to `form-action` only, never to `script-src` or `connect-src`. (2) **What Stripe learns.** The product name is "Repeat prescription request" and the hosted page collects the payer's email and card name, so Stripe holds the fact that a named person paid a hormone clinic for a repeat prescription. No medicine, answer or patient name is sent. Stripe's own customer receipts would repeat the product name. A DPA and the receipt setting are on the checklist. (3) **Staff can withdraw an unpaid request** (`cancel_repeat_request` allows a doctor or PA in scope; admins were removed in `c94f9bb`, and a pgTAP case now shows an admin cannot decide), and **staff can approve an unpaid request** (`decide_repeat_request` does not look at `paid_at`). Both are needed while Stripe is off, but the knowledge base access table lists only "change status" for staff. Record it there, or make approval need `paid_at` once Stripe is on. (4) **Resolved in `c94f9bb`:** `payRepeat` now passes the row's `fee_pence` to the Checkout Session. (`requestRepeat` uses the constant for a brand-new request, which is correct.) (5) **`private.is_weight_loss_name` is `SECURITY DEFINER` and `EXECUTE` for `authenticated`** with no caller check. It returns only whether a name matches the clinic's list, which every signed-in person can read anyway. It could be `SECURITY INVOKER`. Matching by the first word can be avoided by misspelling (a clinical point, not access). (6) **Bell notices on payment** (`lib/prescriptions/notify.ts` lines 28 to 38; unchanged by the fix) go to every admin and PA whose account is not suspended, including one whose staff profile is switched off, and to doctors on the care team without checking their account. The notice names no patient and the link needs an active role to open. (7) **An archived medicine on the clinic's list** drops out of a patient's own request view (the embed returns null and the page shows "A medicine from the clinic's list"). It is not a leak, but the patient loses sight of what they asked for. (8) **Held:** an empty `payment_intent` is stored as `''` rather than null. `stripe_checkout_session_id` is unique. Every message is generic, and the decline reason stays in the portal. The cron route copies the hardened pattern of `revoke-google`. | OPEN |
| S-149 | Other points in the CQC run (`feat/cqc-audit`) [2026-11], from the code. (1) **Who reads the action note.** The read policy is `in_scope(patient_id)`, the staff branch of the `lab_reports` read policy, so PAs and admins read every sign-off and care-team doctors, nurses and health coaches read their patients'. The action note is clinical free text written for the team; this matches the clinic's access table (admins read every clinical record) and the migration's "for the team only". Recorded under Documented decisions, not as a gap. (2) **Older releases have no sign-off.** Reports released before `20261116120100` have no row, and the report screen says "Released before sign-offs were recorded"; the rule "every release is signed off" holds only from the migration onwards. Whether the clinic wants those signed off after the event is a CQC question, not a security one. (3) **Truncate guard, committed in `d309621` [2026-11].** The permanence trigger is row-level, so in `7cf5ad8` a `TRUNCATE` by the table owner bypassed it (`service_role` has no `truncate`). `d309621` adds `lab_report_signoffs_no_truncate`, a `before truncate` statement trigger with the same error, and a test 18 check ("or emptied"); held on reading. The same gap remains on `audit_log` (S-28). (4) **Option columns.** `clinical_notes` gains `insert` and `update` grants on the five columns for `authenticated`, under the existing policies (author, draft, in scope), and the lock trigger (`20261113120000_notes_final_and_unmatched_files.sql`) refuses every update of a signed or archived note, whichever column; test 18 shows both a no-op under RLS and a refusal for the owner. Nothing requires a box to be ticked before signing; that is a product choice. `options_offered` is limited to 5,000 characters in the database and the action. (5) **Tests.** No case for a guardian, nurse, health coach or PA reading sign-offs, a patient embedding them under `lab_reports`, a health coach or admin calling `release_report`, or `anon` on the table; all hold on reading (see the CQC section below). | OPEN |
| S-161 | Other points in the nineteenth run (`signaturerx`) [2026-11], from the code. (1) **Default off holds.** `isSignatureRxSigningEnabled` is true only when `SIGNATURERX_SIGNING_ENABLED === "true"` and all three credentials parse; the variables are commented out in `.env.example`; `sendToSignatureRx` returns "switched off" before reading the form; the panel hides the form and shows a line. `checkSignatureRxStatus` does not need the switch, only the credentials, so a prescription already sent can still be checked when signing is off, which is intended. (2) **SSRF holds.** `SIGNATURERX_BASE_URL` is a two-value enum; the path is fixed and the prescription id is `encodeURIComponent`-encoded and comes from our own table; a redirect is the only way off the origin (S-155 item 1). (3) **Service role only after the checks it needs, with one gap.** In `sendToSignatureRx` the service role reads the doctor's own code (`user_id = user.id` from the session) and writes through the record function after the role, switch, form and request checks; in `checkSignatureRxStatus` it reads the sender's code after the row was read through the caller's session. The scope gap was S-150 (resolved in the twentieth run). `createAdminClient` is not reachable from the Client Component (`signaturerx-panel.tsx` imports only the action, types and constants; `lib/signaturerx/client.ts` and `lib/data/signaturerx.ts` start with `server-only`). (4) **Clinic Hub keeps no prescription content.** The table holds the SignatureRx id, action, state, sender and times; the medicines, quantities and directions typed by the doctor, and what was approved in the request, are not stored here, so the clinic's own record of what was prescribed is SignatureRx's. That is the stated design ("no medicine, quantity, direction or PIN is kept here") but it makes S-152 and a loss of access to SignatureRx matter more, and the medicines sent are free text typed by the doctor, not tied to the approved request lines, so a doctor in scope may send something that was not approved. (5) **Address columns.** `patient_profiles` has table-wide `select` for `authenticated` (`20261102111731` line 56), so the new columns are readable by everyone the existing row policy lets read a patient's row, including the patient, a guardian and care-team nurses and coaches; the column comment ("only the clinical team reads") is wrong, and the address is not a secret from its owner. Writes are by PAs only (column grants plus the existing update policy; test checks a patient's attempt changes nothing). [2026-11 twentieth run: a PA now enters it on the clinic patient record, see S-163.] (6) **A PA's status check uses the doctor's code** (see S-157). (7) **No `delete` or `update` for anyone on either table:** `revoke all` from `service_role` too, then only `select` (and a policy for `authenticated` on the prescriptions table); writes only through the definer functions, which are `EXECUTE` for `service_role` (record, refresh) or `authenticated` (set and has code) and revoked from `anon` and `PUBLIC`. All four set `search_path = ''`; the two for doctors check `auth.uid()` and `has_role('doctor')`; the two for the server check the request and the sender's roles. (8) **Gender defaults to "Other"** when the stored sex is not male or female, and an empty `status` is stored as `''` (the screen treats both). (9) **No `CASCADE`** in the migration; foreign keys are `on delete restrict`; the audit trigger is on both tables; `00_schema_checks` is updated and passes. | OPEN |
| S-164 | Other points in the twentieth run [2026-11], from the code. (1) **New service-only functions.** `start_signaturerx_send`, `finish_signaturerx_send`, `fail_signaturerx_send`, `refresh_signaturerx_prescription` and `private.staff_may_send_prescription` are `EXECUTE` for `service_role` only (revoked from `public`, `anon`, `authenticated`; pgTAP checks the first and the last), all `search_path = ''`. They take the sender (`_by`) as an argument and trust it, because server code has no session: that is safe only while nothing but the action calls them, and `service_role` is the app's own key. `start` checks the sender itself; `finish` and `fail` check only that the row is `sending`, so any holder of the service key can close any open send with any SignatureRx id (accepted by design for a service key; note it). (2) **The scope function mirrors `in_scope`.** `staff_may_send_prescription` re-implements the care-team test (`care_team` join `staff_profiles`, `since <= london_today()`, `until` null or later, `private.is_active_staff`) plus the self and guardian exclusion. If `in_scope` changes (for example a covering-doctor rule or care-team dates), this copy will not follow, in either direction. Add a pgTAP check that compares them for a sample of doctors and patients, and a comment in `in_scope`. It is stricter than `in_scope` on purpose (only doctors, never self). (3) **A typed integration code is saved after `finish`**, so if the save fails the doctor is asked again next time; the error is ignored. (4) **A missing sex now stops the send** (`lib/data/signaturerx.ts` line 88) instead of "Other"; `toSrxGender` still maps any non-male, non-female value to "Other", so a value such as "intersex" is sent as "Other" without a prompt. (5) **`fail_signaturerx_send` stores SignatureRx's refusal text** (S-155 item 2). (6) The status check still uses the sender's code for a PA (S-157 item 3) and still is not audited by person. (7) A doctor can still send medicines typed freehand that were not approved (S-161 item 4). | `20261117120000_signaturerx.sql`; `lib/signaturerx/actions.ts`; `lib/data/signaturerx.ts` | As in each point. | OPEN |
| S-167 | `contact_email` and the account's sign-in email are two things, and the PA form edits only the first (twenty-first run) [2026-11]. Read from the code: `contact_email` is a column on `patient_profiles` under the existing grant; the sign-in email lives in `auth.users` and `profiles.email` and no screen here changes it. (1) `getPatientForSignatureRx` uses `contact_email` **before** the account email, so a PA who changes it changes where SignatureRx sends its messages for that patient, with no check that it belongs to the patient and no note on the screen that the sign-in address is different. (2) An invitation is accepted only by an account whose email equals the invitation's address (`private.accept_invitation`, `account_email <> inv.email`), not `contact_email`, so changing it does **not** unlock an invitation; it only moves the 'this differs from the clinic's record' warning when the next invitation is sent. (3) Zod trims, caps at 254 and checks the format with `z.email()`; an empty box clears it. (4) The change is audited as 'the column `contact_email` changed', by the PA, with no old or new value. Nothing here is exploitable by a patient or a non-PA. | `lib/patients/actions.ts` `updatePatientContact`; `lib/data/signaturerx.ts` lines 72 to 74; `components/patients/contact-form.tsx` | Label the field 'Contact email (not the sign-in address)'. ## What was checked and held in the twenty-first run [2026-11]

Read from the code on the uncommitted `signaturerx` branch. `pnpm db:test` (761 tests) and `pnpm db:lint` run by me, both PASS. Nothing attacked, no app run, no SignatureRx call.

- **Settling a send (S-162, S-165).** Both functions: definer, `search_path = ''`, row lock, `sending` required, role and care-team check on the caller's own `auth.uid()`, wrappers are invokers so the caller is the actor in the audit; anon, public revoked. The action checks the doctor or admin role first and the database repeats it. A doctor outside the team, a PA, a nurse and a patient are refused in pgTAP. A used SignatureRx id is refused. Neither function can create or send a prescription; at worst a wrong close allows a second send (S-165 items 1 and 2) and a wrong id shows a wrong state (item 4).
- **Duplicate, hiding, someone else's id.** Duplicate: possible by a person's error or by closing during the first call (S-165). Hiding a real one: a close sets `failed` on a row with no SignatureRx id, so the real prescription would be unlinked, not erased; it is still in SignatureRx and the doctor is told to look there first. Someone else's id: only by typing one that is not already used here (S-165 item 4).
- **Admin.** An admin can close or confirm but cannot send, sign or hold a code; recorded as an owner decision in S-165 item 3.
- **PA contact form.** Column updates only, through the PA's session, under the existing policy and grants; `id`, `status`, `user_id` and `clinic_ref` are not in the update; a refused update is reported; sex is an enum; date of birth is validated and the column is `not null`; the audit trigger records changed column names by the PA. The pending-invitation effect of a date change is S-166; contact email versus the sign-in email is S-167.
- **Not checked:** a browser, the audit actor of the new functions by running them, SignatureRx's real behaviour, concurrency under load, whether a date such as 31 February is caught by Zod or only by Postgres (either way it is refused, and the screen would show the generic or specific message).

## What was checked and held in the twentieth run [2026-11]

Read from the code on the uncommitted `signaturerx` branch after the fixes. `pnpm db:lint` and `pnpm db:test` run by me (both PASS). Nothing attacked, no app run, no SignatureRx call.

- **Scope (S-150).** `staff_may_send_prescription` needs a `doctor` row in `user_roles`, active staff, a current care-team row, and the patient is neither the sender nor a guardian-linked person of theirs. The action calls the public wrapper `can_send_to_signaturerx` through the doctor's session (so `auth.uid()` is the caller's), and `start_signaturerx_send` repeats it for `_by`. Both set `search_path = ''`. The wrapper cannot be made to answer for another person: it takes only the request id.
- **Double send (S-151).** Row lock on the request, then the check, then the insert; the unique index is the second guard (the `unique_violation` is caught and turned into 23505). `finish` and `fail` update only a row still `sending`; `finish` raises if none matched, `fail` does not (a repeat is harmless). The audit trigger records each step (actor the doctor for the opening, `signaturerx` system job for the closing, S-62).
- **Order of work in the action.** Role and switch, form, throttle, request through the doctor's session, scope, patient data, address of origin, code, then open, then call SignatureRx. The service role is used only after those and only for the code and the five server functions.
- **PA contact edit.** See S-163: grants, policy, validation, refusal reporting and audit hold; nothing writes a column outside the six.
- **Twenty-first run additions** (see the section below).
- **Not checked:** a browser, a build, the real SignatureRx behaviour, the guardian branch by test, any concurrency under load.

## What was checked and held in the nineteenth run [2026-11]

Read from the code on the uncommitted `signaturerx` branch. `pnpm db:test` was run by me (PASS); `pnpm db:lint` was run by me (FAIL, S-160, not this branch). Nothing was attacked, no app was run, no SignatureRx call was made.

- **The PIN, step by step.** (1) It enters as `formData.get("pin")` in `sendToSignatureRx`, only after the doctor role, the switch and the form are checked; (2) `sendSchema` requires six digits and its error message is fixed text, with no input in it; (3) it goes to `createPrescription` as `securePin` and into `JSON.stringify(body)` as `secure_pin`; (4) nothing else touches it: it is not in `console.error` (both calls are fixed strings, the second names only a SignatureRx prescription id), not in `SignatureRxError` messages, not in the returned `SignatureRxFormState` (`error`, `saved`, `sentAt` only), not in the database (no column, no function argument; the function signatures hold the request, the SignatureRx id, action, state and sender), not in the audit log (column names only, and no such column), not in `revalidatePath`, and not on the clinic page. (5) In the browser the field has a `name` and no `value`, is not in `useState` (only the medicine rows are), is cleared by React's form reset after the action and by the `key` that remounts the form after a send. (6) Next.js carries the action's form data in the request body only; `useActionState` keeps only the returned state; the page's RSC payload carries `panel` and the sent list, neither of which holds a PIN or a code. What could still expose it: SignatureRx's own echo (S-155 item 2), a password-save offer (S-158), a future `console.log` of the body, and an exception thrown with the body in its message (none today). Not tested in a browser or on a build.
- **The integration code:** saved through `set_my_signaturerx_code` under the doctor's own session (role and identity checked inside, 42501 for anyone else), read back only by `service_role`, sent to SignatureRx in the request body and as the `integration-code` header; never returned to the browser (`has_my_signaturerx_code` returns a boolean); not in the audit log. Plain text at rest (S-153).
- **Who can do what (database).** `anon`: nothing (no grant, no policy; execute revoked). `authenticated`: no grant at all on `signaturerx_prescribers`; `select` on `signaturerx_prescriptions` through one policy, for doctors, PAs and admins in `in_scope` (a nurse, a health coach, a patient and a guardian read nothing); no insert, update or delete grant on either. `service_role`: `select` only on both tables; writes only through the two definer functions. The record function refuses a request that is not approved or is withdrawn and a sender who is not an active doctor. No `FOR ALL`.
- **Who can do what (application).** `sendToSignatureRx` refuses anyone without the doctor role or who is suspended before anything else; the database refuses a non-doctor a second time; a nurse, PA, admin, health coach, patient or guardian cannot send (pgTAP covers PA, admin and patient saving a code and a PA as the sender). `checkSignatureRxStatus` is for a doctor or PA and reads the row through the caller's session, so an out-of-scope prescription cannot be checked. The form is shown only to a doctor, for an approved request, with the switch on and the patient record complete.
- **Outbound.** Two SignatureRx origins only; no user input in the host or path; token request and calls have timeouts; the token cache is in memory and clears on a refusal; `pnpm srx:check` never prints the id, secret or token (read).
- **Not checked:** the app side by any test; the behaviour on a real SignatureRx account (staging); whether SignatureRx locks a PIN, repeats a `client_ref_id` or sends anything to the patient; a browser's treatment of the two password inputs; the proxy and CSP (unchanged by this branch).

## What was checked and held in the eighteenth run [2026-11]

Read from the code on `feat/repeat-prescriptions`; nothing was run or attacked by me (see Test results).

- **RLS on the new tables:** `prescribable_medicines` and `repeat_request_items` are enabled and forced. `anon` and `PUBLIC` get nothing, and `service_role` loses delete and truncate. `prescribable_medicines` has one `SELECT` policy (current entries for everyone signed in, archived entries for admins and doctors) plus `INSERT` and `UPDATE` policies for admins and doctors, with column grants (`name`, `weight_loss`, `sort_order`, `archived_at`) and no delete. `repeat_request_items` has one `SELECT` policy (`my_patient_ids()` or `in_scope`) and no write grant. No `FOR ALL` and no `CASCADE`. Both tables carry the audit trigger.
- **`repeat_requests` is closed to direct writes:** the insert and update policies are dropped and `insert, update` revoked from `authenticated`. That revocation also removes the earlier column grants. The coordinator's test checks a patient cannot set `paid_at` or insert. The read policy is unchanged.
- **Definer functions:** all six are in `private`, `SECURITY DEFINER`, `set search_path = ''`, with `SECURITY INVOKER` wrappers in `public` and `EXECUTE` revoked from `PUBLIC`, `anon` and `authenticated` before the grants. `request_repeat` checks `auth.uid()` is set, then `can_act_for(_patient)` or a PA in scope. It validates every line before writing. A current medicine must be the same patient's, active and not archived, and the composite key enforces this again. A list medicine must be current. Lines may not repeat and there are at most ten. The requester is `auth.uid()`, never a parameter. `cancel_repeat_request` and `decide_repeat_request` lock the row and check the caller against the row's own `patient_id`, so the id passed cannot widen access. `decide` allows only forward moves, needs a reason to decline and refuses a withdrawn request. `mark_repeat_request_paid` and `mark_run_out_reminded` are for `service_role` only and set `app.system_actor` (`stripe`, `prescription-reminder`), so the audit log names the job.
- **Another patient's request:** creating one needs `can_act_for` in the database, whatever `patientId` the form sends. Reading one goes through RLS, and the portal page also requires the portal's chosen patient. Withdrawing one is checked by the database. Paying for one is the gap in S-141, and a payment cannot move to a different request because the request id comes from the session metadata our server set.
- **Stripe URLs:** `success_url` and `cancel_url` are `SITE_URL` + `/portal/prescriptions/` + a request id that the database returned or that passed `z.uuid()`. No request field or host header is used. The redirect target is Stripe's returned URL, accepted only if it starts with `https://checkout.stripe.com/`. The `?paid=1` banner only says payment is being confirmed and changes nothing.
- **Webhook:** it answers 404 when Stripe is not configured. The body is capped at 256 kB. The signature is HMAC-SHA256 over `t.body`, checked in constant time against every `v1` value, and refused outside ±300 s. Each event id is stored once (`unique (source, external_event_id)`). A retry of a finished event does nothing, and a retry of an unfinished one completes it. The payment is recorded under a row lock, once, only for `payment_status = 'paid'`, `gbp`, and an amount equal to `fee_pence`. Only the session id, request id and amount are stored. Not under a proxy-protected prefix, so Stripe reaches it without a session.
- **No clinical detail leaves:** the Stripe line item is generic and the metadata is the request id. All four new emails (`repeatPaidAlertEmail`, `repeatReceivedEmail`, `repeatUpdateEmail`, `runOutReminderEmail`), every WhatsApp text and every bell notice carry at most the first name and a link, never a medicine, dose, weight or side-effect answer, or decline reason. The clinic alert names no patient. `webhook_events.payload` holds no customer detail.
- **Service role:** used only in `app/api/stripe/webhook/route.ts`, `lib/prescriptions/notify.ts` and `lib/prescriptions/reminders.ts`, all server-only (the two `lib` files and `lib/stripe/client.ts` start with `import "server-only"`). The Client Components `components/prescriptions/forms.tsx` and `request-form.tsx` import only the server actions and `constants.ts`. `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` and `STRIPE_WEBHOOK_SECRET` are read through `getStripeEnv()`, which asserts the server and validates the format. Neither is `NEXT_PUBLIC_`, and neither is logged.
- **Server actions:** every action reads the person from the session and checks the role on the server, and the database checks again. `requestRepeat`, `payRepeat` and `decideRepeat` share the `repeat-request` throttle (40 an hour per account). Doctors' medicine writes go through RLS (`Doctors add/change medications`, `in_scope`), with `prescriber_id` taken from the session, and `run_out_reminded_for` has no client grant.
- **Cron:** `/api/cron/prescription-reminders` fails closed without a `CRON_SECRET` of 32 or more characters, compares the whole header in constant time, and returns counts only.
- **CSP and headers:** `lib/csp.ts`, `next.config.ts` and `proxy.ts` are unchanged. No Stripe script or frame is loaded. `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin` means Stripe sees only the origin, not the request path.

## What was checked and held in the CQC run [2026-11]

Read from the code on `feat/cqc-audit`; nothing was run or attacked by me (see Test results).

- **Patients and guardians never read a sign-off:** RLS is enabled and forced on `lab_report_signoffs`; `anon` and `public` have nothing; `authenticated` has `select` only, under one policy, `private.in_scope(patient_id)`, which is false for anyone without a staff role and for a staff member's own record or a record they look after as a guardian (`my_patient_ids`). An embedded `lab_report_signoffs(...)` under `lab_reports` is filtered by the same policy, so a patient gets null. The only reader in the app is `getReport` (`lib/data/results.ts`), used only by `/clinic/results/[id]`; no portal query names the table.
- **Nobody forges a sign-off through the app or the Data API:** no `insert` grant or policy for `authenticated`, and since `d309621` none for `service_role` either (S-147) (test 18 checks a direct insert is refused); `private.release_report` is `SECURITY DEFINER` with `search_path = ''`, locks the report row, takes `signed_by` from `my_staff_id()`, `patient_id` from the report and `ordered_by` from `lab_orders`, and validates the outcome and note itself. The public wrapper is `SECURITY INVOKER` and `anon` has no `execute`. The exception is `service_role` (S-147).
- **Nobody alters or removes one:** a `before update or delete` trigger refuses every role, owner included (test 18), and the `before truncate` trigger, committed in `d309621`, closes `TRUNCATE` (S-149 item 3); no `update` or `delete` grant for anyone. Every foreign key is `ON DELETE RESTRICT`; no `CASCADE`. The audit trigger logs each insert, with no clinical values (S-148 for the missing id).
- **Only doctors in scope sign off:** `has_role('doctor')` (which needs an active staff profile) and `in_scope` inside the definer; a PA and a patient are refused in test 06, a nurse in test 09; a health coach and an admin fail the same check. A second doctor on the care team may sign off another doctor's order, which is recorded (`ordered_by` differs from `signed_by`), as the clinic decided. A doctor cannot sign off their own record (`in_scope` excludes it).
- **The dropped signature has no caller:** `public.release_report(uuid, text)` and `private.release_report(uuid, text)` are dropped; the only caller, `releaseReport` in `lib/results/actions.ts`, passes four named arguments and validates the outcome with Zod before the call; `lib/database.types.ts` shows only the new arguments; test 09's one-argument call still resolves and is refused with 42501.
- **Signed notes' option columns cannot change:** see S-149 item 4. `updateNote` and `signNote` write the options through the user's session, so RLS and the lock decide.
- **Not checked:** anything over HTTP; the rendered pages; whether the coordinator's test run used a freshly reset database.

## What was checked and held in the seventeenth run [2026-11]

Read from the code on `feat/small-items`; nothing was run or attacked by me (see Test results).

- **Patient switcher:** `choosePortalPatient` validates the id as a UUID and sets the cookie only if it is one of the rows `get_my_patient_record` returns for the caller; `getPortalPatient` matches the cookie against the same list on every read and otherwise falls back to the person's own record, then the first person they look after. A forged or stale value (another patient, another account's choice, garbage) shows nothing new. Every screen behind it (`getPortalPatientId`, `getPortalHome`, `getMyResults`) still reads through the session, so RLS and the release gate apply. The portal document route also passes the chosen patient to `signedDocumentUrl`, which then fails closed for a document of a different patient. Before this branch, `getPortalHome` and `getMyResults` took `data[0]` while other pages preferred `is_self`; they now agree.
- **Cron route:** not under a protected prefix in the proxy, so Vercel Cron reaches it without a session; fails closed without a 32-character `CRON_SECRET`; constant-time comparison of the full header; no secret, token or user id in the response or logs.
- **Queued token:** encrypted with AES-256-GCM, bound to the user id, before it ever reached the database; copied as ciphertext by the trigger; read only by `service_role` in a `server-only` module; cleared on success. The audit log would record only column names if a trigger were added (S-138).
- **Allergy removal:** `removeAllergy` checks the role in the app (doctor, nurse, PA), matches both the allergy id and the patient id, touches only unarchived rows, and RLS limits it to the care team; `patient_allergies` is audited, so who archived it is recorded. Test 16 shows a patient's update changes nothing. After `d777b66` the update policy's `with check` still allows `archived_at` to be set (it is unchanged), while its `using` refuses any row already archived, so archiving works once and only once.
- **Not checked:** anything over HTTP, the real Vercel Cron header, Google's revoke behaviour for a re-issued grant (S-140 item 1).

## What was checked and held in the fifteenth run [2026-11]

Read from the code on `feat/clinic-build-2`; nothing was run or attacked by me (see Test results).

- **Notes and unmatched files:** no insert grant includes `supersedes_id` and the trigger refuses it; `with check` on `documents` and `result_imports` refuses a doctor or nurse setting `patient_id` to null; `import_items` follow their import; the `unmatched/` Storage prefix is for PAs and admins only.
- **Photos and guardians:** the avatars read policy needs the reader to be active staff for anyone else's photo, and in scope for a patient's; removal of a guardian is an update of two booleans by a PA or admin in scope, never a delete, and never of their own link.
- **Booking:** RLS enabled and forced on both new tables, all grants revoked from `public`, `anon` and `authenticated` first, no delete for anyone (`service_role` included), audit triggers on both; only admins write types; hours have no update grant on `staff_id`; `book_appointment` takes the patient from `can_act_for` or `in_scope` (a patient cannot book for someone else, and a staff member who is also a patient books as a patient), checks an active type, an active clinical staff member, the window (more than an hour, less than 120 days), the clinician's London hours and, after locking the clinician row, overlaps with live bookings; `cancel_appointment` and `get_bookable_clinicians` check the caller inside; `set_appointment_calendar_event` and `set_patient_ghl_contact` are `service_role` only and set a named job; the booking action takes ids as UUIDs, leaves authorisation to the RPC, and redirects to fixed paths only.
- **Recording:** RLS forced on `call_recordings`, read for staff in scope only (patients never), insert for a doctor, nurse or coach in scope as themselves, no update grant for anyone, `service_role` reads only and writes through three definer functions that set the actor; the path check constraint ties the object to the row's patient; the recorder must be the one who sends; the note is a draft that only its author can sign.
- **Third parties:** the GoHighLevel token, AssemblyAI key and webhook secret are server-only and never logged; GoHighLevel and AssemblyAI errors are reduced to fixed messages; the Google event title is the type and clinic reference, with a fixed description.
- **Not checked:** PostgREST and Storage over HTTP (including whether `createSignedUploadUrl` checks the insert policy for the caller), the recorder in a browser, real GoHighLevel and AssemblyAI behaviour, and the CSP for the direct upload (`connect-src` already allows the Supabase origin).

## What was attacked and held in the fourteenth run [2026-11]

On the local stand-in described in the Test results table, in rolled-back transactions, as seed users; the app was not run. Commit `8e6e3a6` and the committed migrations unless marked.

- **Care team and scope:** a doctor not on a patient's team was refused a `care_team` insert for herself (RLS); an update of `patient_id` or `staff_id` on her own row was refused (no column grant), so a row cannot be moved to another patient or person; the `with check` calls a `STABLE` function, which sees the statement's snapshot, so a row cannot vouch for itself in the same statement (from the code; a multi-row insert was not tried); a nurse could not add to a care team; a coach on no team read 0 care-team rows; a coach read no safeguarding alert. `in_scope`, `is_clinical_staff`, `log_record_view`, `enter_report`, `register_import`, `is_doctor_only`, `inviter_covers_patient`, `may_invite`, `create_invitation`, `resend_invitation`, `revoke_invitation`, `get_invitable_patients`, `sign_clinical_note` and `course_is_mine` are `SECURITY DEFINER` with `search_path = ''`; the invitation helpers are `EXECUTE` for `service_role` only, `sign_clinical_note`, `course_is_mine` and the scope helpers for `authenticated`; none for `anon` or `PUBLIC`. The public wrappers are `SECURITY INVOKER`.
- **Doctor invitations:** `create_invitation` takes the inviter from `user.id` in the session (`lib/invitations/actions.ts` line 196), never from the form; a doctor-only inviter is refused for a patient not on their team, and a new patient gets a `primary_doctor` row for that doctor only; resend and cancel check the same rule. A doctor sees only the patient invitations they sent.
- **Notes:** the insert grant leaves out `status`, `signed_at`, `signed_by`, `version` and `id`, so nobody inserts a signed note; only the author signs, through the function, and only while in scope; a signed note refused an update from its author (0 rows) and from the database owner (the trigger raised); there is no delete grant and the trigger refuses deletes; `service_role` has no update.
- **Education and wearables:** RLS enabled and forced on all five tables; no `FOR ALL`; no delete for anyone; course content reaches a patient only when the course is published, not archived and assigned to them (`course_is_mine` reads `my_patient_ids()` for the caller); a guardian without `can_act` was refused a wearable insert; only the person who entered a reading can archive it, while they can still act. Video links are `https` only in the table and the action, and render with `rel="noopener noreferrer"`; module text is rendered as text.
- **Server actions:** every new action reads the person and staff id from the session, validates ids as UUIDs, and leaves the patient to RLS through the user's own session; none uses the service role; no Client Component imports `lib/supabase/admin`.
- **Not tested:** PostgREST itself (embedded joins on the new tables, the OpenAPI listing), Storage over HTTP, and the app in a browser.

## What was checked and held in the thirteenth run [2026-11]

Read from the code only; nothing was executed against a database or Google.

- **Table:** RLS enabled and forced. One policy, `SELECT` for `authenticated`, own row only and only while `private.is_active_staff(auth.uid())`. No `FOR ALL`. Every privilege revoked from `public`, `anon`, `authenticated` and `service_role`, then `authenticated` gets `SELECT (user_id, google_email, calendar_id, connected_at)` and `service_role` gets `SELECT`. No insert, update or delete grant, so the only writes are the two functions. `user_id` is the primary key and `references profiles on delete restrict`.
- **Functions:** `private.save_google_calendar_connection` and `private.remove_google_calendar_connection` are `SECURITY DEFINER` with `search_path = ''`, set `app.actor_id` with `is_local = true`, and are `EXECUTE` for `service_role` only; the public wrappers are `SECURITY INVOKER` with `search_path = ''` and the same grants. They take the user id as an argument rather than reading `auth.uid()`, which is acceptable only because `authenticated` cannot execute them; both callers pass the id from `getCurrentUser`, never from a form. Save refuses anyone who is not active staff (42501).
- **Audit:** `write_audit_log` records the column names that changed, never values, so the token is not copied into `audit_log`.
- **OAuth:** state is a 32-byte random nonce in `ch_google_state` (`HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax`, `Path=/api/google`, 10 minutes, `Secure` in production), bound to the user id and compared with `timingSafeEqual` before the code is exchanged; the cookie is cleared on every outcome of the callback. The callback refuses a signed-out, suspended or non-staff caller. The redirect URI comes from `SITE_URL`. The scope Google granted is checked for `calendar.events`, and a token without it is revoked. Logged errors name the step, never a token or code.
- **Token:** AES-256-GCM, fresh 12-byte IV, user id as AAD, key validated as 32 bytes at start; the placeholder in `.env.example` fails that check, so the page says "not set up". Revocation posts the token in the body, not the URL. Disconnect revokes and deletes.
- **Boundaries:** `google.ts` and `crypto.ts` import `server-only`; `event-forms.tsx` (a Client Component) imports only the server actions; no Client Component imports `lib/supabase/admin`. Server actions take the person from the session; the event id is pattern-checked and only reaches that person's own calendar. Google's text is rendered as text, not HTML. The CSP, `next.config.ts` and the proxy are unchanged.

## What was attacked and held in the twelfth run [2026-11]

Verified on the local stack with `psql`, in transactions that were rolled back (the audit log held 325 rows before and after), as seed users. The app was not run; the screen and email points are from the code.

- **Definer functions:** `private.log_record_view` and `private.activity_log` are `security definer` with `search_path = ''`, fully qualified names, and checks inside. `log_record_view` refuses a missing `auth.uid()`, anyone who is not clinical staff (a patient, an admin and the nurse were each refused, 42501), anyone not `in_scope` (a null patient id is refused, because `in_scope` is false for null), and an entity not on the list of two (22023). `activity_log` refuses everyone but an active admin (PA, doctor, nurse, patient and signed out refused, 42501). Both wrappers are `SECURITY INVOKER` with `search_path = ''`.
- **Grants:** all four functions are revoked from `public`, `anon` and `authenticated`, then granted to `authenticated` only; `anon` has no execute (tested by `07`). `audit_log` still has no grant for `authenticated` and no policy.
- **The log stores no clinical value:** a `viewed` row holds the actor, role, entity, a record id and `{patient_id}`; the trigger-written rows hold column names only. `gender_identity` appears in the log only as a changed column name.
- **Append-only holds:** the new rows are ordinary inserts through the definer function; `reject_audit_change` is unchanged. The repeat check is correct for the same record (one row for two opens by the same person in 10 minutes, tested), and its lookup uses `audit_log_actor_id_idx`.
- **The nurse:** zero rows from `patient_profiles`, `lab_reports` and `results`; cannot call either function; cannot read the log. `invitations_kind_fields` allows `nurse` only for `kind = 'team'` with no patient, relationship or `can_act`; an invitation for a patient or guardian cannot carry the role. Acceptance creates `staff_profiles.clinical_role` and `user_roles` from the invitation's role, so a nurse invitation grants the nurse role and nothing broader.
- **Keyset paging:** `_before` and `_limit` are typed (`bigint`, `integer`); a null limit gives 50 and a huge limit is capped at 100 (tested). The page accepts only 1 to 15 digits for `before`, so a malformed cursor is ignored.
- **Did not hold, then fixed and re-read:** S-100 and S-101 above (and S-102 on the page).
- **Not tested:** the Activity page and the report page in a browser, the nurse's navigation, a real email render, and load on `activity_log` with a large log (it scans by primary key descending with a limit; the joins are per row).

## What was attacked and held in the eleventh run [2026-11]

Verified on the local stack with `psql` in rolled-back transactions, as seed users (`request.jwt.claims` and `set local role authenticated`). The app was not run and no file was uploaded; the Storage and upload steps are from the code.

- **Who can call each function (from `pg_proc`):** `private.enter_report`, `private.register_import`, `private.release_report` and `private.result_flag` are `EXECUTE` for `authenticated` only (not `anon`, not `service_role`); `public.enter_report`, `public.register_import` and `public.release_report` are `EXECUTE` for `authenticated` and `service_role`, not `anon`. The three private entry functions are `SECURITY DEFINER` with `search_path = ''`; the three public wrappers are `SECURITY INVOKER` with `search_path = ''`; `result_flag` is immutable invoker. A `service_role` call to a wrapper runs the private function as `service_role`, which has no `EXECUTE` on it, so it fails on privilege (from the grants; not run). Even with it, `auth.uid()` is null and `my_staff_id()` would refuse. `private` is not exposed through the Data API.
- **Release is for doctors only:** the seed patient (Eleanor) and the seed PA were each refused `release_report` ("You cannot release this report."); a patient was refused `enter_report` for her own record. The doctor's release worked, and the release function locks the row, refuses a second release and sets `reviewed_by`, `reviewed_at` and `released_at` together (the table check ties `status = 'released'` to `released_at`).
- **Patients see results only after release:** the policy reads `report_is_released`; the new `original_value` and `original_unit` columns are covered by the existing table-level `select` and the same row policy, so no new route. The patient screen asks for `released_at is not null` as well as relying on the policy.
- **A doctor who is also a patient:** the seed doctor-patient (Laura) read her own unreleased result through the staff policy, as `in_scope` allows (the Data API gives staff that read by design), and `getMyResults` filters to released reports and to her own record, so `/portal/results` showed none until release. See S-92 for entering and releasing her own.
- **Audit actor for authenticated calls:** a doctor's release (a definer function) and comment edits were recorded under the doctor's profile id with role `doctor` and `changed: ["doctor_comment"]`; `auth.uid()` is unchanged inside a definer function, so the actor is the signed-in person. `marker_conversions` has the audit trigger (it has no delete grant). Old comment text is not kept (S-89).
- **The signed URL:** `getReport` signs the lab file for 300 seconds through the person's own session, after reading the `documents` row under row-level security, so the path is looked up from a row the caller may read and is never taken from a request; the Storage select policy repeats that check. The link opens with `rel="noopener noreferrer"` in a new tab and is shown only on the clinic report screen. A patient has no screen that signs the file. A signed link stays valid until it expires if copied (S-30).
- **Inputs:** a value of `NaN`, infinity or more than 10^9 is refused; more than 80 rows, a repeated marker, an unknown marker, a missing conversion and a future or pre-1990 sample date are refused by the function; the form's JSON is parsed by Zod first. Row counts and lab-name length are limited in both places.
- **No cascade, no delete:** the new table uses `on delete restrict`; no new grant gives `authenticated` a delete.
- **Not tested:** a real file upload, the Storage policies with a PA's path for an unknown patient, the 11 MB boundary, the Vercel body limit, the throttle's limits, and the screens in a browser.

## What was attacked and held in the tenth run [2026-11]

All verified unless marked, on the local stack, with the app as a production build of a copy of the tree on port 3141.

- **Every new function through the Data API:** `create_invitation`, `resend_invitation`, `revoke_invitation`, `get_invitation` and `accept_invitation` returned 401 for `anon` and 403 for a patient, doctor, health coach, PA and admin (granted to `service_role` only). `get_invitable_patients` returned 401 for `anon`, an empty list for the patient, doctor and health coach, and the 11 patients (name, reference, account flag) for the PA and the admin. The helpers `person_has_role`, `may_invite` and `invitation_lifetime` are in `private`, which the API does not expose (a request with `Accept-Profile: private` for `person_has_role` got a 404; the one that returned a row was the `public` wrapper of the same name).
- **The table:** `select token_hash` and `select *` are permission denied for every role; the allowed columns read only what the policy allows (PA: patient invitations only; admin: all; doctor, health coach, patient and `anon`: nothing). `POST`, `PATCH` and `DELETE` as an admin and as a PA are refused. Column grants for the service role are the whole table (S-28).
- **Server actions with forged form data:** `_inviter`, `inviter`, `invited_by`, `role=admin` and a 64-character `token_hash` added to a patient invitation changed nothing (the row was made for the signed-in PA, with the role `patient`); a PA asking for `team` with the role `admin` or `pa` got "You cannot send this kind of invitation"; a team role `patient` is refused by the schema and by the database; a bad uuid, an unknown patient, an unknown relationship, a future or 1850 date of birth, a 121-character name and the PA's own address were all refused with plain messages; resend and cancel of a team invitation by a PA got "not found" and "You cannot cancel this kind"; a doctor, a patient and a health coach calling `sendInvitation` or `cancelInvitation` were redirected; signed out they got a 307 to `/login`; a post with a foreign or `null` `Origin` returned 500 and wrote nothing. The `/clinic/team` page itself redirects a doctor and a health coach and 307s a patient; its nav item is not in their HTML.
- **Service-role calls with the wrong person:** an unknown inviter, a patient, a doctor, a PA asking for team, a team role `patient`, the inviter's own address, an existing staff member, a patient who has an account, an unknown or archived patient and a short hash were each refused with the function's own message (the short hash by the table check). A patient invitation given a team job title and the role `admin` was stored as a plain patient invitation.
- **Acceptance:** concurrent accept of one link (two `psql` sessions): one success, the other "already been used", one staff profile, one role, one notification. Expired, cancelled, used, wrong address (case-insensitively equal addresses accepted), suspended invitee, inviter deactivated, inviter without the role, and an invitation whose patient has since got an account were each refused. A guardian link made for an existing doctor was `verified_by` the PA's staff profile and logged under her. A patient record created from minimal details got the next `SC-` reference from the private sequence, an invitation expiring in exactly 3 days, and the PA as actor on the insert and on every step of the acceptance.
- **Constraints:** `invitations_kind_fields` refused a guardian with a job title, a team invitation with a patient, a team role `patient` and a patient invitation with `can_act`; `invitations_not_used_and_cancelled`, `invitations_expiry_is_short` and the unique `token_hash` index held.
- **The link:** the route sets an `HttpOnly`, `Secure` (production), `SameSite=Lax`, `Path=/invite`, one-hour cookie only for a 43-character token (42, 44 and junk set nothing and the page says "Open your invitation link"); it ignores `next=` and a forged `Host` and `X-Forwarded-Host` (no open redirect); `POST` is 405; the response is `no-store`; the accept page has the CSP with a fresh nonce and `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, contains no token, and the log held no token. Timing: median 7.3 ms for an unknown token against 8.1 ms for a valid one (the difference is the extra work to render the form; the lookup is an index equality on a 256-bit hash). Fixation: a victim signed in as Thomas with a cookie planted for another address got "Sign out to continue" and no create form, and an accept call was refused with "Sign in with the address the invitation was sent to"; a call with no session redirected to the same page.
- **Account creation:** short, mismatched and 80-character passwords were refused and created nothing; a good password created a confirmed account, the role and the profile, and signed the person in with the right session kind (portal for patients). Throttle on the accept page's sign-in: refused from the 8th wrong password, with the same message for any account.
- **Service role in the client:** the only imports of `lib/supabase/admin.ts` are four server files (`throttle.ts`, `actions.ts`, `accept-actions.ts`, `lookup.ts`, which is `server-only`); the three client components import only the action modules, and `grep` of every file in the built client chunks found neither the service role key nor `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`, `create_invitation`, `accept_invitation` or `token_hash`.
- **Emails:** the typed name is escaped in the template (`<b>Zed</b>` as a patient name appears as text); the link is built from `SITE_URL`. No email was sent (none is set up locally), so Resend, delivery and what a real recipient sees were not tested.
- **Not tested:** any real email through Resend (and whether click tracking rewrites the link); the send limits (40 an hour per inviter) by sending that many; Safari and Firefox; the accept page on a very slow network under the production CSP; tablet and mobile layouts (not security); the hosted project; concurrent create-account by two tabs through the app (the database side was run at the database); an email change on an invited address (reasoned: `get_invitation` and `accept_invitation` read `auth.users.email`, which changes only after the new mailbox confirms, so a pending change does not move it).

## What was attacked and held in the ninth run [2026-11]

All verified unless marked, on the local stack. App checks used a production build of a copy of the tree on port 3142 (no `.env*`, local keys, `SESSION_COOKIE_SECRET=local-development-only-secret-0123456789`), driven from the browser pane with same-origin form posts and direct action calls; Storage, Data API and Auth checks used `fetch` with tokens from the seed users' real sign-ins. What did not hold is under S-37, S-68, S-69, S-70, S-71 and S-72.

- **Server actions with forged form data:** extra `userId`, `email`, `path` and `bucket` fields changed nothing. No action reads an id from the form: the person comes from `getCurrentUser()`. A signed-out call to `uploadSignature` got a 307 to `/login`. A PA calling it directly (the action id taken from a doctor's page, sent as a real action request on three paths) got "Only doctors have a signature", and Storage refused the same PA's upload on its own (403). A patient's call was not repeated through the app: the code path is the same role check, and the Data API refused patient and PA uploads to `signatures`.
