- **Uploads through the app:** a text file, an SVG named `.png`, a GIF, PNG bytes declared as WebP or JPEG, and an empty file were refused with "That file is not a valid image" or "Choose an image first"; SVG declared as SVG was refused by type; a 2.5 MB and 2.9 MB image by the action's size rule; a 3.3 MB one by Next.js (S-73). A signature of 1.5 MB, 2.5 MB and SVG was refused. A valid PNG was accepted and stored at exactly `<id>/avatar` or `<id>/signature`, with only that object created.
- **Storage through the Data API:** patient, PA and admin uploads to `signatures` refused; a doctor's upload to another name, to another doctor's folder, with `..`, as SVG and at 1.5 MB refused; a second doctor's upload to the first doctor's path refused. In `avatars`: into another folder, with `..`, with a folder name that only starts with the person's id, SVG and 2.5 MB refused. Nobody else could read or sign the doctor's signature (second doctor, PA, admin, coach, patient: 400, "Object not found"); listing it returned an empty list; the owner could read and sign it. A patient could not read another patient's photo; a guardian and the coach could not read a patient's; a PA, a doctor and an admin could. Deleting a file is refused (403), though `move` worked inside the folder (S-70). The bucket has no public route (anonymous `public/avatars` answered 400).
- **Profile and preference writes:** a patient cannot update `profiles.email` (403 by grant), cannot update another person's profile or preferences (0 rows), cannot add a preference for another person, move a row to another person, change its topic or channel, or delete it (all refused); only `enabled` can change on an existing row, and a made-up topic is refused by the enum. A patient could point her own `avatar_path` at another person's path, which gives nothing: the signed URL is made under her session and Storage refuses it. A doctor's `signature_path` pointing at another doctor's folder is refused by a constraint; another doctor cannot update her row; a clinical-role change is refused.
- **Password change through the app:** wrong current password, mismatch and weak password all refused, validation ahead of the throttle; the throttle stopped the 9th wrong attempt for one address and account, with the same message that says nothing about the account. A forged `x-real-ip` restarted the count (S-72), bounded by the account limit.
- **Email change through the app:** a wrong password was refused; a taken address (two different accounts) and an unused one all gave the same reply in about the same time; the person's own address, in any case, was refused with a different, harmless message. The Auth API does not behave so (S-69).
- **S-23 trigger:** a confirmed email change reached `profiles.email`; the client cannot set it.
- **CSP and images:** the profile page's CSP has `img-src 'self' data: blob:` plus the Supabase origin; the signed photo loaded under it in the browser; the page was `no-store`; `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin` and `nosniff` were present. A signed URL works anonymously for its hour (S-30).
- **Tests:** `pnpm db:test` 197 tests passed in the ninth run (after the local data was put back); 202 pass after the fixes, with and without a pre-existing signature, and the `04_profile` cases (own signature stored, other names and folders refused, patient and PA refused, nobody else reads it) ran.
- **Not tested:** hosted Auth and Storage behaviour; the staff 12-hour limit through an email-change request (from the code); a deactivated doctor's upload through the app (S-16, from the code); `revalidatePath` effects beyond reading the code; tablet widths and dark mode (not security).

## What was attacked and held in the fifth run [2026-11]

All verified unless marked, on a production build and the local stack. What did not hold is under S-31 (what remains), S-07 (still to do), S-10, S-57, S-58 and S-59 to S-62.

- **CSP on every route:** see S-12. No page tested served without the header apart from the matcher exclusions, and none with an unreplaced or foreign nonce. A forged `Content-Security-Policy` or `x-nonce` request header was overwritten.
- **Signed cookies:** see S-31. Twelve forms of edited, malformed, truncated, foreign and mis-named values were refused; correctly signed ones were accepted and aged correctly. The secret failed closed when missing or short.
- **Session guard:** see S-48.
- **Narrowed scope:** see S-10. The altered policies, `result_imports` with a null patient, and `import_items` following their import all held.
- **Service-role writes:** see S-07. PostgREST, a definer wrapper, the Auth service and the owner's SQL were all tried.
- **Nothing regressed in the database:** `pnpm db:test` 188 tests, `db:lint` and `db:advisors` clean. A PA's Data API write is still attributed to the PA.
- **Open redirects on `/auth/confirm`:** `next=//evil.com` with a junk token still ended at `/login?error=link`, with the CSP.
- **Secrets:** the local secret appeared in 0 files of `.next` and 0 lines of the server log. The 500 body when the secret is missing is `Internal Server Error`, with no detail.

## What was attacked and held in the third run [2026-11]

All verified unless marked. What did not hold is under S-31, S-32 and S-48 to S-54. S-46 and S-47 were fixed and re-tested in the fourth run.

- **Redirect loops:** an expired session on `/login` goes once to `/login?error=expired` with all `sb-` and `ch_*` cookies cleared, and that request then returns 200. The redirect clears the cookies itself, so a failed sign-out cannot loop (from the code).
- **Server action on expiry:** a `POST` with an expired session gets a 307 to `/login?error=expired` with the cookies cleared, and the action does not run on that hop. A browser repeats the `POST` there. A sign-out action then got a 200 with a JSON body on the dev server and did not redirect. From Next's client code, that raises "An unexpected response was received from the server", so the person sees the error boundary, with their session already ended, in place of the sign-in page. Not run in a browser, because the `HttpOnly` cookie cannot be aged there. Actions with no session reach only RLS, which gives `anon` nothing.
- **Sign-out:** clears all three cookies and the refresh token returns 400.
- **Forged tokens:** an access token with an edited `amr` and `sub` redirected to `/login`.
- **Header spoofing against the app's account limits:** rotating `x-real-ip` and `x-forwarded-for` never got past the 30 per account limit. A blocked `x-real-ip` wins over a new `x-forwarded-for`. Whether a client can rotate them in production depends on the host: see the checklist.
- **Service role and bundle:** `lib/supabase/admin.ts` is now imported only by `lib/auth/throttle.ts`, which starts with `import "server-only"`. `SERVICE_ROLE`, `service_role` and `auth_throttle` appear in 0 files of `.next/static` and `.next/dev/static`. The throttle's error log holds no email or address (from the code). This updates the second run's statement that nothing imported `admin.ts`.
- **Catalogue:** `pnpm db:lint`, `pnpm db:test` and `pnpm db:advisors` pass with the new migration. The counters are in `private`, not the exposed schema.

## What was attacked and held in the second run [2026-11]

All verified unless marked.

- **Doctor who is a patient on `/portal`:** the portal's results query, sent over HTTP as a doctor for a patient with an unreleased report, returned 30 of the 32 rows RLS allows, so the query's own `released_at` filter works. In a rolled-back transaction with Dr Bennett's own released and unreleased reports, only the released value returned. Her own record comes first. Only the letter query lacks the gate (S-41). On `/clinic` she still sees her own unreleased report (S-21).
- **Open redirects:** `next` of `//evil.example`, `https://evil.example`, `/\evil.example` and an encoded variant all ended at `/login?error=link`, with no valid token. With a valid token `next` is limited by an exact allow-list (from the code). Unknown `type` values are refused. The `/login?error=` text comes from a fixed list apart from S-33.
- **Account enumeration:** sign-in gives one message for every failure, and "no access" appears only after a correct password. Reset always replies the same. Timing is S-34.
- **Sessions on screens:** `lib/data/`, `lib/auth/session.ts` and every page read through `lib/supabase/server.ts`. Nothing imports `lib/supabase/admin.ts` or `lib/email/send.ts` at all yet.
- **Client Components:** those importing from `lib/data/` use `import type` only. `lib/auth/actions.ts` reaches the browser only as server action references.
- **Secrets in the bundle:** the values of `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` and `RESEND_API_KEY`, and those names and `service_role`, appear in 0 files of `.next/static` and `.next/dev/static`. `.env.local` was never committed. No key pattern in `app`, `components`, `lib` or `.env.example`.
- **CSRF on server actions:** a `POST` with a foreign or `null` `Origin` returned 500 and the action did not run. The same request with the same origin ran. A request with no `Origin` runs, but only a non-browser client sends that, and it has no victim's cookie. Cookies are `SameSite=Lax` (from the code). See S-44 for `X-Forwarded-Host`.
- **Layout routing, with real session cookies:** a patient on `/clinic` goes to `/portal`, and a doctor with no patient record on `/portal` goes to `/clinic`. Signed-out requests go to `/login`. A crafted router request for `/clinic` as a patient was redirected and rendered no page. Pages do not check the role themselves, but RLS would return a patient nothing there.
- **Data in the page:** the admin's `/clinic` holds no seeded patient name and 0 to review. The health coach's has 0 to review. A patient's `/portal` holds no other patient's name, no external ids and no tokens. Authenticated pages send `Cache-Control: no-store`. Error boundaries show no error text.
- **Catalogue:** RLS enabled and forced on all 32 tables, no `FOR ALL`, no `SECURITY DEFINER` in `public`, every function sets `search_path`, no `anon` table grants, no `CASCADE` or `SET NULL`, no table in the Realtime publication. `authenticated` can delete only from `staff_locations` and `lab_panel_markers`, which are not clinical.
- **Headers and sign-up:** `disable_signup` is true locally. `next.config.ts` is unchanged.

## What was attacked and held in the first run [2026-11]

All verified unless marked.

- **Other patients' data:** direct reads and filters returned nothing on results, reports, documents, medications, diagnoses and guardian links.
- **Unreleased results:** `lab_reports` and `results` by id returned `[]` over HTTP. Embeds from `lab_orders` to `lab_reports`, `documents`, `patient_profiles` and `staff_profiles` returned only released reports and nulls.
- **Other schemas:** `private`, `auth` and `storage` profiles were refused with 406. GraphQL was refused. Public `/rpc/` has only two no-argument functions, and neither is `SECURITY DEFINER`.
- **Storage:** private buckets, `object/public` refused, listing returned nothing, signing an unreadable path failed. Writes to another patient's folder and `unmatched/` were refused. No patient overwrite or rename. No bucket creation.
- **Roles:** no signed-in user can write `user_roles`. A doctor or PA cannot change their own `clinical_role`, `gmc_number`, `active` or `archived_at` (trigger). Own `status` and `email` changes refused.
- **Escalation by invitation:** a PA cannot invite `admin` or `doctor`, or spoof `invited_by`. A suspended PA cannot insert an invitation.
- **Payment and values:** patients cannot set `fee_pence` or `status`. Repeat requests for another patient, or a medication of another patient, are refused. A PA cannot set `paid`. A result value cannot be changed from the client.
- **Safeguarding:** PA, health coach, admin, patient and guardian all read zero rows.
- **Audit log:** no client access; `postgres` and `service_role` cannot update, delete or truncate. Metadata holds column names only.
- **Admin:** reads no `patient_profiles`, `results`, `documents`, `questions` or guardian links.
- **Catalogue:** RLS enabled and forced on all 32 tables, no `FOR ALL` policy, no `CASCADE` or `SET NULL`, every function sets `search_path`. `authenticated` has no `create` on any schema. No table in the `supabase_realtime` publication. A new table or function in `public` starts with no grants, but also with RLS off, which `00_schema_checks` would catch.
- **App code:** `lib/supabase/admin.ts` and `lib/email/send.ts` start with `import "server-only"`, and no committed file imports either from a Client Component. `lib/env.ts` reports variable names only. `.env.local` is ignored and untracked. No secret found in tracked files in scope. The seed uses only `@example.test` addresses.

## Not reviewed in the first two runs [2026-11]

- Server actions and functions for invitations, release, import confirmation and webhooks: not built.
- Actual uploads through the Storage API, because they would have left data. Storage policies were tested at database level only.
- A valid recovery link, a sent reset email, or a production build. Checks ran against the dev server.
- Suspended and deactivated users in the running app, because that needs a committed change. Their reads were tested by SQL, and their routing by reading the code.
- Rate limits, because using them up would lock the shared local stack.
- The hosted project: never connected to.

Not reviewed in the fifth run: the real 30-minute idle wait and a sleeping device (S-61), the CSP in a real hosted response (Vercel may add, drop or cache headers: see the checklist), `style-src-elem` tightening, Supabase's behaviour under a real `SESSION_COOKIE_SECRET` rotation, and any server function that sets the actor (none exists).

Also not reviewed in the third run: a production build or Vercel, a valid recovery link end to end, the throttle's failure path in a running server, Supabase's per-IP limits (not active locally), and the idle timer in a real browser.

## Documented decisions

- [2026-11] **Superseded [2026-11] by the clinic's answers of 11 November (below).** **Admin is deliberately not clinical staff.** `is_clinical_staff` excludes admin, and tests confirm no clinical reads. Admins do read all `profiles`, `care_team` links and `invitations.patient_id` (verified: 11, 12 and 3 rows), so they can see who is a patient and who cares for them. See S-03 for the route around this decision. Open question 2 in `docs/PRD.md`.
- [2026-11] **Superseded [2026-11] by the clinic's answers of 11 November (below).** **`in_scope` is open until the clinic answers.** See S-10. Open question 1 in `docs/PRD.md`.
- [2026-11] **Roles live in `user_roles`, written only by server code,** never read from the JWT.
- [2026-11] **Definer code lives in `private`,** not exposed through the Data API. `authenticated` needs `usage` on it because policies call the helpers. Hidden columns are served through invoker wrappers in `public`.
- [2026-11] **No hard deletes.** No `delete` for `authenticated` on clinical tables, and none for `service_role` either. No delete policy on Storage.
- [2026-11] **A staff role needs an active staff profile.** `private.is_active_staff` is checked by `has_role`, `is_clinical_staff` and `is_team`, so deactivating someone ends their access without touching their roles.
- [2026-11] **No browser client.** All Supabase calls run on the server and auth cookies are `HttpOnly`. Any later browser feature, such as direct uploads or Realtime, needs its own design. The Supabase origins in `connect-src` can probably go.
- [2026-11] **Session limits are enforced by the app.** 12 hours and 30 minutes idle for staff, 7 days and 2 hours for patients, read from a signed `amr` claim and from `ch_*` cookies signed with `SESSION_COOKIE_SECRET`. The cookies were unsigned until the fifth run. The residual risk (a copied refresh token, which only Supabase's Pro-plan limits end) is recorded in S-31, and no human has yet formally accepted it, so S-31 stays OPEN.
- [2026-11] **CAPTCHA (S-32) and MFA for staff (S-11) are deferred by the owner** until the clinic has the accounts. Both stay OPEN, and both must be done before real patient data is held.
- [2026-11] **Service-role writes must name an actor or a system job.** Server code sets `app.actor_id` (a profile id) or `app.system_actor` (a job name) with `set_config(..., true)` inside the one transaction that writes. A write without either is refused for the `service_role` role only. See S-07 and S-62.
- [2026-11] **One hosted environment for now,** with the local stack as development. Owner's decision. See the checklist and S-56.
- [2026-11] **The CSP is set per request by the proxy,** with a nonce and `strict-dynamic`, no `unsafe-eval` in production and `style-src 'unsafe-inline'` (React and Radix write style attributes). Every page is rendered per request (`await connection()` in the root layout) so each can carry its nonce. See S-12 and S-57.

- [2026-11] **Invitations: who invites whom.** Admins send every kind; PAs send patient and guardian invitations; doctors, health coaches and patients send none. Links work once and for 3 days (`private.invitation_lifetime()`; the table allows 14). There is no second approver for any kind (PRD open question). Sign-up is invitation only; an existing account accepts by signing in, and its email must equal the invited address. Accounts made from a link are created confirmed, on the strength of the link reaching the address. Acceptance is audited under the inviter. S-74 and S-75 say what this leaves open. [2026-11] Patient and guardian links also need the patient's date of birth, with five tries per link (the fifth cancels it); a patient invitation to an address other than the record's needs the sender to confirm; doctor and admin invitations notify the other admins, and a guardian who can act notifies the other PAs. Team links need no second fact. **Changed [2026-11]:** doctors now send patient invitations for their own care-team patients (below).
- [2026-11] **The service role is used for invitations, and nowhere else for screens.** `sendInvitation`, `resendInvitation`, `cancelInvitation`, the accept page's lookup and the three accept actions call the service-role client after checking who is asking in code; each database function checks the named person again (`may_invite`, `person_has_role`) and sets `app.actor_id`. The list on the Team screen and the patient picker are read through the person's own session.
- [2026-11] **Profile: who can do what.** Everyone changes their own name, phone and photo, and requests an email change (with the current password) and a password change (with the current password, which signs other devices out). Only doctors store a signature, in its own private bucket with one fixed name. Nobody deletes a photo or signature file. Patient contact details on the record are read-only in the app. These are the app's rules; the Data API is the same people with fewer questions (S-37, S-68, S-69, S-70, S-71).
- [2026-11] **Photos are readable by all signed-in people for active staff, and by the team for patients.** The `avatars` policy is intended for names and photos of staff in the staff directory. Patient photos going to every doctor, PA and admin regardless of scope is the current behaviour (S-70); whether that is right is a PRD question.
- [2026-11] **Results: entry by PA or doctor, release by a doctor, writes only through functions.** The tables still give signed-in people no insert and no update of reports or results (apart from `doctor_comment`, doctors only). Entry (`enter_report`), registering a file for entry (`register_import`) and release (`release_report`) are `SECURITY DEFINER` functions in `private` with `SECURITY INVOKER` wrappers in `public`, so the checks run inside the function. A patient or guardian may register their own uploaded file for entry. The database converts units and sets the flag; nobody edits them afterwards. The clinic's unit list and ranges are still to come (S-93). Whether released comments can change and whether a doctor may release their own results are open (S-89, S-92).
- [2026-11] **Result-ready email: the service role reads one address, after release.** `tellPatientByEmail` runs only when `release_report` has succeeded, and the service role is used only to read `profiles.email` and `status` for the patient's own user id (the screen reads use the doctor's session). The email has the first name and a link to `/portal/results`; results are never emailed. It also reads the patient's `notification_preferences` row (topic `results`, channel `email`) and sends nothing if `enabled` is false; no row counts as on. A failure does not undo the release; the doctor is told. See S-96 (resolved), S-97 and S-98.

- [2026-11] **Superseded [2026-11] by the clinic's answers of 11 November (below).** **A nurse has no patient access until the clinic decides.** `private.is_clinical_staff` does not list `nurse`; a nurse can be invited as a team member and signs in to `/clinic`, but every patient table returns nothing. Owner's decision, recorded as given; the clinic's answer needs new policy work (S-105).
- [2026-11] **Who opened a record is written to the audit log, and an admin reads it.** `log_record_view` writes `viewed` rows with the actor, role, entity, record id and patient id, and never a value; `activity_log` shows an admin who, what and which patient (name and clinic reference). Owner's decision. The report page fails closed if the row cannot be written (S-102), the log names only record kinds on a fixed list (S-100) and only real records of that patient (S-101); coverage is still the report page only (S-103).
- [2026-11] **Staff Google Calendar: one encrypted token per person, read only by the server.** As built (uncommitted): the refresh token is encrypted in the app with `GOOGLE_TOKEN_ENCRYPTION_KEY` before it is stored, signed-in people see only whether they are connected and to which address, and the service role reads the token and writes only through two service-role-only functions that log the change under that person. Disconnecting deletes the row rather than archiving it, because it is a credential, not a clinical record; this is the one deliberate exception to "no hard deletes", and it is in the audit log. Event changes are not logged (PRD). Which Google accounts may be connected is not decided (S-106).

- [2026-11] **Who sees which patient (the clinic's answers of 11 November 2026, knowledge base questions 1 to 4 and 7).** PAs and admins see every patient's whole record, admins included (questions 2 and 27: admins read every clinical record, locked notes included). Doctors, nurses and health coaches see only the patients on whose care team they currently are, from `since` to the day before `until`, in London time; inside that, the whole record except safeguarding alerts (doctors only), with diagnoses, prescribing and release kept for doctors. PAs, admins and doctors maintain the care team. The single gate is `private.in_scope` (`20261112120100_access_by_care_team.sql`). A doctor who is also a patient should see only what a patient sees (question 4): **not enforced yet, S-112.** Doctors still read every care-team row (S-58), and unmatched files are open to all doctors and nurses (S-117).
- [2026-11] **Doctors invite their own patients (question 6).** A doctor who is not also a PA or admin may send, resend or cancel a patient invitation only for a patient on their current care team, or for a new patient, who joins that doctor's care team as primary doctor. Doctors and admins can still be invited with no second approval; each such invitation emails `drgina@theschoemanclinic.com` as well as the in-portal notice (production only, in the uncommitted working tree).
- [2026-11] **Clinical notes are locked once signed (question 15).** Doctors, nurses and health coaches write drafts for their care-team patients; only the author signs; a signed note never changes for any role and nothing is deleted; a correction is a new signed version. Everyone in scope reads notes, PAs and admins included; patients and guardians do not. Who may correct whose note is open (S-115).
- [2026-11] **Signed notes are final; unmatched files are for PAs and admins (clinic decisions of 12 November 2026).** Nobody, the author included, corrects a signed note (`20261113120000`). Files and imports not yet matched to a patient are read and handled by PAs and admins only.
- [2026-11] **Photos and guardians (questions 23 and 5).** Staff see staff photos and the photos of patients they can open; patients see only their own. A PA or admin removes a guardian by switching off `can_view` and `can_act`; a patient cannot, and nothing is deleted.
- [2026-11] **Booking in Clinic Hub (question 15).** Admins set types; admins, PAs and the clinician set hours. A patient or a guardian who can act books with a current care-team clinician; staff in scope book any clinician; only staff cancel (48-hour policy). As built, busy times are open to every signed-in person (S-119).
- [2026-11] **Call recording replaces Fathom (question 16).** Doctors, nurses and coaches on the care team record; the browser uploads straight to the private `recordings` bucket; AssemblyAI (EU) transcribes from a short-lived link; the transcript becomes a draft note in the recorder's name. The knowledge base says the clinic already holds patients' consent to record. Provider retention: S-122.
- [2026-11] **Patient messages go through GoHighLevel when it is configured (questions 17 and 25),** by email and, if the patient chose it, WhatsApp (sent as a GoHighLevel text the clinic's plug-in diverts); otherwise email goes through Resend. Address handling: S-121.
- [2026-11] **Education and wearables (questions 21 and 30).** Admins build and publish courses; any staff member in scope (and admins and PAs for every patient) assigns them; a patient or guardian sees a course only once published and assigned. Patients, and guardians who can act, type in wearable readings; staff in scope read them; nothing is deleted. The limits on readings are checked by the app and, since `2ca92de`, by a database trigger (S-116, resolved).
- [2026-11] **Ask the clinic, letters and documents, consents and the fuller record (knowledge base questions 14, 20 and 22, as built on `feat/finalise-points`).** A patient, or a guardian who can act, asks and replies; staff who can open the patient reply and close; a view-only guardian reads but cannot write. Replies and closing go only through definer functions that decide the side from `in_scope`. The clinic's inbox gets a content-free alert; the patient gets a content-free "the clinic has replied" by bell, email and WhatsApp. Doctors, nurses and PAs add documents, which stay hidden until shared, and a patient opens one through a 60-second link served by her own session. Consents are insert-only rows with the wording's version. Allergies are for doctors, nurses and PAs, diagnoses and safeguarding alerts for doctors, as the knowledge base access table says. Messages never carry a subject, document title or appointment type.
- [2026-11] **Allergies are archived, never deleted, and archiving is final (as built on `feat/small-items`).** Doctors, nurses and PAs in scope archive an allergy entered by mistake by setting `archived_at`; the row stays for staff in scope, is hidden from the patient and guardians, and can no longer be edited or restored. To change an allergy, archive it and add it again.
- [2026-11] **The portal shows one chosen patient at a time (as built on `feat/small-items`).** The choice is an `httpOnly` cookie, `portal_patient`, that is only a preference: it is honoured only if the id is among the rows `get_my_patient_record` returns for the session, and otherwise the portal falls back to the person's own record. Authorisation stays with RLS.
- [2026-11] **A leaver's Google grant is revoked by a scheduled server job (as built on `feat/small-items`).** Switching off a staff profile or suspending an account deletes the connection at once and copies the still-encrypted token into `google_revocations`, which only `service_role` reads. A daily Vercel Cron call to `/api/cron/revoke-google`, authenticated by `CRON_SECRET`, revokes each token at Google and clears it. The database never holds the key and never calls Google.
- [2026-11] **Repeat prescriptions: requests and payment through functions only (as built on `feat/repeat-prescriptions`, knowledge base flow 8 and question 13).** A patient, a verified guardian who can act, or a PA in scope asks for up to ten medicines. Each line is the patient's own current medicine, one from the clinic's list, or a typed name. The patient side or staff in scope withdraw an unpaid request. A doctor, PA or admin in scope approves, declines (with a reason) or marks a request sent to the pharmacy. Nobody writes `repeat_requests` directly. Admins and doctors keep the medicine list. The £30 fee is taken on Stripe's hosted page, over Stripe's REST API with no SDK and no Stripe script in the browser. Stripe is told the amount and the request id only. Only the signed webhook, through the service-role function `mark_repeat_request_paid`, marks a request paid. Every message about a request names no medicine. Prescribing stays in SignatureRx, which is not connected.
- [2026-11] **Results sign-off for CQC (clinic decision of 12 November 2026, as built on `feat/cqc-audit`).** Releasing a lab report needs a doctor's sign-off, action (with the action described) or no action, recorded with who signed and who ordered. Another doctor in scope may sign off; it is recorded, not blocked. External reports with no order are signed off the same way. The sign-off and its action note are for staff who can open the record (`in_scope`), never patients or guardians, and are permanent. Clinical notes carry four treatment-option confirmations and an optional list of options offered, fixed with the rest of the note once signed.
- [2026-11] **SignatureRx: the doctor's signing PIN is typed each time and never kept (as built on `signaturerx`, uncommitted; knowledge base flow 8).** A doctor sends an approved repeat request to SignatureRx, as a draft or signed and issued. The PIN goes from the form to SignatureRx in one request and is held nowhere: no column, log, audit row, error or return value. Each doctor's integration code is kept on the server in a table only server code reads, and used for that doctor's sends and for status checks of their prescriptions. Clinic Hub keeps the SignatureRx id, the action, the last known state, who sent it and when; prescribing and signing stay in SignatureRx. Sending is switched off unless `SIGNATURERX_SIGNING_ENABLED` is exactly `true` and the credentials are set, and the clinic's own security review (this document) is meant to come first. SignatureRx is told not to notify the patient. **This is a design statement, not an acceptance of any risk:** S-150 and S-151 were resolved in the twentieth run and S-162 in the twenty-first (see those findings); S-152 is OPEN, with S-165 beside it. A send is opened before SignatureRx is called and is left open if the answer is unclear, so nobody sends twice before checking.
- [2026-11] **SignatureRx is used only for staging and live addresses it names.** `SIGNATURERX_BASE_URL` is one of `https://stage-srx.signaturerx.co.uk` and `https://app.signaturerx.co.uk`, so a mistyped or tampered value cannot send prescriber or patient details to another host. Staging is for tests with fictional patients only.

## Manual configuration checklist

A human does these in the hosted project. None can be verified from this repository. Items left open with a reason are the owner's decisions [2026-11], not oversights.

**Before any real data**

- [x] Auth: "Allow new users to sign up" is off. The owner read `disable_signup: true` from the hosted `/auth/v1/settings` [2026-11] (S-13, resolved on that observation; this review did not see it). Recheck after any Auth settings change.
- [ ] Auth: custom SMTP through Resend, with a verified sending domain. **Deferred until there is a domain.** Until then hosted password reset is not reliable (S-25) and the built-in mail cap will be reached sooner. The reply no longer reveals an account when it is (S-47, resolved), but a person whose reset is limited gets no email.
- [ ] Email (S-65): the sending domain's spelling is confirmed by the owner; the domain is verified in Resend with SPF, DKIM and a DMARC record (start at `p=none` with a report address, then tighten); the Resend API key is restricted to sending on that domain; it is a server-only variable in Vercel and the SMTP password in Supabase; `SITE_URL` is set per environment with no trailing slash; the Supabase Site URL and redirect allow-list list only the production domain (and `/auth/confirm`).
- [ ] Email templates (S-25, S-65): paste the four templates and subjects from `supabase/templates/` into the dashboard, after `pnpm email:build`; the password-changed notification is on; OTP expiry is 3600 seconds, as the copy says; no unreplaced `{{ ... }}` placeholder appears in a received email.
- [ ] Auth: set the recovery email template to the content of `supabase/templates/recovery.html`, with its subject. **Deferred with SMTP, for the same reason** (S-25).
- [ ] Auth: site URL and redirect allow-list hold production URLs only, with no localhost and no broad wildcards (S-44).
- [ ] Auth: session time-box and inactivity limit are set. **Needs the Pro plan** (Supabase docs: "only available on Pro Plans and up"). Until then only the app enforces limits (S-31). They apply to the whole project, so set them no shorter than the patient limits.
- [ ] Auth: leaked password protection is on. **Needs the Pro plan.** Minimum 12 characters (maximum 128 in the app rules), as in `config.toml`.
- [ ] Auth: rate limits reviewed, knowing that sign-in and reset through the app all arrive from the app server and direct calls do not (S-32). Check whether the project supports the `Sb-Forwarded-For` header.
- [ ] Auth: anonymous sign-ins and manual linking are off, as in `config.toml`.
- [ ] Auth: secure password change and double-confirm email change are on, as in `config.toml`. **Know what they do and do not do** (S-37, S-68): locally, `secure_password_change` did not stop a password change through `PUT /auth/v1/user` from a session under a day old, and one link finished an email change. After setting them, request an email change on a test account on the hosted project and check whether the old address's link is also needed; do not claim protection that was not seen.
- [ ] Auth: the email-changed notification (to the old address) is switched on in the hosted dashboard, with the template from `supabase/templates/email_changed_notification.html` (build with `pnpm email:build`), next to the password-changed one (S-68). It is in `config.toml` as `[auth.email.notification.email_changed]` and works locally; the hosted project is set by hand. Check on a test account that the old address gets it.
- [ ] Migrations `20261104120000_profile_email_sync.sql`, `20261104120100_signatures_bucket.sql` and `20261104120200_profile_limits_and_avatar_path.sql` are applied by a human after review. The third adds the name and phone checks (S-71) and tightens the avatar upload policies to `<id>/avatar` (S-70); if a hosted profile already holds a longer name or a phone with other characters, it fails, so check first. The second creates the private `signatures` bucket and its three policies on `storage.objects`; if the hosted storage settings are set by hand, check the bucket is private, 1 MiB, png, jpeg and webp only, and that **no delete policy exists**. Until the first is applied, `profiles.email` does not follow a confirmed change (S-23 reopens on hosted).
- [ ] Migration `20261105120000_invitations.sql` (invitations) is applied by a human after review. It adds a column to a live table, an enum, new functions and a sequence; run it before deploying the code, and rerun the advisors. The two demo invitations in the seed must not reach the hosted project (S-85).
- [ ] Migration `20261105130000_invitation_identity.sql` (the 21st) is applied by a human after review, after `20261105120000`. It adds `invitations.failed_attempts`, drops and recreates `create_invitation` and `accept_invitation` with an extra argument, and adds `verify_invitation_identity`. Check on the hosted database that all six functions are executable by `service_role` only, and that the clinic knows a patient's date of birth is now asked at acceptance: a patient record with a wrong or empty date cannot be accepted until it is corrected (S-86). Also decide who reads the in-portal notices about doctor, admin and can-act guardian invitations (S-75).
- [ ] Invitations need, in the host as server-only variables: `SITE_URL` (the production address, `https`, no trailing path: the link is built from it, S-44), `RESEND_API_KEY` and `EMAIL_FROM` (verified domain, S-65), and `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` (the sending, accepting and cancelling all use it). Without the email variables the screen saves the invitation and says email is not set up, which is safe but sends nothing.
- [ ] **A real end-to-end test of an invitation email,** after the above: invite a test address as a PA (patient), as a PA (guardian) and as an admin (team); check the sender, the SPF, DKIM and DMARC results, that the link in the message is `SITE_URL/invite?token=…` and not rewritten (turn Resend click tracking off for the sending domain, S-82), that it opens the accept page with no token in the address bar, that a second open after use says it was used, and that the 3-day expiry is stated. Then send the same link through a link scanner or open it twice and confirm it still works once.
- [ ] Confirm the `ch_invite` cookie on production carries `Secure`, `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax` and `Path=/invite`, and that `/invite` and `/invite/accept` are reachable signed out (S-31).
- [ ] Vercel request logs: the invitation token is in the path of `GET /invite?token=…` (S-82). Check the retention and who can read the logs, and treat a link as live for its 3 days.
- [ ] Storage: the `avatars` bucket stays private, and nothing but profile photos goes in it (S-70). Signatures go to `signatures` only.
- [ ] Auth: MFA for staff is on, and enforced by code (S-11). **Deferred by the owner until the clinic has the accounts;** required before real patient data.
- [ ] Auth: CAPTCHA on sign-in and password reset (S-32). **Deferred by the owner until the clinic has the accounts.**
- [ ] **Set `SESSION_COOKIE_SECRET` in Vercel** as a server-only variable, a random value of at least 32 characters (`openssl rand -base64 48`), different from the local development value in `.claude/launch.json`, and never with `NEXT_PUBLIC_`. Set it before the first deployment is used: without it every signed-in request returns 500 (S-60, S-31). Changing it later signs everyone out.
- [ ] Migrations `20261102143000_auth_throttle.sql` and `20261102150000_scope_and_audit_actor.sql` are applied (a human pushes them after review), and `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` is set in the host. Without either, the throttle is silently off (S-50).
- [ ] Project in the London region (`eu-west-2`).
- [ ] Data Processing Agreement signed with Supabase, and with Resend, Vercel and any other processor that sees personal data.
- [ ] Separate dev and prod projects, with separate keys. **Decided against for now:** one hosted environment, with the local stack as development. Real data then needs extra care: stop pointing `.env.local` at that project before it holds any (S-56).
- [ ] Data API: exposed schemas are `public` only. `private` must never be added (S-20).
- [ ] Never run the seed against a hosted project (no `--include-seed`): it creates accounts, including an admin, with one shared password written in the file.
- [ ] Realtime: no tables in the publication, public channel access off, or disable it if not used.
- [ ] Rotate keys if ever exposed. `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY`, `SESSION_COOKIE_SECRET` and `RESEND_API_KEY` are set as server-only variables in the host, never with `NEXT_PUBLIC_`.
- [ ] Point-in-time recovery or backups are on, with a tested restore. **To check: whether automatic backups need a paid plan.** If they do, real data needs that plan or another tested backup.
- [ ] Network restrictions on direct database connections, with SSL enforced.

- [ ] Branch `feat/clinic-build-2` [2026-11]: `pnpm db:lint` and `pnpm db:test` pass on a real local Supabase stack (the fifteenth run had a stand-in only), then migrations `20261113120000` to `20261113120600` are applied by a human in order; `20261113120400_booking_source.sql` adds an enum value and must commit before `20261113120500` uses it.
- [ ] Branch `feat/finalise-points` [2026-11]: `pnpm db:lint` and `pnpm db:test` pass on a real local Supabase stack (the sixteenth run had none), including `15_finalise` and `16_questions_and_record`; then a human applies `20261114120000_finalise_hardening.sql` and `20261114120100_question_replies.sql` after review. Before pushing, confirm no environment has already applied `20261113120000`, `20261113120300` or `20261113120500`, which `29f602f` edited in place (S-136 item 11).
- [ ] Offboarding (S-134, S-139) [2026-11]: set `CRON_SECRET` in Vercel (Production) as a server-only variable of at least 32 characters (`openssl rand -base64 48`), never `NEXT_PUBLIC_`; without it the revocation job answers 404 and nothing is revoked. After deployment, confirm the cron appears in the Vercel project and that a run answers 200. Until S-139 is fixed, check `google_revocations` for rows with `revoked_at is null` and `attempts >= 10`, and remove Clinic Hub's access by hand for those people (the person at myaccount.google.com/permissions, or a Google Workspace admin).

**After deployment**

- [ ] Confirm the host overwrites `X-Forwarded-Host`: a request with a foreign `Origin` and a matching `X-Forwarded-Host` must not run a server action (S-44).
- [ ] Confirm Vercel overwrites the client-address headers (S-51). With a forged `x-real-ip` and `x-forwarded-for` that change on each request, send 11 wrong passwords for one invented `@example.test` address from one machine. The 11th must say "Too many attempts". If it does not, the host is passing the headers through and the address limits can be bypassed. Repeat after any change of host or any proxy put in front.
- [ ] Confirm the throttle is running at all, by the same test: an error in the log or no refusal means it is failing open (S-50).
- [ ] Confirm the `sb-` and `ch_*` cookies all carry `Secure` and `HttpOnly` in production (S-31).
- [ ] **Confirm the CSP header and the nonce on production responses** (S-12). With `curl -sI` on `/login`, `/forgot-password`, a missing path and an `/auth/confirm` redirect: the header is present; `script-src` has `'nonce-…'` and `'strict-dynamic'` and no `'unsafe-inline'` or `'unsafe-eval'`; the nonce is different on every request; `curl -s` of the page shows that every `<script` tag has `nonce="…"` with the same value; and `Cache-Control` is `no-store` (a cached page would reuse a nonce). `connect-src` and `img-src` hold the production Supabase origin, since `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` is read at request time. Open the sign-in page in a browser and check the console for CSP violations.
- [ ] Confirm that a request for a missing image path (`/x.png`) is understood: it returns an HTML 404 with no CSP until S-57 is fixed.
- [ ] Confirm sign-in works after the deployment and that the three cookies carry `Secure` and `HttpOnly` (S-60: a missing secret shows up here).
- [ ] After any Auth settings change, recheck that `/auth/v1/settings` still reports `disable_signup: true` (S-13).
- [ ] Build and test locally from a copy of the tree with no `.env.local`, or with `web-local`, never with a plain `pnpm build` or `next start` in the repository (S-56).
- [ ] Confirm the domain should be HSTS preloaded before relying on the `preload` directive: it is hard to undo.
- [ ] Rerun the advisors in the dashboard after each migration push.
- [ ] Vercel Analytics and Speed Insights (S-63): the owner confirms the Vercel data processing terms cover them and the analytics settings (retention, IP handling), and the clinic's privacy notice and cookie-free PECR position cover them. On Vercel, open `/login?location=x` and an emailed `/auth/confirm?token_hash=x` link with the network tab open: every request to `/_vercel/insights` and `/_vercel/speed-insights` must carry a URL with no query string or fragment, no cookie is set, and the console shows no CSP violation. Keep identifiers out of route paths, or mask them in the hook, before patient pages are built.
- [ ] Knowledge base (S-64, S-99): both `/knowledge-base` and `/knowledge-base/test-results` behave the same way: signed out, redirect to `/login?next=` with the path encoded; the test-results file is in the deployment and the page warns that its steps write permanent data; signed out, `/knowledge-base` redirects to `/login?next=%2Fknowledge-base`; a patient signing in lands on `/portal`; a doctor lands on `/clinic` and is sent back there on opening the page; an admin lands on the page; a suspended or inactive admin does not get in; a foreign `next` (`https://evil.test`, `//evil.test`) is ignored in the form. On production, signed in as an admin, it renders (the Markdown file is in the deployment) and carries `noindex`. The file holds no secret or internal detail, and its access table is re-checked against the policies after any policy, grant or role change.
- [ ] Email test send (S-65, S-67): before going live, run `pnpm email:test` for each template to the owner's own address, and trigger a real hosted password reset. Check the sender, the SPF, DKIM and DMARC results in the message headers, that links go to the production domain and `/auth/confirm`, that the logo loads, that no email holds a result, a value or a diagnosis, and the rendering in Outlook, Gmail and Apple Mail. Resend webhook and bounce handling are not built (S-66).

Rerun the advisors afterwards.
